| RAN1#122 | 11.2 | Agreement | - Study which of the following traffic models are to be used for 6G evaluations, e.g.,<br>  - Full buffer<br>  - FTP Model 1 (in TR 36.814)<br>  - FTP Model 2 (in TR 36.814)<br>  - FTP Model 3 (in TR 36.872)<br>  - XR Traffic models (in TR 38.838)<br>  - VoIP model (as in TR 36.814)<br>  - Instant message (as in TR 38.840)<br>- Study whether to introduce the following traffic models for 6G evaluations considering, e.g.,<br>  - FTP-3 variant with packet delay budget requirement<br>    - Details FFS<br>  - New traffic model considering a mixed/variable packet size and the associated time domain behaviors (e.g., time between adjacent packet arrivals, packet delay budget)<br>    - Details FFS<br>  - New traffic model(s) considering the new use cases or services, e.g., AI/ML services, immersive communication services, etc.<br>    - Details FFS<br>- Study whether to introduce new/additional approaches that can reflect the impact of bidirectional traffic flows on performance metrics (e.g., impact of UL TCP ACK latency on DL throughput/latency)<br>Note: Whether/how to consider the combination of traffic model and loading level will be studied under individual agendas. | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |
| RAN1#122 | 11.3.1 | Agreement | CP-OFDM and DFT-s-OFDM waveforms as defined in 5G NR are supported as the basis for 6GR for uplink<br>- Enhancements/modifications on CP-OFDM/DFT-s-OFDM will be studied as potential additions<br>- Other OFDM based waveforms are not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |
| RAN1#122 | 11.3.1 | Agreement | CP-OFDM waveform as defined in 5G NR is supported as the basis for 6GR for downlink<br>- Enhancements/modifications on CP-OFDM will be studied as potential additions<br>- DFT-s-OFDM or any other OFDM-based waveform will be studied as a potential additional waveform for downlink<br>Note: proponents to identify at least the target use cases, signals/channels to use the waveform, and how the proposal is intended (if applicable) to support multiplexing with CP-OFDM, including MRSS, and how multi-user multiplexing is supported, etc.<br>Note:<br>Proponents are encouraged to provide more detailed information on their proposals for the next meeting, e.g.:<br>Proponents to characterize the main motivation for modification/additional waveform proposals:<br>- Targeted link direction, i.e. DL, UL or both<br>- Targeted use case (e.g. NTN, specific frequency range, etc.), if any<br>- Potential motivations metrics used, and quantified gains for a proposal, e.g.<br>  - Coverage<br>  - Network energy efficiency<br>  - UE energy efficiency<br>  - Spectral efficiency<br>  - High speed tolerance<br>  - Scheduling flexibility<br>  - Integration with ISAC<br>Proponents provide information on the following aspects, if applicable<br>- MRSS compatibility<br>- Target channels/signals, e.g. all channels, PxSCH only, etc.<br>- MIMO (SU and MU-MIMO) compatibility<br>- Target modulations, and impact to other modulations, if applicable<br>- Multi-user multiplexing/scheduling flexibility<br>- Multiplexing/coexistence with baseline waveforms<br>- Impact on synchronization and initial access<br>- Expected specification impact<br>- Transmitter/receiver complexity and impact to power consumption. | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |
| RAN1#122 | 11.3.2 | Agreement | - 6GR takes the following SCS as start point for discussion for all the signals/channels except PRACH.<br>  - For sub 6GHz<br>    - The following subcarrier spacing is at least supported<br>      - 15kHz SCS for FDD, 30kHz SCS for TDD<br>    - FFS: 30kHz SCS for FDD for around e.g., 1-2.5GHz<br>    - FFS: 7.5kHz SCS for sub1GHz (FDD)<br>    - Whether to discuss the FFS will be subject to RANP decision.<br>  - For around 7GHz<br>    - The following subcarrier spacing options can be studied<br>      - 30kHz, 60kHz<br>  - FFS: For around 15GHz<br>    - The following subcarrier spacing options can be studied<br>      - 30kHz, 60kHz, 120kHz<br>    - Whether to discuss it will be subject to RANP decision<br>  - For between 24.25GHz - 52.6GHz<br>    - Subcarrier spacing 120kHz is supported<br>  - FFS whether to allow using additional subcarrier spacing for SSB<br>- FFS subcarrier spacing for PRACH and up to initial access discussion. | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Frame structure]` |

| RAN1#122bis | 11.1 | Agreement | - High-level aspects to consider for the 6GR sync signal structure include, but not limited to<br>  - Sync raster design<br>  - Spectrum allocation<br>  - smallest maximum supported RF and BB UE BW without spectrum aggregation<br>  - mobile broadband service requirements as high priority<br>  - Energy efficiency for both BS and UE<br>  - Detection/tracking performance, latency, and complexity<br>    - Including initial cell search<br>  - Coverage target<br>  - Common design for diverse device types<br>  - Consideration of the supported deployment<br>  - Consideration on whether the single sync signal structure is sufficient<br>  - Note: Aspects impacting on the periodicity is to be discussed under AI11.5 | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.1 | Agreement | - The aspects to consider for supporting NTN include, but not limited to<br>  - Initial access, including cell search and SSB periodicity<br>  - Coverage<br>  - Duplexing<br>  - Capacity<br>  - Signalling overhead<br>  - GNSS-less/resilient/based operation<br>  - Large/varying doppler and propagation delay<br>  - Beamforming / beam management / beam hopping<br>Note:<br>- High-level aspects to consider to enable lower CAPEX/OPEX with respect to current networks include, but not limited to<br>  - UE/NW implementation complexity<br>  - UE/NW energy efficiency<br>  - MRSS<br>  - Spectrum efficiency | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Overall]` |

| RAN1#122bis | 11.2 | Conclusion | The following existing traffic models could be used for 6GR performance evaluations,<br>- Full buffer<br>- FTP Model 1 (in TR 36.814)<br>- FTP Model 3 (in TR 36.872)<br>- XR Traffic models (in TR 38.838)<br>- VoIP model (as in TR 36.814)<br>- Instant message (as in TR 38.840)<br>- Note that which model(s) will be used can be further decided when performing simulations in each individual topic. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.2 | Agreement | For the study traffic model(s) for 6GR AI/ML services:<br>  - A representative AI/ML service is the generative AI, e.g., as defined in TR22.870.<br>Send LS to SA4 (cc RAN2, SA1, SA2) requesting input if any on traffic characteristics for AI/ML services.<br>Note: RAN1 is discussing the following options for the model:<br>  - Option-1a: The model is parameterized by Token, e.g., Token size, Token arrival rate, and Token delay budget.<br>    - Token is the minimum unit of data generated in the application layer.<br>    - How to associate Tokens to PHY layer packets.<br>    - How to reflect the variable importance of tokens.<br>    - Whether other parameters are additionally needed when tokens are encapsulated together into a packet, e.g., packet arrival rate, packet success rate, and packet delay.<br>  - Option-1b: The model is characterized by the parameters of PHY layer packet, including e.g., packet size, arrival rates, latency requirement, reliability requirement, etc.<br>  - Option-1c: reusing or extending the FTP-3/XR traffic model.<br>  - FFS other models/options need to be defined for other AI/ML services. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.2 | Agreement | Study traffic modelling for evaluations related to immersive communication services including but not limited to advanced XR [e.g., TR22.870] and haptics services,<br>- XR traffic models (in TR 38.838) are considered as starting point.<br>  - FFS the detailed modifications on the parameters to the XR traffic model, e.g., higher packet size, higher packet arrival rate, higher packet size deviation, PDB, etc.<br>- FFS how many models need to be defined and the corresponding representative use cases.<br>- FFS how to incorporate haptics traffic (TR26.854).<br>Send LS to SA4 requesting input if any on the relevant traffic characteristics, RAN1 can continue the study before SA4 potential response. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.2 | Agreement | Study extensions to FTP Model 1/FTP Model 3 to incorporate the following:<br>- Multiple packet sizes and associated time-domain behaviors (e.g., inter arrival time)<br>  - FFS number of packet sizes (e.g., 2 or 3).<br>  - FFS whether to have fixed or variable packet size and packet arrival rate for a given UE.<br>  - FFS applicability of multiple packet sizes to only one or both of FTP Model 1/FTP Model 3.<br>  - FFS packet size and arrival rate characteristics.<br>- Packet delay budget (PDB) related parameters<br>  - FFS PDB applicability to packets (e.g., one PDB parameter for only one traffic flow or different PDB parameters for different traffic flows).<br>  - FFS how to consider the PDB, e.g., whether to drop packets when exceeding the budget, PDB aware metric.<br>- Note consider the following for PDB:<br>  - Applicability to the extension to FTP Model 1/ FTP Model 3 with one packet size.<br>  - Applicability or not to the extension to FTP Model 1/ FTP Model 3 with multiple packet sizes. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.2 | Agreement | The attached templates for NTN in R1-2507956 are endorsed in principle. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |

| RAN1#122bis | 11.2 | Agreement | For link budget template, consider the following candidates:<br>- Candidate 1: Reusing the link budget template from TR38.830, i.e., the following table with notes as follows:<br>- The values of the parameters are TBD.<br>- MCL in row (22bis) is TBD.<br>- FFS: whether/how/why to update<br>\| System configuration \| System configuration \|<br>\| Channel for evaluation \|  \|<br>\| Scenarios and Carrier frequency (GHz) \|  \|<br>\| BS antenna heights (m) \|  \|<br>\| UT antenna heights (m) \|  \|<br>\| Cell area reliability (%) \|  \|<br>\| Lognormal shadow fading std deviation (dB) \|  \|<br>\| Tx Diversity \|  \|<br>\| Number of SSB \|  \|<br>\| Transmitter \| Transmitter \|<br>\| (1) Number of transmit antenna elements \|  \|<br>\| (2) Number of transmit TxRUs Note: this row is void (left empty) for uplink \|  \|<br>\| (2a) Number of transmit chains modelled in LLS \|  \|<br>\| (3) Total transmit power (dBm) Note: total transmit power for system bandwidth \|  \|<br>\| (3a) System bandwidth for downlink, or occupied bandwidth for uplink (Hz) \|  \|<br>\| (3b) Power Spectrum Density = (3) - 10 log( (3a) / 1000000 ) (dBm/MHz) Note: no PSD constraint for uplink \|  \|<br>\| (3c) Bandwidth used for the evaluated channel (Hz) Note: (3c) is identical to the number of PRBs assigned to the channel evaluated. For uplink, (3a) = (3c) \|  \|<br>\| (3bis) Total transmit power for occupied bandwidth = (3b) + 10 log ((3c) /1000000) (dBm) \|  \|<br>\| (4) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 3 & antenna gain component 4 of transmitter = (4a) – (4b) (dB) \|  \|<br>\| (4a) Antenna gain at antenna gain component 3 & antenna gain component 4 of transmitter = (4c) + 10 log ((1) / (2)) (dB) for downlink, and = (4c) + 10 log ((1) / (2a)) (dB) for uplink \|  \|<br>\| (4b) Antenna gain correction factor at antenna gain component 3 & antenna gain component 4 of transmitter (dB) \|  \|<br>\| (4c) Gain of antenna element (dBi) \|  \|<br>\| (5) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 2 of transmitter = (5a) - (5b) (dB) Note: zero for uplink \|  \|<br>\| (5a) Antenna gain at antenna gain component 2 of transmitter = 10 log((2)/(2a)) (dB) Note: zero for uplink \|  \|<br>\| (5b) Antenna gain correction factor at antenna gain component 2 of transmitter (dB) Note: zero for uplink \|  \|<br>\| (8) Cable, connector, combiner, body losses, etc. (enumerate sources) (dB) (feeder loss must be included for and only for downlink) \|  \|<br>\| (9) EIRP = (3bis) + (4) + (5) – (8) dBm \|  \|<br>\| Receiver \| Receiver \|<br>\| (10) Number of receive antenna elements \|  \|<br>\| (10a) Number of receive TxRUs Note: this row is void (empty) for downlink \|  \|<br>\| (10b) Number of receive chains modelled in LLS \|  \|<br>\| (11) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 3 & antenna gain component 4 of receiver = (11a) - (11b) (dB) \|  \|<br>\| (11a) Antenna gain at antenna gain component 3 & antenna gain component 4 of receiver = (11c) + 10 log ((10)/(10a)) (dB) for uplink = (11c) + 10 log ((10)/(10b)) (dB) for downlink \|  \|<br>\| (11b) Antenna gain correction factor at antenna gain component 3 & antenna gain component 4 of receiver (dB) \|  \|<br>\| (11c) Gain of antenna element (dBi) \|  \|<br>\| (11bis) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 2 of receiver = (11bis-a) - (11bis-b) (dB) Note: zero for downlink \|  \|<br>\| (11bis-a) Antenna gain at antenna gain component 2 of receiver = 10 log((10a)/(10b)) (dB) Note: zero for downlink \|  \|<br>\| (11bis-b) Antenna gain correction factor at antenna gain component 2 of receiver (dB) Note: zero for downlink \|  \|<br>\| (12) Cable, connector, combiner, body losses, etc. (enumerate sources) (dB) (feeder loss must be included for and only for uplink) \|  \|<br>\| (13) Receiver noise figure (dB) \|  \|<br>\| (14) Thermal noise density (dBm/Hz) \|  \|<br>\| (15) Receiver interference density (dBm/Hz) \|  \|<br>\| (16) Total noise plus interference density = 10 log (10^(( (13) + (14))/10) + 10^((15)/10)) (dBm/Hz) \|  \|<br>\| (18) Effective noise power = (16) + 10 log ((3c)) (dBm) \|  \|<br>\| (19) Required SNR (dB) \|  \|<br>\| (20) Receiver implementation margin (dB) \|  \|<br>\| (21) H-ARQ gain (dB) Note: Only applicable if HARQ is not considered in LLS \|  \|<br>\| (22) Receiver sensitivity = (18) + (19) + (20) – (21) (dBm) \|  \|<br>\| (22bis) MCL = (3bis) – (22) + (5) + (11bis) (dB) \|  \|<br>\| (23) Hardware link budget, a.k.a. | RAN1#122bis | 11.3.1 | Agreement | - For uplink low-PAPR proposals, the link level performance evaluation criterion is Net Gain assuming same spectrum efficiency as the reference<br>  - Net Gain [dB] = Tx power gain relative to the reference – SNR degradation relative to the reference @10% BLER<br>    - A realistic PA model should be used<br>    - When calculating the Tx power gain, the RAN4 metrics on the Tx power should be taken into account.<br>    - For SNR degradation, fading channel and non-ideal channel estimation, including DMRS configuration, and equalization is encouraged.<br>    - FFS: Other evaluation metrics<br>    - Note: Companies to report how to calculate the Tx power gain, modulation and coding | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.3.1 | Agreement | - Study the evaluation method for evaluating DFT-s-OFDM for UL with number of layers > 1. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.3.2 | Agreement | For communication, 6GR considers NR frame structure used as a starting point for the study item,<br>  - Resource defined by one subcarrier and one symbol is called as resource element (RE).<br>  - Resource block (RB) is defined where the number of consecutive subcarriers per RB is the same for all numerologies and the number of subcarriers per RB is 12<br>  - Radio Frame length is 10ms<br>  - Each radio frame is split into 10 subframes, each with a duration of 1 ms<br>  - For given SCS and for given symbol, the symbol duration, normal CP length and boundary is same as NR design.<br>  - A slot is defined as supporting 14 consecutive symbols for normal CP case and all subcarrier spacings. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Frame structure]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.3.2 | Agreement | 6GR study assumes same SCS between 6GR Sync signals and other channels/signals (except PRACH) for a given band.<br>- FFS: same/different SCS between 6GR sync signal and other channels/signals (except PRACH) for FR2-1.<br>- Note: ISAC is separately discussed in ISAC session. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Frame structure]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.3.2 | Agreement | - RAN1 assumes 400MHz maximum channel bandwidth at network side and 30kHz SCS around 7GHz<br>- Study whether and how to enable UE to support 400MHz bandwidth | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Frame structure]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.4.1 | Working Assumption | - Study 6G data channel coding for higher throughput than 5G with acceptable performance-complexity tradeoff for both NW side and UE side,<br>  - Target peak data rate is assumed to be 2 times of the target peak data rate defined in TR38.913<br>Note: The other target throughput is up to company to report.<br>Note: Applicability of the potential channel code will be further discussed. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.4.1 | Agreement | - For 6G channel coding, LDPC is used for data (including SIBs) and Polar code is used for L1 control information (larger than 11 bits, including PBCH)<br>- For 6G LDPC<br>  - Working assumption: For data rate within NR range, reuse of NR LDPC design is supported<br>  - For data rate beyond NR range, study LDPC extension with acceptable performance-complexity tradeoff for both NW side and UE side<br>    - Note: Applicability of the potential LDPC extension to data rate within NR range will be further discussed<br>- For 6G Polar code<br>  - Working assumption: For control information within NR range (larger than 11 bits), reuse of NR Polar code design is supported<br>  - For control information beyond NR range, study Polar code extension with acceptable performance-complexity tradeoff for both NW side and UE side<br>    - Note: Necessity for control information beyond NR range is to be further discussed<br>    - Polar code maximum mother code length is kept as 1024.<br>  - FFS: further motivation(s) for potential extension/enhancement until RAN1#123 | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.4.2 | Agreement | For 6GR constellation shaping evaluation for CP-OFDM, and improved MCS table, the proposed scheme will be compared with non-shaping with NR MCS table. The evaluation and comparison should consider at least the following:<br>- BLER performance under AWGN channel (at least for performance calibration)<br>  - 1st transmission (baseline) and with HARQ re-transmission<br>- BLER performance under fading channel with fixed MCS<br>  - 1st transmission (baseline) and with HARQ re-transmission<br>- Throughput performance with link adaptation (adaptive MCS and rank) under fading channel<br>  - Needs to provide assumptions on rate adaptation (e.g., target BLER for 1st transmission, maximum # of retransmissions)<br>- Transmitter and receiver complexity (e.g., shaping/deshaping, demapper), latency, parallelism implementation, and storage requirements,<br>- Other KPI not excluded, such as PAPR, EVM, MPR/A-MPR<br>- Expected spec impact<br>- FFS detailed assumption of constellation shaping and improved MCS table<br>- System level evaluation can be done after link level evaluation. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.4.2 | Agreement | For 6GR constellation shaping study, proponent is encouraged to provide details for the PS/GS schemes considered for evaluation and comparison, including at least the following<br>- Probabilistic shaping for CP-OFDM and DFT-s-OFDM<br>  - Use the list of spectrum efficiencies in NR MCS table as starting point, and provide constellation (including normalization), coding rate and target probabilistic distribution for each SE<br>    - If multiple coding rate and target probabilistic distribution pairs are provided for each SE, how to switch between them<br>  - Relationship between shaping and FEC, coded bits to modulation symbol mapping, and other modules (such as scrambling, interleaving), in transmit and receive chains. How to handle HARQ retransmission<br>  - PS algorithm details (for example, source coding based, channel coding based, etc) and parameters (such as block length, rate loss)<br>- Geometric shaping for CP-OFDM and DFT-s-OFDM<br>  - Use the list of spectrum efficiencies in NR MCS table as starting point, and provide target constellation shape (including normalization) (1D-NUC, 2D-NUC, QAM-CS, etc) for each SE<br>    - If multiple constellation shapes are provided for each SE, how to switch between them<br>  - GS mapping details, such as bit to constellation point mapping and shape<br>  - Relationship with other blocks (such as scrambling, interleaving). How to handle HARQ retransmission | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.4.2 | Agreement | For link level simulation for modulation evaluation, companies are encouraged to evaluate with the following assumptions and should report the exact scheme evaluated.<br>- channel configuration, including Channel profiles,Tx/RX antenna settings<br>- For MIMO scenario: SU-MIMO or MU-MIMO, follow agenda item 11.2 for MIMO when available.<br>- Precoder assumption<br>  - Close loop MIMO (reciprocal beamforming (e.g., SVD, SLR/RZF, etc.), codebook based)<br>    - Realistic CSI/SRS/AP-SRS periodicity and delay, and SRS chanEst assumptions,<br>    - or genie beamforming<br>  - Open loop MIMO<br>- Receiver assumption (for MIMO): LMMSE (baseline) for UL, rML or LMMSE for DL<br>- LLR demapper: Max-log (baseline) or Log-MAP<br>- Channel estimation: Realistic (baseline) or ideal<br>- Other assumptions: Channel coding NR-LDPC (baseline), PxSCH bandwidth, SCS, FD interleaver used or not, 5GNR BICM interleaver usage<br>- Note: For MIMO, SIMO, MISO and SISO are included when possible | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.4.2 | Agreement | For 6GR constellation shaping evaluation for DFT-s-OFDM, and improved MCS table, the proposed scheme will be compared with non-shaping with NR MCS table. In addition to what has been agreed for CP-OFDM in earlier agreement, the evaluation and comparison should further consider at least the following:<br>- PAPR/CM of the resulting waveform<br>- EVM, MPR/A-MPR | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.4.2 | Agreement | For the study of uniform 4096QAM for DL and uniform 1024QAM for UL, need to study performance (assuming realistic channel estimation, time/freq synchronization assumption, phase noise assumption, etc), complexity/power consumption, requirements, benefit/necessity under applicable scenarios, associated restrictions, and challenges (such as EVM requirement, PAPR increase, MPR or A-MPR increase under realistic PA model).<br>- FFS: How to involve RAN4 early<br>- FFS: Shaping of higher order modulation<br>- System level evaluation can be done after link level evaluation. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.5 | Agreement | At least the following NR metrics,<br>- Network energy saving gain relative to baseline for BS<br>- UE energy saving gain relative to baseline for UE<br>- Impact to UPT (User-Perceived Throughput), if applicable,<br>as well as the metrics<br>- Impact to latency, if applicable<br>- Impact to QoS/delay budget satisfaction rate, if applicable<br>are used for 6G energy efficiency evaluation. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.5 | Agreement | Apply the following evaluation methodology framework for Quantitative analysis,<br>- For NW unloaded/empty load case or UE idle/inactive mode:<br>- For energy saving: analytical calculation<br>- For performance impact: analytical calculation, LLS<br>- For loaded cases and connected-mode UEs<br>- For energy saving: SLS<br>- For performance impact: LLS, SLS | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.5 | Agreement | For evaluation purposes, expand the existing BS power model reference configuration with a set for ~7 GHz operation with the following parameters:<br>\| Property \| Configuration for Set 4 around 7 GHz \|<br>\| Duplex \| TDD \|<br>\| BW \| [100, 200, 400] MHz \|<br>\| SCS \| [30 kHz, 60 kHz] \|<br>\| Number of TRP \| 1 \|<br>\| Total number of DL TX RUs \| [128, 256] \|<br>\| Total DL power level \| [56] dBm \|<br>\| Total number of UL Rx RUs \| [128, 256] \|<br>Note: Bracketed values to be confirmed. Other values are not precluded.<br>The above configuration has no implication on supported BW, SCS for 6GR. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.5 | Agreement | Study whether/how to further update the BS model considering the following aspects, e.g.,<br>- Whether to downselect between Cat.1 and Cat. 2,<br>- Updates of parameter values (including defining a new Cat),<br>- Updates of power scaling, power states (including additional PSs)<br>- Etc.<br>Note: The defined BS power models does not preclude use case-specific enhancements regarding, e.g., multi-TRP, SBFD, multi-carrier etc | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.5 | Agreement | Study and evaluate NW energy savings and the impact on UE performance and user experience with respect to 20ms and longer periodicities of sync signal(s) at least for initial access with the following consideration, but not limited to:<br>BS assumptions:<br>- Cell-common signaling (e.g., sync signal(s), broadcast PDCCH, SIB-1, SIB, paging, PRACH), e.g.,<br>  - Clustered provisioning of different cell-common signaling,<br>  - On-demand provisioning of different cell-common signaling,<br>- UE-specific signaling (for low, light, medium loads), e.g.,<br>  - Clustered provisioning with cell-common signaling,<br>  - Unclustered provisioning with cell-common signaling,<br>UE impact:<br>- Cell search complexity and latency, including frequency search latency,<br>- UE Power consumption,<br>- Sync signal detection, coverage and tracking performance,<br>- RRM, mobility,<br>- Beam management,<br>- Other properties are not precluded,<br>- Improvements to address identified impact, e.g.,<br>  - Additional sync signal needs,<br>  - Adaptation of sync signal transmission periodicity,<br>  - Sparser synch raster. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.5 | Agreement | Study and evaluate on-demand and/or periodic SIB-1 transmission with respect to<br>- NW energy savings potential and UE power consumption impact,<br>- SIB-1 acquisition delay,<br>- NW and UE complexity,<br>- Coverage,<br>- Applicable deployment scenarios, e.g.,:<br>  - Standalone cell/carrier,<br>  - Multiple TRPs/cells/carriers. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.5 | Agreement | For 6GR energy efficiency evaluation purposes, reuse the existing UE power consumption model FR1 and FR2 reference configurations in TR 38.840 for operation up to around 7GHz and within 24.25 GHz – 52.6 GHz, respectively.<br>- Scaling rules can be updated, including additional rule(s) for scaling UE power consumption, and including around 7GHz specific update<br>  - FFS: details.<br>- Power value and transition time update, if necessary, including around 7GHz specific update<br>- No implication on supported BW, SCS, modulation and antenna setting for 6GR<br>- Revisit if SCS for around 7GHz is different with respect to the reference configuration | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.5 | Agreement | Study and evaluate DL WUS of OFDM based sequence and corresponding mechanisms for 6GR EE improvement, regarding at least the following aspects:<br>- Coverage target for DL WUS (e.g., same as PDCCH, common sync signal, or other)<br>- Measurements and/or synchronization.<br>- System overhead and network energy consumption/UE energy saving for UE operation with the DL WUS.<br>- RRC states<br>- Other functionalities | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.5 | Agreement | For evaluation purposes, study extending NR UE power consumption scaling w.r.t. at least BW and/or antenna setting, considering at least the different characteristics in RF/BB power consumption and static/dynamic power consumption. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.5 | Agreement | For evaluation purposes, study extending NR UE power consumption model for UE operation with DL WUS of OFDM-based sequence, regarding the following aspects:<br>- Power state(s), sleep and non-sleep, and corresponding characteristics and power value(s)<br>- Transition energy and time for each of sleep state(s)<br>- Companies to report the assumption(s) for achieving the proposed power value(s), e.g., time/frequency domain detection, noise figure assumption(s), synchronization assumption(s), BW/antenna assumption(s), etc. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.5 | Agreement | Study and evaluate on-demand sync signal(s) mechanisms for 6GR energy efficiency, considering, e.g.,:<br>- On-demand sync signal(s) for single cell/carrier, multi-carrier/cell, multi-TRP,<br>- Network-triggered and UE-triggered on-demand sync signal(s),<br>- Idle and/or connected modes,<br>- Other mechanisms/aspects/signals/channels are not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| RAN1#122bis | 11.5 | Agreement | Study and evaluate multi-carrier/cells/TRPs mechanisms for 6GR NES, considering, e.g.,:<br>- Sync signal-less carriers/cells/TRPs for at least intra-band and collocated inter-band multi-carrier/cell/TRPs, including potential extensions to additional deployments and scenarios,<br>- RRC states,<br>- UE energy consumption and complexity,<br>- Other mechanisms/aspects/signals/channels are not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| RAN1#123 | 11.1 | Agreement | If the minimum spectrum allocation is 3MHz with 15kHz SCS for 6GR,<br>- Opt1: Design of the common signals/channels (at least for SSB) for initial access by assuming bandwidth larger than 3MHz, which is applicable to any spectrum allocations with adjustment, if applicable<br>- Opt2: A single design of the common signals/channels (at least for SSB) for initial access by assuming minimum spectrum allocation as target bandwidth 3MHz, which is applicable to any spectrum allocations | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| RAN1#123 | 11.1 | Agreement | Skeleton for TR 38.760-1 “Study on 6G Radio RAN1 aspects” v0.0.3 in R1-2509569 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| RAN1#123 | 11.1 | Agreement | - For scalable 6GR design for diverse device types, RAN1 can at least consider the following, targeting applicable to all 6G device types,<br>  - Basic initial access procedures from RAN1 perspective<br>  - Other PHY features after initial access procedure, e.g., Other DL/UL control, scheduling/HARQ<br>  - Coverage features to meet the identified coverage target<br>  - Energy saving both at BS and UE sides<br>  - MRSS<br>  - Note: whether these features are supported, mandatory or optional is separate discussion | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| RAN1#123 | 11.1 | Agreement | - For the RAN1 study of “Re-use of existing 5G mid-band (~3.5GHz) site grid for 6G deployments in at least around 7 GHz and targeting comparable coverage to 5G mid-band”,<br>  - The link budget template candidates 1 and 2 are used to calculate the metric(s) as starting point to compare existing 5G mid-band and 6G deployments in at least around 7 GHz, with potential future update.<br>  - During initial access/random access<br>    - Coverage target is referring the bottleneck channel (i.e. Rel-15 NR Msg3) during initial access/random access for existing 5G mid-band<br>  - FFS target value(s) of data rate for data channels relative to 5G mid-band<br>  - Following deployment scenarios are considered<br>    - Urban macro (both O2I and outdoor)<br>    - Sub-urban macro (both O2I and outdoor)<br>  - Following carrier frequencies are considered to calculate the metric(s)<br>    - [4 GHz] as the existing 5G mid-band<br>    - 7 GHz as 6G deployment<br>  - Template in R1-2509615 is to be used for collecting inputs on the values from companies.<br>[Post-123-R20-6GR-Overall] – Shinya (DCM)<br>Email discussion for collecting inputs on the values from companies for link budget template, Jan 26th until 30th | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| RAN1#123 | 11.1 | Agreement | - For the smallest maximum supported RF and BB UE BW without spectrum aggregation for at least one low-tier device type supported by 6GR framework, from physical layer perspective, RAN1 to consider at least<br>  - Overall device complexity<br>  - Overall system performance impact<br>  - Energy efficiency for both BS and UE<br>  - Aim at a single common signals/channels design in idle mode and initial access for diverse device types, as well as meeting mobile broadband service requirements as high priority | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| RAN1#123 | 11.2 | Agreement | Updating the BS antenna modelling agreed in the last meeting as follows:<br>- For around 700MHz carrier frequency, for BS antenna modelling,<br>- update the (8, 4, 2, 1, 1; x, y) to be (8, 4, 2, 1, 1; 1, 4).<br>- For around 2GHz carrier frequency, for BS antenna modelling,<br>- for outdoor combination 1 (i.e., 32AE/4TXRU), update the (M,N,P,Mg,Ng; Mp,Np) to be (8, 2, 2, 1, 1; 1, 2)<br>- For around 7GHz carrier frequency, for BS antenna modelling,<br>- for outdoor combination 1 (i.e., 768AE/128TXRU), update the (M,N,P,Mg,Ng; Mp,Np) to be (24, 16, 2, 1, 1; 4, 16).<br>- for outdoor combination 3 (i.e., 1536AE/256TXRU), update the (M,N,P,Mg,Ng; Mp,Np) to be (48, 16 ,2, 1, 1; 8, 16). | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |

| RAN1#123 | 11.2 | Agreement | For FTP Model 3, the packet delay budget (PDB) can be additionally considered,<br>- The latency characteristic of the traffic in RAN side (i.e., air interface) is modelled as packet delay budget (PDB). The PDB is a limited time budget for a packet to be transmitted over the air from a BS to a UE for DL, or from a UE to a BS for UL.<br>- For a given packet, the delay of the packet incurred in air interface is measured from the time that the packet arrives at the BS to the time that it is successfully transferred to the UE for DL, or from a UE to a BS for UL. If the delay is larger than a given PDB for the packet, the packet is said to violate PDB, otherwise the packet is said to be successfully delivered.<br>- Values for PDB, e.g., {10ms, 20ms, 30ms, 50ms, 100ms, 200ms, 300ms, 1000ms, 2000ms} can be considered.<br>- Which values will be used will consider the use case for the evaluations. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |
| RAN1#123 | 11.2 | Agreement | For 6GR evaluations related to Massive Communication (IoT),<br>- For comparability with 5G results and verify that 6G can meet the IMT-2030 connection density requirements, the mMTC traffic model from IMT-2020 (TR 37.910) may be used as a starting point. This traffic model can be applied in UL or DL.<br>- FFS: necessity of new traffic model(s) for 6GR evaluation in RAN1, e.g., for the following traffic types.<br>  - Triggered/polled reporting<br>  - Autonomous reporting (event-driven or periodic)<br>  - Remote actuation<br>  - Firmware/software upgrade | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |

| RAN1#123 | 11.2 | Agreement | - For FTP3 extension with multiple packet sizes (the number of packet size X =FFS: 2 or 3), FTP 3-extension 1<br>  - For each packet size S_i, the packets arrive according to Poisson distribution (as FTP 3) with mean inter-arrival time T_i  (or arrival rate λ_i where T_i = 1/ λ_i)<br>  - Y packet sizes are simulated for each UE<br>    - Down-select one from following<br>      - Alt1: Y=1; X=e.g., 2 or 3<br>      - Alt2: Y=X; X=e.g., 2 or 3<br>      - Alt3: Either Alt1 or Alt2 can be used depending on the evaluation purpose<br>  - FFS: values of S_i and T_i, and their inter-relation (if any)<br>  - FFS: change “packet size” to “File size” (terminology)<br>  - FFS timing relationship for different packet sizes if Y=X.<br>  - FFS the number of UEs for each of X different sizes in a drop if Y=1.<br>- Note: PDB can be considered separately if needed<br>- Note: modeling sessions with multiple packets in each session can be discussed separately if needed.<br>- Down-selection between X=2 and 3. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |
| RAN1#123 | 11.2 | Agreement | For traffic model(s) for AI/ML services, the following can be considered:<br>- Packet size:<br>  - How to model the packet size, a fixed one or multiple values, or modelled as a random variable.<br>- Packet arrival:<br>  - FFS the details to determine the packet arrival rate, e.g.,<br>    - N multiple packets arrive together as a burst. The burst interval time is modelled as a random variable.<br>      - Within the burst, the N packets arrive according to a statistical distribution.<br>    - Packets arrive separately.<br>  - FFS whether/how to model the Jitter and the relation with the packet arrival.<br>- FFS: Whether the packet importance is known. Whether/how to reflect the packet importance.<br>- Whether/How to consider the PDB, e.g., Packet delay budget: The latency characteristic of the traffic in RAN side (i.e., air interface) is modelled as packet delay budget (PDB). The PDB is a limited time budget for a packet to be transmitted over the air from a base station to a UE, or from a UE to a base station<br>- FFS Whether/how to consider the Packet success rate requirement: [xx%] and the relation with the PDB.<br>- FFS how to model different cases, e.g., image-based GenAI, video-based GenAI, and chatbot, etc.<br>- FFS: Whether/how other traffic models (e.g., XR, FTP1/3) can be used to reflect above characteristics.<br>Note: input from SA4 if any will be considered. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |

| RAN1#123 | 11.4.1 | Agreement | For the study of BG(s) and PCM(s) for LDPC extension for data rate beyond NR range, at least the following evaluation assumptions will be considered.<br>\| Parameters \| Values or assumptions \|<br>\| Channel \| AWGN \|<br>\| Modulation and code rate \| Uniform QAM modulations and corresponding code rates (i.e., (8,682.5/1024), (8, 797/1024), (8, 885/1024), (8, 948/1024)) in NR MCS Table 5.1.3.1-4 as starting point. Other modulation order, if used, can be reported. \|<br>\| Interleaver \| Same as 5G NR. Other interleaver scheme (intra-CB level), if used, can be reported. \|<br>\| Code block size (bits with CRC) \| CB size: same as 5G NR (8448 as baseline, other values less than 8448 can be reported). CB size: other value(s) larger than 8448 and no larger than 8448*2, e.g.,16k. Company to report the TBS. \|<br>\| Target CB BLER \| BLER=10-2, 10-4 \|<br>\| Decoding algorithm of LDPC \| Layered BP or min-sum(offset, normalized or adjusted min-sum) Company to report the details of offset, normalized or adjusted min-sum decoding algorithm, if used. Max number of iteration times: 5:1:20 Other iteration times can be reported. Decoding order: reversed order Other decoding order, if used, can be reported. \|<br>\| Demodulation algorithm \| Max-log-map \|<br>Note: For any comparison among 5G BG and BG(s)/PCM(s) proposed by companies, the BLER performance is compared at least under the same CB size.<br>Note: all evaluation assumptions above are for simulation only and have no implication on the final design of BG(s)/PCM(s).<br>For candidate comparison, both performance and complexity should be considered.<br>The comparison of BLER performance is under the same computation complexity,<br>- Computation complexity is defined as (the number of iteration times for required BLER) *(the number of ones in the lifted parity check matrix)/ (CB size) | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |

| RAN1#123 | 11.4.2 | Agreement | For PS/GS fixed MCS performance reporting for 10% BLER (other target x% BLER can also be reported), adopt the following format for simulation as a starting point for result reporting.<br>\| NR reference \| NR reference \| Scheme A (e.g, PS, 1D-NUC, 2D-NUC etc) \| Scheme A (e.g, PS, 1D-NUC, 2D-NUC etc) \| Scheme A (e.g, PS, 1D-NUC, 2D-NUC etc) \|<br>\| SE \| (Mod order, coding rate) \| SE point specific parameters \| Baseline (uniform QAM) SNR to reach target BLER \| Gain/loss in dB wrt NR baseline at target BLER x% \|<br>\| SE x \| (modOrder, coding rate) \| … \|  \|  \|<br>\| SE y \| (modOrder, coding rate) \| … \|  \|  \|<br>\| SE z \| (modOrder, coding rate) \| … \|  \|  \|<br>\| SE point independent assumptions \| SE point independent assumptions \| Common assumptions for the scheme simulated, including channel type (AWGN, SISO, SIMO, MIMO) and antenna configuration, number of spatial layers, number of RB allocated, TB size, shaping algorithm used (including block length), freq domain interleaver applied or not, receiver assumption, precoding assumption, realistic channel estimation, etc \| Common assumptions for the scheme simulated, including channel type (AWGN, SISO, SIMO, MIMO) and antenna configuration, number of spatial layers, number of RB allocated, TB size, shaping algorithm used (including block length), freq domain interleaver applied or not, receiver assumption, precoding assumption, realistic channel estimation, etc \| Common assumptions for the scheme simulated, including channel type (AWGN, SISO, SIMO, MIMO) and antenna configuration, number of spatial layers, number of RB allocated, TB size, shaping algorithm used (including block length), freq domain interleaver applied or not, receiver assumption, precoding assumption, realistic channel estimation, etc \|<br>Note: For NR MCS reference, since NR has multiple MCS tables, it is not enough to provide the MCS index. Instead, need to provide the (modulation order, coding rate) pair for the simulated SE<br>Note: For SE point specific parameters:<br>- For GS, this can be a pointer to the constellation used for this SE point<br>- For PS, this can be a constellation size, coding rate and shaping parameter used for this SE point<br>- Note: Other metrics (at least complexity) will be merged into the same table with other columns, if details of the metrics are agreeable.<br>- Note: For AMC study, if possible, we can use the same table format | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| RAN1#123 | 11.4.2 | Agreement | On how to evaluate complexity, storage requirement, delay and parallelism/serialism for PS and GS compared with uniform QAM.<br>- For PS<br>  - The demapper complexity is compared with uniform QAM demapper complexity<br>    - Can report the demapper complexity with PS and the demapper complexity of NR MCS with the same spectrum efficiency, and the ratio of the complexities<br>      - Also report the number of spatial layers, dm-algorithm used and the receiver type (e.g., LMMSE or rML), and fixed point assumed or floating point assumed.<br>  - The Distribution Matcher (DM)/Distribution De-Matcher (DDM) complexity and/or storage requirement as a function of the DM algorithm used (ESS, CCDM, etc), precision of fixed point implementation, block length, and the number of bit levels shaped per symbol<br>    - For complexity, can report the complexity normalized by the number of information bits<br>      - As a reference, can also report the computation complexity of LDPC decoding with 10 iterations.<br>    - For storage requirement, can report the overall storage needed for DM/DDM for supporting all MCS in the MCS table and all shaping related parameters<br>    - DM and DDM complexity and storage will be counted separately<br>  - The DM/DDM processing delay, parallelism/serialism, as a function of DM design and block length, and their impact to throughput<br>- For GS,<br>  - The demapper complexity is compared with uniform QAM demapper complexity<br>    - Can report the ratio of GS demapper complexity over the uniform QAM demapper complexity<br>      - Also report if 1D-NUC or 2D-NUC is used, # of spatial layers, and the receiver type (e.g., LMMSE or rML)<br>    - Also need to report the assumption on complexity counting, e.g, fixed point assumed or floating point assumed<br>  - The storage requirement for storing all the constellations corresponding to the MCS indices in the MCS table, as a function of precision of constellation point storage<br>  - Processing delay and parallelism/serialism, if applicable, and their impact to throughput<br>Note: the complexity is represented by the numbers of comparison, addition/subtraction, and multiplication/division operations, normalized by the number of information bits.<br>Note: For complexity as a function of SE point, will add a column in the already agreed performance reporting table.<br>Note: For complexity/storage not as a function of SE point, will add a row in the already agreed performance reporting table.<br>Note: Spec impact will be separately evaluated, include BICM, scrambling, etc<br>Note:<br>For 4K uniform QAM DL and 1K uniform QAM UL link level performance study, the following format can be used for performance reporting.<br>\| (modOrder, coding rate*1024, SE) \| Assumed TX/RX EVM \| Channel 1 \| Channel 2 \| Channel 3 \|<br>\| (modOrder, coding rate*1024, SE) \| Assumed TX/RX EVM \| SNR to achieve target BLER \| SNR to achieve target BLER \| SNR to achieve target BLER \|<br>\| (10, 900.5, 8.7939) for DL evaluation (8, 916.5, 7.1602) for UL evaluation \| Legacy EVM for 1K QAM for DL and 256QAM for UL respectively \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| (10, 948, 9.2578) for DL evaluation (8, 948, 7.4063) for UL evaluation \| Legacy EVM for 1K QAM for DL and 256 QAM for UL respectively \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| SE1 \|  \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| SE2 \|  \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| SE3 \|  \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| Other parameters \|  \|  \|  \|  \|<br>- For assumed TX/RX EVM, before we receive any concrete numbers from RAN4, companies can provide their assumptions. One example can be 6dB tighter than the EVM of 1K QAM for DL and 256QAM for UL.<br>- Other parameters include: Channel estimation assumption (genie or realistic), channel configurations (AWGN, SISO, SIMO, MIMO and TX/RX antenna configurations, channel types, number of spatial layers,), assumed residual freq offset range, number of allocated RBs, etc<br>- Two highest MCS points in DL 1K QAM and UL 256QAM in NR added in the table for comparison.<br>- This is preliminary result and not intended for TR<br>Note: For high order uniform QAM for DL 4K QAM and UL 1K QAM, to provide the UPT with the high order QAM (DL 4K QAM and UL 1K QAM) over the UPT without the high order QAM under the assumed deployment scenario. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| RAN1#123 | 11.4.2 | Agreement | For PS/GS fixed MCS performance reporting for 10% BLER (other target x% BLER can also be reported), adopt the following format for simulation as a starting point for result reporting.<br>\| NR reference \| NR reference \| Scheme A (e.g, PS, 1D-NUC, 2D-NUC etc) \| Scheme A (e.g, PS, 1D-NUC, 2D-NUC etc) \| Scheme A (e.g, PS, 1D-NUC, 2D-NUC etc) \|<br>\| SE \| (Mod order, coding rate) \| SE point specific parameters \| Baseline (uniform QAM) SNR to reach target BLER \| Gain/loss in dB wrt NR baseline at target BLER x% \|<br>\| SE x \| (modOrder, coding rate) \| … \|  \|  \|<br>\| SE y \| (modOrder, coding rate) \| … \|  \|  \|<br>\| SE z \| (modOrder, coding rate) \| … \|  \|  \|<br>\| SE point independent assumptions \| SE point independent assumptions \| Common assumptions for the scheme simulated, including channel type (AWGN, SISO, SIMO, MIMO) and antenna configuration, number of spatial layers, number of RB allocated, TB size, shaping algorithm used (including block length), freq domain interleaver applied or not, receiver assumption, precoding assumption, realistic channel estimation, etc \| Common assumptions for the scheme simulated, including channel type (AWGN, SISO, SIMO, MIMO) and antenna configuration, number of spatial layers, number of RB allocated, TB size, shaping algorithm used (including block length), freq domain interleaver applied or not, receiver assumption, precoding assumption, realistic channel estimation, etc \| Common assumptions for the scheme simulated, including channel type (AWGN, SISO, SIMO, MIMO) and antenna configuration, number of spatial layers, number of RB allocated, TB size, shaping algorithm used (including block length), freq domain interleaver applied or not, receiver assumption, precoding assumption, realistic channel estimation, etc \|<br>Note: For NR MCS reference, since NR has multiple MCS tables, it is not enough to provide the MCS index. Instead, need to provide the (modulation order, coding rate) pair for the simulated SE<br>Note: For SE point specific parameters:<br>- For GS, this can be a pointer to the constellation used for this SE point<br>- For PS, this can be a constellation size, coding rate and shaping parameter used for this SE point<br>- Note: Other metrics (at least complexity) will be merged into the same table with other columns, if details of the metrics are agreeable.<br>- Note: For AMC study, if possible, we can use the same table format | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| RAN1#123 | 11.4.2 | Agreement | To evaluate the proposal to allow a single spectrum efficiency point to be supported by multiple MCS entries (with different modulation order and coding rate combinations with uniform QAM or with different shaping parameters, coding rate, and constellation size combinations for PS and different coding rate and constellation combinations for GS).<br>- When providing results, to provide the following information<br>  - Details on the design of MCS table with overlapping MCS entries and expected size of MCS table, including performance comparison of designs with the same expected size of MCS table<br>  - Performance benefit under different channel and rank assumptions<br>    - As baseline, provide performance with legacy MCS table up to 256 QAM<br>      - Can additionally provide performance with legacy MCS table up to 1K QAM<br>    - For PS/GS, provide performance allowing each SE point to be mapped to one or more MCS entries<br>      - For PS/GS, provide performance allowing each SE point to be mapped to only one MCS entry (from the set of one or more MCS entries)<br>    - For uniform QAM, provide performance allowing each SE point to be mapped to one or more MCS entries<br>  - MCS selection mechanism across multiple MCS corresponding to the same spectrum efficiency.<br>    - If UE feedback is needed for gNB to select between multiple MCS entries corresponding to the same SE, provide details on what is to be fed back<br>- FFS: How different MPR for different modulation order is captured in the simulation for uplink<br>- FFS: How different EVM for different modulation order is captured in the simulation<br>- For the purpose of this study, the same set of SE points as in legacy uniform QAM table will be used as starting point.<br>- When reporting performance, also report other assumptions, including channel type (AWGN, SISO, SIMO, MIMO) and antenna configuration, number of spatial layers, number of RB allocated, TB size, shaping algorithm used (including block length), freq domain interleaver applied or not, receiver assumption, precoding assumption, realistic channel estimation, etc<br>- To propose how to align shaping parameters or how to align coding rate for facilitating comparison. | [Chair notes RAN1#123 - eom3](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_123/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23123%20-%20eom3.docx)<br>`[123-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |

| RAN1#124 | 10.1 | Agreement | Regarding FTP3 extension with multiple packet sizes:<br>- The number of packet sizes X = 2;<br>- For each packet size S_i, the packets arrive according to Poisson distribution (as FTP 3) with mean inter-arrival time T_i  (or arrival rate λ_i where T_i = 1/ λ_i);<br>- For Alt1: Y=1 packet size is simulated for each UE<br>  - For FTP3-extension with X=2, K is the ratio between arrival rates of the packet sizes, i.e., λ_1= K·λ_2, with K>=1, assuming S_1<S_2,<br>  - G>=1 is the ratio between the number of UEs with packet size S_1 and S_2, respectively.<br>  - Values of S_i, λ_i, i=1, 2, and G can be decided in evaluation phase.<br>  - Note: The following table is an illustration of the traffic configurations.<br>\|  \| Example 1 \| Example 1 \| Example 2 \| Example 2 \| Example 3 \| Example 3 \|<br>\| Packet Type \| Small size, i=1 \| Large size, i=2 \| Small size, i=1 \| Large size, i=2 \| Small size, i=1 \| Large size, i=2 \|<br>\| S_i \| 10KB \| 500KB \| 4KB \| 400KB \| 10KB \| 100KB \|<br>\| λ_i \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \|<br>\| G \| N_1= G·N_2, e.g., G=[2, 4, 9] \| N_1= G·N_2, e.g., G=[2, 4, 9] \| N_1= G·N_2, e.g., G=[2, 4, 9] \| N_1= G·N_2, e.g., G=[2, 4, 9] \| N_1= G·N_2, e.g., G=[2, 4, 9] \| N_1= G·N_2, e.g., G=[2, 4, 9] \|<br>\| G \| N_1 is the number of UEs with small packet size S_1; N_2 is the number of UEs with large packet size S_2. \| N_1 is the number of UEs with small packet size S_1; N_2 is the number of UEs with large packet size S_2. \| N_1 is the number of UEs with small packet size S_1; N_2 is the number of UEs with large packet size S_2. \| N_1 is the number of UEs with small packet size S_1; N_2 is the number of UEs with large packet size S_2. \|<br>- For Alt2: Y=X=2 packet sizes are simulated for each UE<br>  - The packet of each size is generated following the independent Poisson Process (S_i, λ_i) with i=1,2.<br>  - K is the ratio between arrival rates of the packet sizes, i.e., λ_1= K·λ_2, with K>=1, assuming S_1<S_2.<br>  - Values of (S_i, λ_i) with i=1,2 can be decided in evaluation phase.<br>  - Note: The following table is an illustration of the traffic configurations.<br>\|  \| Example 1 \| Example 1 \| Example 2 \| Example 2 \| Example 3 \| Example 3 \|<br>\| Packet Type \| Small size, i=1 \| Large size, i=2 \| Small size, i=1 \| Large size, i=2 \| Small size, i=1 \| Large size, i=2 \|<br>\| S_i \| [4]KB \| [800]KB \| 100KB \| 500KB \| 10KB \| 100KB \|<br>\| λ_i \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| λ_1= K·λ_2, e.g., K=[3, 5, 8, 10] \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |

| RAN1#124 | 10.2.1 | Agreement | Following metrics are used for SLS evaluations for multi-layer UL DFT-s-OFDM and CP-OFDM studies.<br>- User perceived throughput (UPT), including:<br>  - High percentile (90%)<br>  - mean<br>  - median<br>  - cell edge (5 & 10-percentile)<br>- Optional for full buffer traffic only: cell average throughput<br>- Companies are encouraged to report the CDF of instantaneous UL TX power across all UEs<br>- Companies are encouraged to report the statistics on the UL TX rank.<br>- Companies are encouraged to report the statistics on the applied MCS. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600788](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600788.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.2.1 | Agreement | Following metrics are used for LLS evaluation for multi-layer UL DFT-s-OFDM and CP-OFDM studies.<br>- BLER curves (for a subset of NR MCS (covering whole range with spanning), HARQ re-transmissions disabled) for same transmission rank, for same resource allocation and same transmission power for DFT-s-OFDM and CP-OFDM<br>  - Companies may derive Link-level throughput vs SNR based on BLER curves<br>- Netgain | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600788](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600788.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.2.1 | Agreement | For 2-layer/2Tx UL DFT-s-OFDM and CP-OFDM studies, following two cases are considered.<br>- UE capable of only non-coherent precoder<br>  - For UL CP-OFDM and DFT-s-OFDM rank-2 transmission, only non-coherent precoder option is allowed.<br>- UE capable of fully-coherent precoder<br>  - For UL CP-OFDM rank-2 transmission, all NR precoder options are allowed.<br>  - For UL DFT-s-OFDM rank-2 transmission, only non-coherent precoder option is allowed. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600788](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600788.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.2.1 | Agreement | For the multi-layer UL DFT-s-OFDM and CP-OFDM studies, the NR reference should be evaluated assuming the Release 16 full power mode 1 to be enabled. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600788](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600788.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.2.1 | Agreement | The following UL low-PAPR proposals for DFT-s-OFDM are for further consideration:<br>- Non-AI-ML-based<br>  - FDSS (R1-2601092, R1-2600751, R1-2600801, R1-2600823, R1-2600914, R1-2601156)<br>  - FDSS – spectrum extension (R1-2600027, R1-2601092, R1-2600823, R1-2600751, R1-2600914, R1-2601156, R1-2600261)<br>  - FDSS – spectrum truncation for π/2 BPSK (R1-2601268, R1-2601212, R1-2601092, R1-2601156)<br>  - Tone Reservation (R1-2600261, R1-2600716, R1-2601268)<br>    - For π/2 BPSK or other modulation orders<br>  - GMSK-Approximation based FDSS (R1-2600823)<br>  - 3-tap filter based FDSS (R1-2508684)<br>  - CFR-SE (R1-2600499)<br>  - Offset-QAM or π/2-PAM with FDSS-spectrum truncation (R1-2600909, R1-2600138, R1-2601268, R1-2600751)<br>  - Offset-QAM or π/2-PAM with FDSS-spectrum extension (R1-2600909, R1-2600138, R1-2600751, R1-2600823)<br>  - Interpolation-based modulation (R1-2600261)<br>  - AFDM based on DFT-s-OFDM (R1-2600999, R1-2601019)<br>- AI-ML-based<br>  - AI/ML-based waveform (R1-2600499, R1-2600751)<br>Note: tdoc numbers described in each proposal provide information on the proposal for further consideration, and it does not mean these tdocs support the proposal. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600789](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600789.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |

| RAN1#124 | 10.3.1 | Agreement | For performance evaluation purposes, the corresponding evaluation assumptions for study the Polar code segmentation enhancement used for L1 uplink control information with payload size beyond NR range and larger than 1706 bits, following evaluation assumptions are considered.<br>\| Channel \| AWGN \|<br>\| Modulation \| QPSK other modulation orders can be reported by companies \|<br>\| UL \| Code rate: 1/8, 1/6, 1/5, 1/4, 1/3, 1/2, 2/3, 3/4 Information bit length (bits w/o CRC) A: 1706+S: S: X Coded bit length X is up to companies to report. S: 16 \|<br>\| Decoding algorithm for Polar code \| CRC-aided SCL decoding Companies to report the list sizes either 8 or 16. \|<br>\| Target BLER of UCI payload \| 10-2, 10-3 \|<br>Note: 11 bits CRC per segment, rate matching, channel interleaver and code-block concatenation follow NR.<br>Note: Companies to report other values that are used in their evaluations. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601568](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601568.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.3.1 | Agreement | For the study of LDPC extension beyond NR range, the maximum lifting size is not larger than 384<br>- FFS: the exact value of the maximum lifting size | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601569](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601569.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.3.1 | Agreement | For the base graph of LDPC extension beyond NR range, the following is identified in RAN1#124 for further study<br>- The LDPC extension is quasi-cyclic LDPC codes, the parity check matrix of quasi-cyclic LDPC Codes is defined at least by a matrix H of size (mb×z)×(nb×z), which consists of sub-block matrices of size z×z, where each sub-block matrix is composed by circularly shifted matrices or zero matrices. Wherein, mb, nb and z are integers larger than 1.<br>- The values of mb, nb and z are FFS.<br>- The row weight of circularly shifted matrices is less than or equal to 2.<br>- FFS: Whether row weight of all circularly shifted matrices is 1, or the row weight of some circularly shifted matrices is 1 and the row weight of some circularly shifted matrices is 2. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601666](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601666.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.3.1 | Agreement | For study of LDPC extension beyond NR range, at least the following metrics are considered for the performance comparison among BG1 and BG(s)/PCM(s) proposed by companies<br>- Computational complexity difference to achieve the same BLER performance as BG1 under the reference maximum number of iterations of BG1.<br>- SNR performance difference with the same computation complexity as BG1 under the reference maximum number of iterations of BG1.<br>- The reference maximum number of iterations is 5, 7, 10, 15 based on BG1.<br>  - Other values can be also reported by companies<br>- Note: Companies can report the average number of iterations difference to achieve the same performance as BG1 under the reference maximum number of iterations of BG1. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601666](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601666.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.3.2 | Agreement | - Parameters affect the PS complexity/storage/latency and BLER and throughput performance trade-off are at least:<br>- DM output length in unit of I/Q symbol,<br>- # of bits shaped per I/Q,<br>- # of shaping parameters<br>- # of DM blocks needed to support the target throughput of 6GR<br>- DM algorithm and bit-width of variables in the DM algorithm<br>- Impact of mismatch on the quantization bitwidth between DM and DDM.<br>- Parameters affect the GS complexity/storage/latency and BLER and throughput performance trade-off are at least:<br>- 1D-NUC or 2D-NUC<br>- Constellations and  # of constellations<br>- Bitwidth for describing the constellation<br>- Bits to constellation mapping<br>- The values of these parameters used in evaluations shall be submitted together with performance results.<br>- Companies are encouraged to provide evaluations for different combination parameters to study different performance and complexity/storage/latency trade-offs to provide proper assessment including feasibility . | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |

| RAN1#124 | 10.3.2 | Agreement | - For PS, potential impact to the TX/RX chain functionality blocks are identified as follows:<br>- TX chain<br>  - (Modified) TBS calculation<br>  - (Modified) CB segmentation<br>  - (New) DM functionalities<br>    - Bit splitting: Split to shaped bits and unshaped bits<br>    - DM<br>    - Bit concatenation/multiplexing: Concatenate/multiplex DM output and unshaped bits<br>  - (Modified) Bit interleaver and Bit selection<br>  - (Modified) Scrambling: shaped bits should not be scrambled to keep the target distribution<br>  - (Modified) Modulation: Power normalization needed for shaped constellation<br>- RX chain<br>  - (Modified) TBS calculation<br>  - (New) DM functionalities<br>    - Bit splitting: Split to shaped bits and unshaped bits<br>    - DDM<br>    - Bit concatenation/demultiplexing: Concatenate/demultiplex DDM output with unshaped bits<br>  - (Modified) Bit de-interleaver and Bit selection<br>  - (Modified) Descrambling:<br>  - (Modified) Demodulation: Prior probability used in demodulation<br>  - (Modified) CB concatenation<br>Companies are encouraged to provide design details for the modification needed for above functionalities.<br>Companies are encouraged to explain the reason if a functionality block is not impacted. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601551](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601551.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |

| RAN1#124 | 10.3.2 | Agreement | - For GS, potential impact to the TX/RX chain functionality blocks compared to NR are identified as follows:<br>- TX chain<br>  - Mapper<br>    - Bit to constellation symbol mapping<br>  - Modulation symbol generation<br>- RX chain<br>  - Demodulation of received symbols<br>  - Demapper<br>Companies are encouraged to identify potential impact on TX, RX chains if both NUC and uniform QAM are supported.<br>Companies are encouraged to provide design details for the modification needed for above functionalities.<br>Companies are encouraged to explain the reason if a functionality block is not impacted. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601662](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601662.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |

| RAN1#124 | 10.5.0 | Agreement | 6GR shall at least be capable of configuring the same TDD slot configurations as TDD slot configurations deployed in 5G NR. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.5.0 | Agreement | Study link direction determination for dynamic TDD, considering at least<br>- UE PDCCH monitoring efforts and power consumption<br>- Signaling overhead<br>- Scheduling flexibility<br>- Avoid duplicated functionalities<br>- Collision handling | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | For initial access and mobility in 6GR, study the following deployment scenarios<br>- Single beam and multi-beam based deployments<br>- Single TRP and multi-TRP based deployments<br>- Single carrier and multi-carrier deployments<br>- Other deployment scenarios | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |
| RAN1#124 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | For 6GR paging transmission/reception, study at least the following aspects:<br>- Study paging transmission scheme(s) to facilitate network energy savings<br>- Study paging scheme(s) to facilitate UE energy savings<br>- Study necessity of paging capacity enhancement<br>- Study necessity of paging coverage enhancement | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |
| RAN1#124 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | Study 6GR signals, channels and procedures for initial access and idle mobility, considering at least<br>- Cell/Initial Cell search and cell ID identification<br>- Time/frequency synchronization/tracking<br>- Beam measurement<br>- System information acquisition<br>- Whether TRP is transparent/non-transparent to UE during above procedures<br>Study 6GR signals, channels and procedures for idle mobility, considering at least<br>- Cell search and cell ID identification<br>- Time/frequency synchronization/tracking<br>- Beam measurement<br>- System information acquisition<br>- Whether TRP is transparent/non-transparent to UE during above procedures | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |
| RAN1#124 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | For 6GR measurements in initial access and for mobility, study measurement resource, measurement quantity, measurement functionality and measurement procedure, at least including:<br>- L1 measurements<br>- Cell-level/[TRP-level] and beam-level measurement | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |
| RAN1#124 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | Study beam operation during 6GR initial access, including:<br>- Beam acquisition during initial access<br>- Beam report/refinement during initial access<br>- Feasibility and performance of spatial/temporal beam prediction during initial access | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |
| RAN1#124 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | Basic 6GR sync signal structure is defined as 6GR SSB, which consists of primary synchronization signal(s), secondary synchronization signal(s) and physical broadcast channel(s)<br>- FFS: Other types of 6GR sync signal/channel structure or reference signal and their structure | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |
| RAN1#124 | 10.5.1.2 | Agreement | Study random access framework with the following aspects:<br>- Enablement of energy efficient random access procedures (supporting SID objective 1b);<br>  - Including both network and UE power saving<br>- Coverage improvement (supporting SID objective 1d);<br>- Support of random access for diverse device types and capabilities (supporting SID objective 1g);<br>- System performance improvement from overhead reduction, simplification of signaling/configurations (supporting SID objective 1k);<br>- Additionally consider following aspects<br>  - random access latency;<br>  - capacity<br>  - detection reliability;<br>  - high speed mobility;<br>- Note: Other aspects identified during future discussions are not excluded.<br>The following scenarios and assumptions beyond single carrier/TRP are considered for the study of above random access framework:<br>- NTN<br>- SBFD<br>- multi-carrier<br>- multi-TRP<br>- Note: whether/how to support one or more of the scenarios/assumptions, including whether any special handling or functionality needs to be introduced in support of the scenarios/assumptions is part of the study. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |

| RAN1#124 | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | For 6G PDCCH study, consider at least the following aspects:<br>  - Control-channel blocking<br>  - Coverage<br>  - UE energy consumption<br>  - UE complexity<br>  - Coexistence of different device types<br>  - Reliability of PDCCH transmission<br>  - NW complexity and scheduling<br>  - Resource efficiency and overhead<br>  - Latency<br>  - MRSS | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |

| RAN1#124 | 10.5.4.1 | Agreement | Study the adaptation of PDCCH monitoring in 6GR for UE energy efficiency purpose, considering at least:<br>- Impact on network energy efficiency, as well as complexity and performance<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>Note: 6GR DL WUS related discussions are handled in AI 10.6. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
