| RAN1#124 | 10.5.4.1 | Agreement | RAN1 to study L1 signalling framework for time-domain scheduling/resource allocation for downlink and uplink transmission by considering, e.g., within a slot or across the slot boundary, PXSCH repetitions, and other necessary aspects if any.<br>- Note1: PXSCH corresponds to PDSCH or PUSCH.<br>- Note2: it doesn’t mean those examples are confirmed<br>- Note3: The descriptions/figures for scheduling options in [R1-2601584, FL summary] is an example for information. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.5.4.3 | Agreement | In 6GR, DL and UL HARQ operation designs considers at least the following aspects:<br>  - latency<br>  - reliability<br>  - coverage<br>  - power saving (NW and UE)<br>  - NW complexity<br>  - UE complexity<br>  - diverse services/applications/traffics<br>  - system efficiency/system throughput/user throughput<br>  - feedback efficiency/UL and DL overhead<br>Note: the design of DL and UL HARQ does not necessarily be the same | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.5.4.3 | Agreement | For DL HARQ in 6GR, study both following HARQ-ACK feedback mechanisms<br>- Mechanism 1: HARQ-ACK information bits are transmitted via L1 signalling<br>- Mechanism 2: HARQ-ACK information bits are transmitted via higher layer signalling (e.g., MAC CE) | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.5.4.3 | Agreement | For discussion purposes,<br>- Asynchronous HARQ refers to that retransmission(s) occurs in a non pre-determined occasion once the corresponding initial transmission is scheduled.<br>- Adaptive HARQ refers that the transmission parameters and resources for the retransmission can be adaptively adjusted.<br>- For DL and UL in 6GR, support asynchronous and adaptive HARQ operation. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.5.4.3 | Agreement | Study possible HARQ-ACK payload size range. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.5.4.3 | Agreement | In 6GR, support at least TB level granularity for HARQ-ACK feedback | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.5.5 | Agreement | Study the UE-to-UE cross-link interference for 6GR:<br>- Study and identify the applicable scenarios of UE-to-UE cross-link interference.<br>- Study the characteristics of UE-to-UE cross-link interference, for example what is the potential interference signal level, what is the potential impact on system operation.<br>- Study the mechanisms and techniques for handling UE-to-UE cross-link interference, including UE-to-UE cross-link interference measurement and reporting<br>  - For each candidate mechanism, evaluate and analyze the performance benefit, impact to the system and complexity at BS/UE.<br>  - Study the candidate resource or signals/channels for measuring UE-to-UE cross-link interference.<br>  - Study and identify the candidate measurement quantities.<br>  - Study the reporting mechanisms | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.5.5 | Agreement | Study BS-to-BS cross-link interference for 6GR:<br>- Study and identify the applicable scenarios of BS-to-BS cross-link interference.<br>- Study the characteristics of BS-to-BS cross-link interference, for example what is the potential interference signal level, what is the potential impact to the system operation.<br>- Study the mechanisms and techniques that can handle the BS-to-BS cross-link interference, including the mechanisms for measuring and identifying the BS-to-BS cross-link interference:<br>  - For each considered mechanism, evaluate the performance benefit, impact to the system operation, and complexity at BS/UE.<br>  - The candidate resource for measuring BS-to-BS cross-link interference.<br>  - The measurement quantities at least for evaluation purpose. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.5.5 | Agreement | For handling remote interference in 6GR:<br>- Study the applicable scenarios for remote interference between remote cells due to atmospheric ducting;<br>- Study the impact of remote interference to the system, including the impact to the uplink reception.<br>- Study the characteristics of the remote interference.<br>- Study the candidate mechanisms for measuring/detecting/identifying remote interference, mechanisms to mitigate remote interference.<br>  - For each candidate mechanism, evaluate the benefits and impact to the system.<br>Note: UE reporting to request uplink resource scheduling is to be discussed under this agenda | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.5.5 | Agreement | Study the UE reporting mechanism for requesting uplink resource with considering at least the following aspects:<br>- Applicable use cases, at least including requesting resource for UL data transmission<br>- UL transmission latency<br>- Signalling overhead<br>- UL resource efficiency<br>- System capacity<br>- Network/UE complexity<br>- other aspects are not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.6.1.1 | Agreement | Targeting for same coverage as 6G PDCCH in the same band, Study DL WUS coverage by considering at least the following aspects.<br>- Missed detection rate<br>- False alarm rate<br>- RRC state differences<br>- Different use cases<br>- Reference configuration of PDCCH | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.6.1.1 | Agreement | The same SCS is assumed for DL WUS as for the 6GR Sync signals in the same band if sync signals and data channels use the same SCS.<br>FFS SCS of DL WUS if sync signals and data channels use different SCS in FR2-1 | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.6.1.1 | Agreement | Study the following aspects of DL WUS design:<br>- Time or frequency domain sequence definition<br>- Time and frequency resource allocation<br>- Multiplexing and coexistence with other signals and channels, including DL WUS<br>- PAPR and BS/UE processing complexity<br>- Network overhead and NW&UE energy efficiency<br>- Other aspects are not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.6.1.2 | Agreement | Study 6GR DL WUS triggering PDCCH monitoring with and without C-DRX in RRC connected state, considering at least:<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>- Impact to UPT, if applicable<br>- Impact to latency, if applicable<br>- Impact to QoS/delay budget satisfaction rate, if applicable<br>- Network overhead/complexity<br>- Network energy consumption<br>- Other impacts, if any | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.6.1.2 | Agreement | For RRC idle state, study serving cell RRM measurement based on 6GR measurement signal (e.g., at least 6GR sync signal) by EE processing, considering at least:<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>  - measurement based on EE processing together with DL-WUS monitoring vs measurement based on non-EE processing together with DL-WUS monitoring<br>- coverage (e.g., achievable SINR/SNR) and accuracy<br>- Impact on the EE processing complexity<br>For RRC idle state, study neighboring cell RRM measurement based on 6GR measurement signal (e.g., at least 6GR sync signal) by EE processing, considering at least:<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>  - measurement based on EE processing together with DL-WUS monitoring vs measurement based on non-EE processing together with DL-WUS monitoring<br>- Neighboring cell identification, measurement and evaluation<br>- Neighboring cell number limitation, if any<br>- Inter-cell interference<br>- Coverage (e.g., achievable SINR/SNR) and accuracy<br>- Impact on the EE processing complexity<br>- FFS the power consumption of neighboring cell RRM measurement based on EE processing<br>Note: It doesn’t mean the measurement in EE processing has to be coupled with DL WUS monitoring | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.6.2 | Agreement | Study further necessity and feasibility of UL WUS under the following deployment scenario (DS):<br>- DS#1: Standalone cell where UE obtains UL WUS configuration e.g., from standalone cell and/or pre-defined in the specifications<br>  - DS#1a: Prior to UL WUS, nothing transmitted on the standalone cell<br>  - DS#1b: Prior to UL WUS, always-on 6GR synchronization signals with or without PBCH on the standalone cell but no SIB1 on the standalone cell<br>  - DS#1c: Prior to UL WUS, always-on 6GR synchronization signals with PBCH on the standalone cell and periodic SIB1 on the standalone cell<br>  - Note: UL WUS is transmitted to the standalone cell<br>- RAN1 to consider at least<br>  - Coverage target of UL WUS<br>  - How UE acquires synchronization for UL WUS<br>  - How UE decides UL WUS transmission power<br>  - RRC states for the above scenarios<br>- Note: The above scenarios can be applicable to multi-TRP deployment scenario if TRP is transparent to UE at the time of UL WUS transmission<br>- Note: The above scenarios are for study purposes only and do not imply 6GR support. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.6.2 | Agreement | Study further necessity and feasibility of UL WUS under the following deployment scenario (DS):<br>- DS#2: Multi-cell/carrier where UE obtains UL WUS configuration from cell/carrier #1<br>  - Always-on 6GR synchronization signals with PBCH on cell/carrier #1, and SIB1 on cell/carrier #1<br>  - DS#2a: Prior to UL WUS, nothing transmitted on cell/carrier #2<br>  - DS#2b: Prior to UL WUS, always-on 6GR synchronization signals with or without PBCH on cell/carrier #2 but no SIB1 on cell/carrier #2<br>  - DS#2c: Prior to UL WUS, 6GR synchronization signals with PBCH on cell/carrier #2 and periodic SIB1 on cell/carrier #2<br>  - Note: UL WUS can be transmitted to cell/carrier #1 or cell/carrier #2<br>- RAN1 to consider at least<br>  - Coverage target of UL WUS<br>  - How UE acquires synchronization for UL WUS<br>  - How UE decides UL WUS transmission power<br>  - Whether the cells/carriers are in the same band or not<br>  - Whether the cells/carriers are collocated or not<br>  - RRC states for the above scenarios<br>  - Whether the above scenarios can be applicable to multi-TRP deployment scenario<br>- Note: The above scenarios are for study purposes only and do not imply 6GR support. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.7.1 | Agreement | For NTN link budget template, RAN1 to take the TN link budget template as baseline with specific rows / values (including adding new rows) to be further discussed. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601469](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601469.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.7.1 | Agreement | RAN1 will define evaluation parameters for at least the following combinations of satellite orbit and bands:<br>- S-band:<br>  - LEO 300, LEO 600, GEO<br>- Ka band:<br>  - [LEO 300], LEO 600, [LEO 1200], GEO<br>- Ku band:<br>  - LEO 1200, GEO<br>NOTE 1: The evaluations for S band are expected to be similar to L-band.<br>NOTE 2: This is only for the purpose of evaluations. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601469](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601469.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.7.1 | Agreement | RAN1 to use the following terminology when discussing GNSS availability at least for physical layer operation.<br>- GNSS-based: Refers to the network mode of operation which relies on devices being equipped with a GNSS receiver and the devices can obtain a position fix within a given accuracy<br>  - FFS: How often the UE may be required to obtain a position fix, which may be related to the required accuracy.<br>  - FFS: if position under this operation can be obtained by means other than GNSS that provides a comparable accuracy, e.g. pre-configuration for a fixed device. This may also include information other than positioning.<br>- GNSS-degraded: Refers to the network mode of operation which relies on devices being equipped with a GNSS receiver, the devices were able to obtain a position fix at some point in time, but the devices may not currently have a position fix within a given accuracy.<br>  - NOTE: The UE may be able to use the position fix for physical layer operation<br>- GNSS-free/GNSS-less: Refers to the network mode of operation which does not rely on devices being equipped with a GNSS receiver, or devices are equipped with a GNSS receiver but do not have a current position fix that can be used for physical layer operation. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601469](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601469.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.7.1 | Agreement | 6GR NTN targets to support GNSS-based operation, GNSS-degraded operation and GNSS-less/GNSS-free operation. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601470](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601470.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.7.1 | Agreement | 6GR NTN uplink time-frequency synchronization follows the same principle as NR NTN as baseline:<br>- The concept of “uplink synchronization reference point” is introduced in 6GR NTN.<br>- 6GR NTN provides satellite assistance information<br>- At least for GNSS-based operation, it is supported that UE uses its own location information + satellite assistance information to perform time-frequency pre-compensation. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601470](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601470.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.7.1 | Conclusion | As a general principle for 6GR NTN study:<br>- Under NTN agenda item, we will identify issues / requirements specific to NTN.<br>- Potential solutions to these issues / requirements may be studied under the NTN agenda<br>  - The outcome of this study may be discussed under other agenda items if common design is possible.<br>  - These solutions may end up resulting in an extension of the TN design.<br>  - This may depend on the solution / issue / requirement<br>- NTN specific solutions may be introduced when a common / extended design cannot meet the NTN requirements.<br>- When targeting a common design TN performance is prioritized. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601470](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601470.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.7.1 | Agreement | 6GR supports large scheduling offsets to accommodate the RTT introduced by the satellite channel. Further discuss how to realize these scheduling offsets:<br>- Option 1: Reuse the k_offset concept from NR as a baseline with potential modifications.<br>  - NOTE: Under this option, additional scheduling offsets (e.g. similar to K1/K2 in NR) may be supported for scheduling flexibility, which apply in addition to k_offset.<br>- Option 2: The large scheduling delays to accommodate the RTT are incorporated into the scheduling offsets (e.g. similar to K1/K2 in NR), which may or may not be common for TN and NTN | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601471](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601471.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.7.1 | Conclusion | When reporting inputs for the link budget template and evaluation assumptions to RAN1#124b, companies are encouraged to provide them in an xls attached to their contribution following the format of the xls attached to R1-2601471. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601471](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601471.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124 | 10.7.1 | Agreement | Study NTN specific issues/requirements at least for the following aspects for 6GR NTN under this agenda:<br>- Uplink time-frequency synchronization<br>- HARQ issues: e.g. enable/disable HARQ feedback (which may include how to efficiently operate with HARQ feedback enabled/disabled), number of HARQ processes, etc.<br>- Timing relationships with large RTT.<br>- Coverage target<br>- Physical layer aspects of multi-satellite operation (including multi-orbit)<br>- Aspects related to Multiple beams per satellite<br>- Aspects related to satellite having more beam footprints than simultaneously active beams<br>Other aspects are not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601471](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601471.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.2.1 | Agreement | Prioritize the following cases in the RAN1 study on UL coverage improvements through low UL PAPR enhancement for DFT-s-OFDM, based on Table in 5.3.1 of R1-2603312:<br>- FDSS [for π/2 BPSK, QPSK, 16QAM]<br>  - Opt1: Transparent filter<br>    - Filter defined through transmitter requirement such as spectrum flatness by RAN4<br>  - Opt2: non-transparent filter<br>    - Filter coefficients specified in RAN1<br>  - Note: This includes all the listed FDSS filtering variants<br>- FDSS for [π/2 BPSK, QPSK, 16QAM] with spectral extension<br>  - For FDSS part,<br>    - Opt1: Transparent filter<br>      - Filter defined through transmitter requirement such as spectrum flatness by RAN4<br>    - Opt2: non-transparent filter<br>      - Filter coefficients specified in RAN1<br>  - Note: This includes all the listed variants of the spectrum extension<br>- FDSS for π/2 BPSK with spectral truncation<br>  - For FDSS part,<br>    - Opt1: Transparent filter<br>      - Filter defined through transmitter requirement such as spectrum flatness by RAN4<br>    - Opt2: non-transparent filter<br>      - Filter coefficients specified in RAN1<br>  - Note: This includes all the listed variants of the spectrum truncation<br>  - Note: At least one variant of spectral extension, offset-QPSK with smaller DFT size can result in the same signal as the FDSS for π/2 BPSK-ST<br>- Note: companies are encouraged to report whether transparent CFR is used or not | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603312](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603312.zip) |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.2.1 | Conclusion | The study on the following listed proposals is noted. Further discussion on these is not planned under the waveform agenda item in RAN1 unless tasked by RAN at or after RAN#112.<br>\| Proposal \| Company providing evaluation (please include the Tdoc#) \| Based on your results, indicate if you believe that the proposal should be considered for potential inclusion to 6GR \|<br>\| TR (downlink) \| ZTE (R1-2602275) \| ZTE: YES \|<br>\| DFT-s-OFDM with enhanced time domain resource multiplexing \| ZTE (R1-2602275) \| ZTE: YES \|<br>\| GFB-OFDM \| ZTE (R1-2602275) \| ZTE: YES \|<br>\| SLM \| ZTE (R1-2508856) CATT（R1-2602492） \| ZTE: YES CATT: YES \|<br>\| Zak-OTFS-over-OFDM \| Cohere (R1-2602263) \| Cohere: YES \|<br>\| Zak-OTFS \| Cohere (R1-2602263) \| Cohere: YES \|<br>\| Interlace OFDM \|  \|  \|<br>\| Spectral Precoding \| NICT(R1-2602831) \| NICT: YES \|<br>\| OSDM \| Sheffield (R1-2602259) \| Shef: YES \|<br>\| CP-OFDM with time-domain precoding \| PCL (R1-2602302) \| PCL: YES \|<br>\| DFT-s-OFDM with flexible frequency domain mapping (e.g., frequency domain data-DMRS multiplexing) \| QC (R1-2603008) CATT（R1-2602492） \| QC: Yes CATT: Yes \|<br>\| Multi-Tx Enhancement for DFT-s-OFDM \| QC (R1-2603008) IITH, Wisig (R1-2602880) \| QC: Yes IITH, Wisig: Yes \|<br>\| Two-segment DFT-S-OFDM \| CATT（R1-2602492） \| CATT:Yes \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603313](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603313.zip) |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.3.1 | Agreement | For the study of LDPC extension beyond NR range, the maximum code block size of 8448 is assumed for further study with the following two options:<br>- Option 1: 44 information columns in BG and a maximum lifting size of 192<br>- Option 2: 22 information columns in BG and a maximum lifting size of 384<br>Note: Aim to decide between option 1 and option 2 during this week. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.3.1 | Agreement | For the study of LDPC extension beyond NR range, the mother code rate of approximately 2/3 is assumed for further study.<br>- The above agreement is only applicable for study of the new BG. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.3.1 | Agreement | For the study of LDPC extension beyond NR range, for the maximum information block size of 8448 bits, the total number of ones in the lifted parity check matrix under mother code rate is no larger than N, where N is determined by the total number of ones in the lifted parity check matrix of BG1 under the code rate of X.<br>- X is not lower than 1/2.<br>- FFS: Value of X. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.3.1 | Agreement | For the study of LDPC extension beyond NR range, for the study of new BG, assume the parity check matrix for encoder consists of five sub-matrices (A, B, C, D, E) for further study, wherein<br>  - Sub-matrix A contains columns that correspond to information bits.<br>  - Sub-matrix C is a zero matrix<br>  - Sub-matrix D: the number of columns of sub-matrix D equals to (the number of columns of Sub-matrix A) + (the number of columns of Sub-matrix B)<br>  - Sub-matrix B contains columns that correspond to core parity check bits<br>  - For sub-matrix B, consider the following options for further study, and strive for down-selection in RAN1#125<br>    - Option B1: A weight-3 column with corresponding shifting values of [0, X, 0] or [X, 0, X], followed by a dual-diagonal structure with corresponding shifting values of [0,0]<br>    - Option B2: At most 2 weight-3 columns and dual-diagonal structure(s) with comparable number of XOR and cyclic shift operations with Option B1, e.g.,<br>        - Option B2-1: Sub-matrix B has a weight-3 column with corresponding shifting values of [a, b, c], followed by a dual-diagonal structure<br>        - Option B2-2: Sub-matrix B can be decomposed into two sets: set 1 and set 2. Each set follows Option B1/ Option B2-1, with additional pair-wise orthogonality across sets, e.g.,<br>  - For sub-matrix E, the following options are identified for further study, and strive for down-selection in RAN1#125<br>    - Option E1: An identity matrix<br>    - Option E2: A lower triangular matrix | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603340](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603340.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.3.1 | Agreement | For the study of LDPC extension beyond NR range, for study of the new BG, consider a single edge BG with 44 information columns and a maximum lifting size of 192, with the following options identified for further study, and strive for down-selection among the options in RAN1#125<br>- Option 1: Row splitting, wherein one or more rows outside the core matrix (i.e., sub matrix A and sub matrix B) are split from at least some rows inside the core matrix, and the core column indices of the non-zero entries of the original row are a superset of those of the corresponding split row,<br>    - The core matrix contains Y1 rows, and all elements of Y2 rows in sub-matrix A are ones.<br>        - Y1= 3 or 4<br>        - Y1Y21<br>            - Sub-option 1-1: With at least the first row in sub-matrix A included in the Y2 rows<br>            - Sub-option 1-2: The first row in sub-matrix A is not included in the Y2 rows.<br>    - No puncturing before rate matching<br>- Option 2: The single edge BG with 44 information columns and a maximum lifting size of 192 can be convertible to a BG with 22 information columns and a maximum lifting size of 384. More specifically, each 2x2 submatrix at column 2j, 2j+1, and row 2k, 2k+1 of the BG can only take one of the following forms:<br>, , , .<br>    - No puncturing or puncturing one or two columns before rate matching, wherein the puncturing (if any) is applied within the first two information columns and/or any core parity columns<br>- Option 3: Quasi-row orthogonality in core matrix (i.e., sub matrix A and sub matrix B) and/or sub matrix D, i.e., in which except for certain columns, the row 2k, 2k+1 do not share non-zero entries in the same column in the sub-matrix A and/or sub-matrix D<br>    - No puncturing or puncturing one or two columns before rate matching, wherein the puncturing (if any) is applied within the first two information columns and/or any core parity columns<br>- The options above are not mutually exclusive and can be combined.<br>- Other options that have a single edge BG with 44 information columns and a maximum lifting size of 192 are not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603411](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603411.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.3.2 | Agreement | At least support 5G NR equal probability uniform QAM constellation for 6G:<br>- For 6GR DL, 5G NR equal probability uniform QPSK, 16QAM, 64QAM, 256QAM and 1024QAM are supported for CP-OFDM for data channel<br>- For 6GR UL, 5G NR equal probability uniform QPSK, 16QAM, 64QAM, and 256QAM are supported for CP-OFDM for data channel<br>- For 6GR UL, 5G NR pi/2 BPSK, equal probability uniform QPSK, 16QAM, 64QAM, and 256QAM are supported for DFT-s-OFDM for data channel<br>  - Note: Further enhancements for low PAPR are being discussed in waveform agenda, and will be decided there<br>Note: The support of equal probability uniform QAM does not have any implication to the study and potential inclusion of shaping based schemes and the study of waveform discussions.<br>Note: Other equal probability uniform modulation schemes and modulation orders, including but not limited to DL 4kQAM and UL 1k QAM, can be separately discussed and can be additionally supported if justified.<br>FFS: Impact on modulation if the waveform agenda item concludes on any waveform enhancements. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603303](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603303.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.3.2 | Agreement | For SLS study for DL 4K uniform QAM without shaping and UL 1K uniform QAM without shaping for CPE in FWA scenario, focus on the following subset of parameters for Dense Urban and Urban Macro:<br>- Layout: Single layer<br>- Frequency: Around 4GHz and/or Around 7GHz<br>- BS antenna model: Outdoor Combination 2 for around 4GHz and Outdoor Combination 2 for around 7GHz<br>- BS power: 44dBm/20MHz for around 4GHz, and 43dBm/20MHz for around 7GHz<br>- O2I penetration loss: Option 2: 50% low loss, 50% high loss<br>- Traffic model: FTP3<br>- UE distribution and UE speed:<br>  - Profile 1 (mixed deployment): 80% Indoor CPE: (0, 0.3] km/h; 20% Outdoor rooftop mounted CPE: (0, 0.3]km/h.<br>  - Profile 3 (Outdoor mounted CPE only): Roof-top mounted, 100% outdoor (0,0.3] km/h<br>  - For outdoor rooftop mounted CPE, consider uniform distributed in 4-8 floors for building height.<br>- UE antenna panel: Combination 3 Alt 1 for around 4GHz, combination 4 for around 7GHz<br>  - Tx configuration of UE antenna panel for UL 1024QAM<br>  - Companies to report antenna orientation<br>- UE power class: 31dBm, 35dBm (Other values can be reported by companies)<br>- Simulation BW: 100MHz, 20MHz<br>- Maximum rank per CPE UE: 2, 4, 8 (optional)<br>- Channel estimation: Realistic channel estimation<br>- Assume MU-MIMO capability is available at gNB<br>- MCS table including the additional MCS entries for DL 4K QAM and UL 1K QAM provided by company<br>- Other parameter setting not precluded and company can report<br>For SLS result reporting, also include<br>- Rank distribution (for indoor CPE and outdoor LOS CPE and outdoor non-LOS CPE separately and jointly)<br>  - Rank distribution when 4KQAM DL and 1KQAM UL is selected.<br>- Modulation order (MCS) distribution (for indoor CPE and outdoor LOS CPE and outdoor non-LOS CPE separately and jointly)<br>- Note: RAN4 input is needed for the following parameters:<br>  - EVM assumption and modelling<br>  - MPR assumption | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603303](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603303.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |

BW scaling for other DL signal/channel processing is applied if the active BW is different from the reference.<br>- BW and antenna scaling rules can be applied<br>- Note: FR1 includes ~7GHz, and FR2 includes 24.25 GHz – 52.6 GHz | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.0 | Agreement | For the RAN1 study of “Re-use of existing 5G mid-band (~3.5GHz) site grid for 6G deployments in at least around 7 GHz and targeting comparable coverage to 5G mid-band”, if link budget template candidates 1 is used to calculate the metric(s), the coverage gap between ~7GHz and 5G mid-band is calculated as follows<br>Coverage gap = MPL1 – MPL2 – PL_diff [– additional margin]<br>  - [FFS: detailed value of additional margin]<br>Note:<br>- MPL1: MPL of the evaluated signal/channel in ~7GHz<br>- MPL2: MPL of the bottleneck channel in 5G mid-band (e.g., Rel-15 NR Msg3 for initial/random access procedure)<br>- PL_diff = 20*log10(f1 / f2), where f1 and f2 are the carrier frequency for ~7GHz and 5G mid-band, respectively | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.0 | Agreement | RAN1 to discuss beamforming gain for DL broadcast channel and UL channel and to align the interpretation of the following parameters in link budget template candidate 1<br>- (4b) Antenna gain correction factor at antenna gain component 3 & antenna gain component 4 of transmitter (dB)<br>- (5b) Antenna gain correction factor at antenna gain component 2 of transmitter (dB)<br>- (11b) Antenna gain correction factor at antenna gain component 3 & antenna gain component 4 of receiver (dB)<br>- (11bis-b) Antenna gain correction factor at antenna gain component 2 of receiver (dB)<br>- (15) Receiver interference density<br>- (27) Penetration margin (dB): Whether account for the indoor distance in the pathloss calculation in O2I scenarios<br>- (25) Shadow fading margin (function of the cell area reliability and lognormal shadow fading std deviation) (dB): Function definition and whether handoff gain is accounted | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.0 | Agreement | For the RAN1 study of “Re-use of existing 5G mid-band (~3.5GHz) site grid for 6G deployments in at least around 7 GHz and targeting comparable coverage to 5G mid-band”, companies are encouraged to at least provide the link budget results for the following signals/channels during initial access in around 7GHz:<br>- DL:NR PSS/SSS, SSB, Common PDCCH, SIB1 PDSCH, Msg2/4 PDSCH<br>- UL:NR PRACH format C2/B4/0, MSG3 PUSCH, MSG5 PUSCH (companies to report assumption of the payload of actual transmission),  MSG4 PUCCH Format 1<br>- The simulation assumptions in section A.1 in TR38.830 are used as a starting point.<br>  - The payload for NR SIB1 PDSCH is 1200 bits.<br>  - FFS: other parameters | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.0 | Agreement | From frequency domain resource mapping perspective, study TB/CB/CBG mapping within a PDSCH with frequency domain resource allocation between 200MHz and 400MHz considering the following options:<br>- Option A: A TB can span up to 400MHz<br>- Option B: A TB can span up to 200MHz<br>  - FFS details<br>Note: For both options, whether multiple TBs are time domain multiplexed within a PDSCH due to other restrictions will be separately discussed in agenda item 10.5.2.2. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.0 | Agreement | For the study of link direction determination for dynamic TDD, consider the following options:<br>- Option 1:<br>  - TDD pattern is based on DL, UL and flexible symbols<br>  - TDD pattern is semi-statically configured<br>  - No dynamic slot format indication as in NR<br>  - For dynamically scheduled single transmission/reception in flexible symbols, UE link direction is determined only based on scheduling DCI<br>  - Study transmissions/receptions without associated DCI in flexible symbols (if configured)<br>- Option 2:<br>  - Semi-statically configured TDD pattern is based on DL, UL and flexible symbols<br>  - Dynamically indicated TDD pattern is based on DL, UL and guard symbols<br>    - Dynamically indicated TDD pattern indicates DL, UL or guard symbols for flexible symbols according to semi-statically configured TDD pattern<br>    - The TDD pattern should be provided to the UE with sufficient processing time to apply the TDD pattern change<br>  - UE link direction is determined based on TDD pattern<br>- Option 3:<br>  - TDD pattern, based on DL, UL and guard symbols (i.e. no flexible symbols)<br>  - UE is dynamically indicated with a TDD pattern out of predefined/preconfigured TDD pattern<br>    - The TDD pattern should be provided to the UE with sufficient processing time to apply the TDD pattern change<br>- Other options are not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.0 | Agreement | For the 6GR smallest maximum UE bandwidth for at least one lower-tier device, RAN1 agrees the following observations for the alternatives agreed in RAN1#124:<br>- According to NR Rel-18 eRedCap study for a device supporting a single FDD band with spectrum allocation no smaller than 20MHz, when comparing on the basis of same PHY peak rate (~10 Mbps), the additional UE modem complexity reduction of Alt 2 compared with Alt 1 is ~10%, and the additional UE modem complexity reduction of Alt 3 compared with Alt 1 is ~5%.<br>HD-FDD 1-Rx UE modem complexity reduction estimates from the eRedCap SI<br>\| Closest RAN1#124 alternative for smallest maximum 6G UE bandwidth \| Modem complexity reduction option in eRedCap SI TR 38.865 \| Modem complexity reduction relative to eRedCap Option PR1 (Alt 1) \|<br>\| Alt 1 \| Option PR1 \| 0% \|<br>\| Alt 2 \| Option BW1 \| 10% \|<br>\| Alt 3 with 5 MHz BB for all channels \| Option BW2 \| 7% \|<br>\| Alt 3 with 5 MHz BB for PxSCH only \| Option BW3 \| 4% \|<br>FD-FDD 1-Rx UE modem complexity reduction estimates from the eRedCap SI<br>\| Closest RAN1#124 alternative for smallest maximum 6G UE bandwidth \| Modem complexity reduction option in eRedCap SI TR 38.865 \| Modem complexity reduction relative to eRedCap Option PR1 (Alt 1) \|<br>\| Alt 1 \| Option PR1 \| 0% \|<br>\| Alt 2 \| Option BW1 \| 8% \|<br>\| Alt 3 with 5 MHz BB for all channels \| Option BW2 \| 5% \|<br>\| Alt 3 with 5 MHz BB for PxSCH only \| Option BW3 \| 4% \|<br>- Placeholder for companies to provide more UE complexity analysis based on inputs to RAN1#125, e.g., multi-band devices | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.0 | Conclusion | For the RAN1 study of “Re-use of existing 5G mid-band (~3.5GHz) site grid for 6G deployments in at least around 7 GHz and targeting comparable coverage to 5G mid-band”, to align the usage of the correction factors of BS antenna gain of common beams in co-site deployment evaluation, the following are observed as the common understanding<br>- the total antenna gain of common beam is assumed to be unchanged/comparable if the beamwidth of SSB beams is unchanged, regardless of whether the number of BS antenna elements or BS TxRUs increases or not.<br>  - The total BS antenna gain above refers to the sum value of entry (4)+(5)+(11bis) in the link budget table.<br>- The total antenna gain of BS UL common beam is expected not to be smaller than that of BS DL common beam if the beamwidth of SSB beams is unchanged.<br>Note:<br>- (4) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 3 & antenna gain component 4 of transmitter = (4a) – (4b) (dB)<br>- (5) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 2 of transmitter = (5a) - (5b) (dB) Note: zero for uplink<br>- (11bis) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 2 of receiver = (11bis-a) - (11bis-b) (dB) Note: zero for downlink | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | For discussion purpose, the following terminologies are defined<br>- 6GR SSB burst: one 6GR SSB or multiple 6GR SSBs each with a different index compose a 6GR SSB burst<br>- 6GR SSB burst set: one or multiple 6GR SSB burst(s) compose a 6GR SSB burst set<br>  - Note: Whether and how to use the ’6GR SSB burst set’ is up to companies to report.<br>- 6GR SSB periodicity: The time interval at which an SSB within a given SSB burst repeats in time<br>Note: “index” is only for illustration purpose to identify a 6GR SSB within a 6GR SSB burst, whether and how to specify the terminologies will be further discussed.<br>Note: all below examples are only used for improving understanding about above terminologies<br>Example 1: 4 SSBs within a SSB burst (no repetition), 1 SSB burst within 1 SSB burst set<br>Example 2: 4 SSBs within a SSB burst (4 consecutive repetitions), 1 SSB burst within 1 SSB burst set<br>Example 3-1: 16 SSBs within a SSB burst (4 consecutive repetitions), 1 SSB burst within 1 SSB burst set<br>Example 3-2: 16 SSBs within a SSB burst (4 consecutive repetitions), 4 SSB bursts within 1 SSB burst set<br>Example 4-1: 16 SSBs within a SSB burst (4 interleaved repetitions), 1 SSB burst within 1 SSB burst set<br>Example 4-2: 4 SSBs within a SSB burst (4 interleaved repetitions), 4 SSB bursts within 1 SSB burst set | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx) |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.1.2 | Agreement | Adopt the following definition for miss detection probability and false alarm probability for RAN1 study (not intended for 6GR requirements)<br>- Note: Definition of evaluation metrics are for the purpose of alignment of definition among companies. Companies are encouraged to provide information on evaluation metrics reported and the implication and importance of the evaluation metric in the contributions with evaluations.<br>- Miss detection probability<br>  - Total probability of following events (for a preamble transmission):<br>    - Not detecting the preamble for a RO that was sent among the target preambles of the detecting BS<br>    - Correct preamble detection but with the wrong timing estimation<br>      - For correct preamble detection, the (residual) timing estimation error should be less than CP/2 of data symbol, e.g., SCS = 30kHz, CP/2 = 1.2 us<br>- False alarm probability<br>  - Probability of detecting any target preamble for a RO when no transmission has occurred (only noise)<br>Adopt the following definition for metrics for evaluations for RAN1 study (not intended for 6GR requirements)<br>- Note: Not all metrics may be applicable for all evaluation scenarios, and it is up to companies to provide appropriate metric(s) for information. The definitions are for the purpose of alignment of definition among companies. Companies are encouraged to provide information on evaluation metrics reported and the implication and importance of the evaluation metric in the contributions with evaluations.<br>- Miss detection probability considering frequency estimation<br>  - Total probability of following events (for a preamble transmission):<br>    - Not detecting the preamble for a RO that was sent among the target preambles of the detecting BS<br>    - Correct preamble detection but with the wrong timing estimation and/or frequency estimation<br>      - For correct preamble detection,<br>        - the (residual) timing estimation error should be less than CP/2 of data symbol, e.g., SCS = 30kHz, CP/2 = 1.2 us, and<br>        - the (residual) frequency estimation error of PRACH detection should be less than some target value. Target value to be reported by company.<br>- Type 1 false detection probability (intra-cell)<br>  - Probability of detecting different preamble(s) for a RO than the one that was sent among the target preambles of the detecting BS.<br>- Type 1 false detection rate (intra-cell)<br>  - A = number of preamble(s) for a RO detected other than the one that was sent among the target preambles of the detecting BS<br>  - B = Sum of A for all ROs that contain preamble transmission<br>  - C = Total number of preamble transmission across all ROs<br>  - Type 1 false detection rate = B divided by C<br>- Type 2 false detection probability (inter-cell)<br>  - Probability of detecting any target preamble for a RO when no transmission has occurred in the cell of detecting BS and transmission has occurred in another cell<br>- Mixed false detection probability<br>  - Probability of detecting multiple preambles (two or more) of which one of the detected preamble is correctly detected (multiple preamble detection for the detecting BS) for a RO when only one preamble among the target preambles of the detection BS was sent. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.1.2 | Agreement | - Study base sequences and preamble sequence generation/construction methods for 6GR PRACH taking into account one or more of the following aspects:<br>  - detection performance (possibly including TA estimation performance, handling of frequency offset, miss-detection, false alarm, false detection, auto-correlation/cross correlation properties, etc)<br>  - detection complexity for BS<br>  - implementation complexity for UE<br>  - Cubic metric/PAPR<br>  - supported sequence lengths & associated SCS of a sequence<br>  - [Preambles per random access occasion (RO) ]<br>  - Inter-cell interference<br>  - Coexistence with 5G NR PRACH sequences<br>  - Note 1: Benchmark various performance metric(s) and considerations against 5G NR PRACH design as baseline<br>  - Note 2: base sequences for 5G NR PRACH are ZC sequence, and preamble sequence generation is done via (time domain) cyclic shift of a ZC root sequence (base sequence) to create a specific preamble sequence.<br>  - Note 3: companies are encouraged to provide the following information:<br>    - detailed information for the sequence generation and construction of a preamble for PRACH<br>    - correlation properties (compared to NR PRACH sequences, including correlation with NR PRACH sequences)<br>    - Cubic metric/PAPR (at least compared to NR PRACH sequences)<br>    - Applicable scenarios | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |

Ideal can be considered for calibration, if needed. \|<br>\| Target BLER \| [10%, 1%] for 1st transmission of MsgA as starting points. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.1.2 | Agreement | - Study and Identify at least the following set of deployment scenario characteristics for 6GR RACH preamble format design:<br>  - target round trip time (RTT)/maximum cell radius<br>  - target time duration of preamble<br>  - target doppler/UE mobility<br>  - target duplex scheme, PRACH SCS, and frequency ranges<br>- Study and identify required preamble formats that correspond to the identified use cases. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.1.3 | Agreement | Study for idle mode,<br>- Whether and how to define a common resource block grid in single-carrier operation | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.1.3 | Agreement | For discussions on frequency location and bandwidth for UEs during initial access and idle mode, "frequency location" is defined as the starting position of the bandwidth | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | - 6GR Control Resource Set (CORESET) to be defined for 6GR PDCCH<br>    - CORESET defines at least duration in time and frequency resources used for PDCCH<br>- 6GR Search Space (SS) set to be defined for 6GR PDCCH.<br>    - A 6GR search space set defines at least a set of PDCCH candidates for a UE to monitor | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | - REG consists of multiple REs.<br>    - REG occupies one OFDM symbol in the time domain.<br>    - FFS for the REG allocation in frequency domain.<br>- CCE consists of a set of REGs.<br>    - FFS how to map a set of REGs to the CCE including the number of REGs per CCE.<br>    - FFS REG indexing<br>- PDCCH candidate corresponds to one or multiple CCE(s).<br>    - The exact number of CCEs used for a single PDCCH candidate equals the Aggregation Level (AL) at least for PDCCH without repetition.<br>Working Assumption from RAN1#124 is not necessarily to be confirmed. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.1 | Working Assumption | - REG bundle consists of a set of REGs. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | Study both non-interleaved and interleaved CCE-to-REG mapping for 6GR PDCCH. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | At least QPSK modulation is used for 6GR PDCCH.<br>- FFS necessity and feasibility of additional modulation(s) other than QPSK to be used for 6GR PDCCH | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | - Study CCE aggregation mechanism for 6GR PDCCH<br>  - Consider ALs 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 as baseline<br>  - Study the necessity, feasibility, and use-cases for supporting of higher and other ALs, e.g., 32.<br>  - It is necessary to consider at least the number of REGs within one CCE, and the number of REs within one REG.<br>- Study the necessity and use-cases of PDCCH repetition for 6GR PDCCH, considering at least the following:<br>  - Intra-slot repetitions<br>  - Inter-slot repetitions<br>- Study the duration of CORESET in OFDM symbols<br>  - Consider 1, 2, 3 symbols as baseline<br>  - Study the necessity, feasibility and use-cases for supporting the CORESET durations longer than 3.<br>- Study the trade-offs/relation among PDCCH repetition, CORESET duration, and the usage of Aggregation Levels. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | - Study details of frequency-domain resource allocation for both contiguous and non-contiguous frequency-domain resource allocation for 6GR CORESET | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | - Study CRC scrambling, e.g., by an RNTI, for 6GR PDCCH.<br>- Study PDCCH payload bit-level scrambling before modulation for 6GR PDCCH. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | - Common Search Space (CSS) set and UE-Specific Search Space (USS) set to be defined for 6GR PDCCH.<br>- A SS set is associated with at least one CORESET | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.1 | Conclusion | - NR and 6GR CORESETs are RAT-specific and configured independently.<br>  - A 6GR UE operating in 6G network is only configured with 6GR CORESET(s). | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | - Study the necessity, feasibility, and ways of dynamic sharing and/or semi-static assignment of time-frequency resources of 5G and 6G PDCCH transmission for MRSS, ensuring 6GR performance<br>  - The above is considered from the NW perspective in a way transparent to the UE. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | - Hash function to be defined for 6GR PDCCH.<br>  - Hash function can be used to determine the CCE index of a 6GR PDCCH candidate associated with a SS set.<br>  - FFS hash function design for 6GR PDCCH. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | - For 6GR PDCCH DMRS, study at least<br>  - How to define DMRS resource mapping.<br>  - How to define DMRS sequence generation and initialization. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | - Support at least slot-level PDCCH monitoring with minimum periodicity of 1 slot for 6GR.<br>  - The details of the slot-level PDCCH monitoring are FFS<br>- Study whether and how to support PDCCH monitoring other than slot-level | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | - For the study of 6GR PDCCH transmit diversity consider at least the following:<br>  - Single-port DMRS-based PDCCH transmission as a baseline<br>  - The necessity and feasibility of the UE non-transparent transmission scheme compared to the transparent<br>  - Precoder granularity, e.g., REG bundle. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - For FDRA for PDSCH on a single physical carrier:<br>  - Support both bitmap-based (“Type 0”) and RIV-based (“Type 1”) mapping<br>  - Support dynamic switching between bitmap-based mapping and RIV-based mapping<br>  - Further study, at least, the following aspects:<br>    - Granularity for bitmap-based and RIV-based mapping, considering support for wider bandwidth (more than 275 PRBs)<br>    - Whether there are restrictions for 400 MHz CBW in case of two RF chains at the UE<br>    - Note: Decision to study scheduling across multiple physical carriers is subject to agreements in other agenda items | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - Support PT-RS for PDSCH at least for FR2<br>  - Study whether PT-RS is necessary for FR1 and around 7GHz<br>  - If PT-RS for FR1/around 7GHz is supported, strive to have a unified design for all bands | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - The phase noise model defined in TR38.803 is used for further evaluation unless further update from RAN4<br>  - Send an LS to RAN4 to check if RAN4 has plan to update it and when it will be updated | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - Study the DMRS assumption for MU-MIMO considering at least the following aspects<br>  - Network indication or UE assumption of DMRS CDM group/ports/density/pattern/symbol(s) for co-scheduled UE(s)<br>  - Network indication of number of CDM groups without data as in NR<br>  - Network indication to assist the UE’s OCC despreading<br>  - Network indication or UE assumption of the DMRS sequence for the co-scheduled UE(s)<br>  - Network indication or UE assumption of the modulation order(s) for the co-scheduled UE(s)<br>  - Network indication or UE assumption of the QCL/TCI information for the co-scheduled UE(s)<br>  - Network indication or UE assumption of PRG allocation for the co-scheduled UE(s) | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - For CPE configuration, details follow corresponding agreements in Agenda 10.1 and 10.5.2.3 | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - For traffic model for SLS EVM, support:<br>- NFB FTP model 1/3 with,<br>  - 500 kB packet size<br>  - 20%, 50%, and 70% resource utilization<br>  - FTP 3-extension 1 model can be optionally considered<br>  - 10 or 30 UEs per cell for applicable models<br>- FB model can be optionally considered, for technical performance requirements (TPR)<br>- Other traffic models and packet sizes are not precluded (companies to report) | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - Study PDSCH and RS for PDSCH based on the following additional SLS EVM assumptions for HST scenario<br>  - Note: Other parameters follow agreements on SLS EVM for non-HST scenario<br>  - Note: LLS EVM parameters for HST scenario are separately discussed<br>\| Parameter \| Value \|<br>\| Carrier frequency and duplex \| Around 4 GHz, TDD Around 7 GHz, TDD Around 30 GHz, TDD \|<br>\| Channel model \| TR 38.901 with spatial consistency (optional) \|<br>\| Scenario and deployment at around 4 and 7 GHz \| Rural macro, 500 m or 1732 m ISD between RRH sites, two TRxPs per RRH site, one or more RRH sites connected to a BBU, with RRH site to railway track distance: 100 m Inter-track distance: 6 m Train length: 400 m Train width: 3.38 m \|<br>\| Scenario and deployment at around 30 GHz \| Rural macro, 500, 1732 m between BBU sites, one or more RRH sites connected to 1 BBU, one TRxP per RRH site, with RRH site to railway track distance: 5 m Inter-RRH site distance: [580 m, 580 m, 572 m] Inter-track distance: 6 m Train length: 400 m Train width: 3.38 m \|<br>\| UE speed \| Rural macro: outdoor (350km/h, 500km/h) \|<br>\| Number of UEs per cell and UE distribution \| 300 (assuming 1000 passengers per train and at least 30% activity ratio) 100% of users in train Companies to report how UEs at distributed in train \|<br>\| Resource utilization \| 50%, 70%, 90% \|<br>\| Multi-TRP scenario \| Maximum number of coordinated TRPs is 2, 4, 8 or 16 Other number of coordinated TRPs are not precluded \|<br>\| CSI-RS periodicity \| 10 ms (optional) 20 ms (baseline) 40 ms (optional) 80 ms (optional) \|<br>\| BS transmit power (for 20 MHz) \| For around 0.7—7 GHz 49 dBm For around 30 GHz 33 dBm Note: BS Tx power scales up with bandwidth proportionally under the limitation of the maximum BS Tx power is 56 dBm Other BS transmit powers are not precluded (companies to report) \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - For TDRA for PDSCH:<br>  - Support a single mapping type, at least for PDSCH within a single slot<br>    - Support flexible starting symbol<br>      - Study which symbol(s) can be the starting symbol<br>    - Study number of symbols<br>      - Granularity is 1 symbol<br>    - Further study DMRS position(s)<br>    - Study time offset from the scheduling PDCCH, considering UE processing capabilities<br>  - Study whether/how to support a single PDSCH transmission crossing slot boundary<br>    - Including the maximum PDSCH duration<br>    - Including whether the single PDSCH transmission can occupy contiguous or non-contiguous symbols<br>    - Including impact on performance and complexity<br>    - Including maximum TB size limit<br>    - Note: If cross-slot boundary PDSCH transmission is supported, a single mapping type is supported also for cross-slot PDSCH transmission<br>  - Study whether/how to support PDSCH repetition<br>- Note: Whether/how to schedule multiple PDSCH transmissions using a single DCI is up to DCI content discussion | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - For channel parameter estimation for LLS EVM:<br>  - Practical channel parameter estimation (baseline)<br>  - Companies to report channel parameter estimation, e.g., delay spread, Doppler spread, delay, SNR, etc. (optional) | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |

| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - Study the sequence of DMRS for PDSCH considering at least the following aspects<br>  - PAPR<br>  - Inter-sequence interference<br>  - Scrambling ID indication | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - For low overhead DMRS with AI/ML receiver for PDSCH reception, link level simulation is used for further study<br>- The performance comparison is based on the following metrics<br>  - Eventual KPI: BLER/SE/throughput<br>  - Complexity KPI: FLOPs, the number of parameters | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | For evaluation of DMRS for PDSCH, the following aspects are considered for further study:<br>- Candidates for FD-OCC length: 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, x<br>- Candidates for TD-OCC length: 1 (i.e., single-symbol DMRS), 2, 4, x<br>- Candidate for number of CDM groups: 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, x<br>- CDM group structure, e.g., comb-based, block-based, etc. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |

| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - Study codeword-to-layer mapping for PDSCH:<br>  - Consider the following aspects<br>    - The number of codewords for each rank<br>    - The maximum number of codewords<br>    - The maximum rank per codeword<br>    - The modulation order per layer(s)<br>  - Including identifying and evaluating important aspects affecting performance and complexity that are impacted by codeword-to-layer mapping<br>  - NR spatial first, frequency second, time third mapping as baseline for evaluation<br>  - NR codeword-to-layer mapping as baseline for evaluation<br>- Further study whether/how to map one or more transport block(s) to time/frequency resources and its impact on codeword-to-layer mapping<br>- Further study aspects related to modulation order and code rate and its impact on codeword-to-layer mapping | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | For 6GR PUSCH transmission,<br>- The supported number of UE antenna ports are {1, 2, 3, 4, 8}<br>  - FFS: Other number of UE antenna ports<br>- The maximum number of SU-MIMO layers is assumed same as the given number of UE antenna ports for CP-OFDM<br>- Separate discussion on the number of antenna ports of UE device(s) | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |

company) \|<br>\| #5-1 MU-MIMO interference model \| FFS: How to model MU-MIMO channel (e.g., can emulate low correlation among MU scheduled UEs) FFS: same RB allocation among MU scheduled UEs \|<br>\| #6 Delay spread \| 30 ns 100 ns 300 ns 1000 ns (optional) \|<br>\| #7 UE speed \| 3 km/h, 30 km/h, 120 km/h, 350 km/h, 500 km/h Additional assumption of 0.3 km/h for FWA \|<br>\| #8 UE or fixed MCS \|<br>\| #12-1 HARQ \| No HARQ as baseline HARQ retransmission as optional \|<br>\| #13 Channel estimation \| Realistic, Ideal (as the upper bound, optional) Note: This also includes | RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | For time domain resource allocation for a PUSCH within a slot, consider at least the following aspects:<br>- Support flexible starting symbol<br>  - Study which symbol(s) can be the starting symbol<br>- Study number of symbols<br>  - Granularity is 1 symbol<br>- Study time offset from the scheduling PDCCH, considering UE processing capabilities.<br>Note: Time domain DMRS position is separately discussed | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | In addition to the agreement on the basic assumption of SLS for evaluation of PUSCH transmission scheme in RAN1#124, for traffic model, support the followings<br>- FTP Model 1/3, 0.5 Mbyte packet size<br>- FTP 3-extension 1 (optional)<br>- Resource Utilization (RU)<br>  - 20%<br>  - 50%<br>  - 70%<br>- Full buffer traffic model (optional, for 6G TPR evaluation)<br>- 10 or 30 UEs per cell, other values are not precluded (companies to report)<br>- Other traffic models and packet sizes are not precluded (companies to report)<br>- Note: This is also applicable to assumption of SLS for evaluation of PUSCH DMRS | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | In addition to the agreement on the basic assumption of SLS for evaluation of PUSCH transmission scheme in RAN1#124, for SLS assumption for PUSCH DMRS, support the followings<br>- Deployment: other deployment is not precluded, and can be reported by company.<br>- Link adaptation & HARQ retransmission are modelled<br>  - Number of HARQ retransmissions is reported by company<br>  - Note: this is also applicable to assumption of SLS for evaluation of PUSCH transmission scheme.<br>- DMRS specific assumptions: reuse the assumption of LLS for evaluation of PUSCH and DMRS<br>  - Reported by company, if additionally needed<br>- This is also applicable with HST scenario. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | For PUSCH transmission scheme and PUSCH DMRS in HST scenario, in addition to the agreement on the basic assumption of SLS for evaluation of PUSCH transmission scheme in RAN1#124, support the followings.<br>\| #1 Frequency range \| Around 4 GHz, TDD Around 7 GHz, TDD Around 30 GHz, TDD \|<br>\| #3 Numerology \| 30 kHz for TDD and around 4—7 GHz 120 kHz for TDD and around 30 GHz \|<br>\| #4 Scenario \| Around 4 GHz or Around 7 GHz: ISD 500 m or 1732 m between RRH sites, two TRxPs per RRH site, one or more RRH sites connected to a BBU. Around 30GHz: 1732 m between BBU sites, 3 RRH sites connected to 1 BBU, one TRxP per RRH site, inter RRH site distance (580 m, 580 m, 572 m) as in Figure 2 below. \|<br>\| #4-1 Deployment \| Macro-layer only: Around 4 GHz or 7 GHz: Dedicated linear deployment along the railway line as in Figure 1 below. - RRH site to railway track distance: 100 m Figure 1 Around 30 GHz: Dedicated linear deployment along the railway line as in Figure 2. - RRH site to railway track distance: 5 m. Figure 2 \|<br>\| #5 Channel model \| TR 38.901 with spatial consistency (optional) \|<br>\| #13 UE speed \| 350km/h, 500km/h \|<br>\| UE distributions \| - Train length: 400m - 300 (for non-full-buffer) (assuming 1000 passengers per high-speed train and at least 30% activity ratio) - 100% of users in train \|<br>\| Multi-TRP scenario \| Maximum number of coordinated TRPs is 2, 4, 8 or 16 Other number of coordinated TRPs are not precluded \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | Support codebook based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, and study at least the following aspects:<br>- UL precoder is selected from an UL codebook, based on UL CSI acquisition in NW to schedule UE<br>  - As baseline, SRS is used for UL CSI acquisition<br>  - FFS: Which RS/method can be used to obtain UL CSI, other than SRS<br>- UL codebook design, at least considering<br>  - Fixed codebook which is pre-defined in specification<br>  - Configurable codebook which can be configured by NW<br>  - Hybrid approach of fixed and configurable codebook<br>  - performance, resolution, overhead, codebook size comparing to 5G NR<br>  - Diverse UE coherency capabilities<br>  - Diverse UE Tx antenna structures | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | For the assumption of LLS for evaluation of AI based receiver for PUSCH DMRS overhead reduction,<br>- Support to reuse the assumption of LLS for evaluation of PUSCH and DMRS in RAN1#124b.<br>- In addition, in RAN1#125, the following template is used as starting point for collecting related parameter values.<br>- Further discuss on detail of values<br>\| Parameters \| Value \|<br>\| #1 Receiver assumptions \|  \|<br>\| #2 Model input \| (Including model input dimension) \|<br>\| #3 Model output \| (Including model output dimension) \|<br>\| #4 Label \| (Labelling error model can be reported optionally) \|<br>\| #5 Training type \|  \|<br>\| #6 Number of MIMO layers \|  \|<br>\| #7 Number of FLOPs \| (Number of FLOPs per RB/slot/layer can be reported optionally) \|<br>\| #8 Number of parameters of AI Model \|  \|<br>\| #9 Benchmark \| Receiver: non-AI based receiver with realistic assumption Other aspects (including DMRS patterns, etc) can be reported by company \|<br>\| #10 Aspects to consider for generalization and scalability performance \|  \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | In addition to the agreement on the basic assumption of SLS for evaluation of PUSCH transmission scheme in RAN1#124, for CPE configuration, support the followings<br>- Indoor and outdoor CPE pre-selection criterion or mechanism<br>  - Approach 0. Random selection between indoor/outdoor CPE, as baseline<br>  - (optional) Approach 1. A pre-selection criterion is applied to ensure that the CPE/FWA devices experience sufficiently good link quality and the fraction of the cell over which service is sufficiently good is reported.<br>  - (optional) Approach 2. The approach first assumes CPEs are indoors. Then according to the criteria, a number of worst performing households are either rejected or reassigned as outdoor CPEs. The number of outdoor CPEs is limited to some fraction (20% in the example of Profile 1), and the households with the worst performance that exceed the number of allowed outdoor CPEs are rejected.<br>    - In cases where CPEs are 100% indoor or outdoor, prequalification can be used, and a fraction of worst performing households are rejected.<br>    - The criteria can be e.g RSRP, minimum UL or DL data rate, etc.<br>  - Other approaches are not precluded and can be considered (reported by company if considered)<br>  - Note 1: This is only for Profile 1 (mixed deployment of indoor and outdoor CPE).<br>  - Note 2: This is also applicable for assumption of SLS for evaluation of PUSCH DMRS | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | In addition to the agreement on the basic assumption of SLS for evaluation of PUSCH transmission scheme in RAN1#124, for CPE antenna configuration, support the followings<br>- Details follow corresponding agreements in Agenda 10.1.<br>- Other antenna location/configuration are not precluded and can be considered (reported by company if considered), e.g.,<br>  - For Alt 1 in agreement of AI 10.1, Outdoor CPEs use horizontally directive antenna elements.<br>  - For Alt 1 and Alt 2 in agreement of AI 10.1, Single panel indoor and outdoor CPEs with horizontally directive antenna elements are oriented to point at the serving base station. Indoor CPEs whose antenna elements are omnidirectional in the horizontal plane are not constrained to be oriented to point at the serving base station.<br>- Note: This is also applicable for assumption of SLS for evaluation of PUSCH DMRS. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.4.1 | Agreement | For the study of the adaptation of PDCCH monitoring in 6GR in connected mode, study at least the time domain adaptation of PDCCH monitoring and the corresponding signalling mechanisms, considering at least techniques in NR as starting point | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603347](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603347.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
