| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.4.1 | Agreement | RAN1 to study how to determine various DCI sizes that the UE monitors. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603348](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603348.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.4.1 | Agreement | RAN1 to study for a given DCI, the methods to determine the functions carried by the DCI<br>  - e.g. single or multiple Header/Identifier field(s), RNTI, DCI size,… | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603348](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603348.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.4.1 | Agreement | For 6GR, a single PDCCH carrying the entire DCI information is the baseline at least for DCI transmitted for UEs in idle mode. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603348](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603348.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.4.1 | Agreement | For the study of two-stage DCI in 6GR, consider at least the following aspects:<br>- The targeted scenarios and use cases<br>- Functionalities of the two stages and the information carried in each stage<br>- Physical [channel/signal] for each stage<br>- Timing relationships between first-stage [DCI], second-stage [DCI] and scheduled PDSCH(s)/PUSCH(s)<br>- Monitoring and blind decoding assumptions for each stage<br>- Network/UE complexity<br>- Impact on coverage<br>- Impact on reliability<br>- Impact on blocking probability<br>- Resource efficiency/overhead<br>- Impact on UE/network power consumption<br>- Impact on DCI decoding latency, and data processing timeline<br>- Impact on scheduling delay and user experience<br>- Pros and cons compared to single-stage DCI<br>Note: companies are encouraged to provide their assumptions/designs used for studying above aspects | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603348](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603348.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.4.3 | Agreement | In 6GR, study whether and how to support downlink HARQ-ACK feedback granularity smaller than TB-level, considering at least the following factors.<br>- Feedback overhead<br>- Retransmission efficiency<br>- Implementation feasibility | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603251](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603251.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.4.3 | Agreement | In 6GR, study whether and how to support HARQ-ACK bundling across multiple TBs | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603251](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603251.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.4.3 | Agreement | Study HARQ-ACK codebook(s) and its operation for downlink HARQ operation in 6GR, considering at least the following aspects:<br>- HARQ-ACK codebook generation<br>- HARQ-ACK feedback granularity, e.g., TB-level, smaller than TB-level, bundling across multiple TBs, etc.<br>- Feedback overhead<br>- HARQ-ACK payload size and bits order misalignment between NW and UE due to DCI missing<br>- Ambiguity between DTX of DL assignment and PDSCH decoding failure when network decodes a NACK in a HARQ-ACK codebook<br>- Coordination between schedulers, e.g., between multiple carriers, between DL and UL, etc.<br>- Feedback complexity | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603253](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603253.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.4.3 | Agreement | In 6GR, the minimum downlink HARQ-ACK payload size is 1 and the upper limit supported by 5G NR Polar coding can be reused (i.e., 1706 bits) at least for Mechanism 1 of HARQ feedback if supported.<br>- Note1: this upper limit does not imply the max HARQ-ACK payload size would be 1706.<br>- Note2: the exact value of the maximum size may be determined at a later stage if needed. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603253](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603253.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.4.3 | Agreement | In 6GR, study the details and pros and cons of the following methods to determine timing and resource for downlink HARQ-ACK feedback.<br>  - Method 1: Dynamic indication in DCI<br>  - Method 2: UE initiated HARQ-ACK feedback<br>  - Method 3: Next available/feasible time slot of semi-static configured resources that satisfy UE processing time/capability<br>  - Method 4: Only based on configuration via RRC<br>  - Note: combinations of above methods and other methods are not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603253](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603253.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.4.3 | Agreement | For 6GR UL: Study an explicit indication or implicit indication for a given HARQ process failure to trigger faster ARQ procedure in UL.<br>- Note: Companies are encouraged to check RAN2 agreements and LS if any on fast ARQ in RAN2#133bis | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603326](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603326.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.5 | Agreement | Study the following mechanisms with NR 1-bit SR mechanism as the benchmark for study:<br>- Study the mechanism of transmitting multiple (>1) bits in SR.<br>- Study enhancements of resource allocation and L1-aspect of triggering for SR for 1-bit.<br>  - Note: This also applies to multiple bit SR study.<br>- Study the L1-aspects of BS UL pre-scheduling mechanism.<br>- Study the L1-aspects of contention-based BSR transmission.<br>- Study the L1-aspects of BSR transmission in preconfigured PUSCH (contention free). | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.5 | Agreement | Study UE-to-UE CLI handling mechanisms for 6GR:<br>- The studies of UE-to-UE CLI focus on deployment scenarios of dynamic TDD, semi-static SBFD, TDD- semi-static SBFD coexistence, and consider inter-subband, intra-subband and intra-band CLI, both intra-cell CLI and inter-cell CLI.<br>- Study the following UE-to-UE CLI measurement and reporting mechanism, considering at least the following aspects:<br>  - Reference signals or/and channels for measuring CLI<br>  - Timing misalignment for measurement<br>  - Measurement metric<br>  - CLI reporting contents.<br>  - CLI reporting mechanisms.<br>  - Network-controlled reporting.<br>  - UE-initiated/Event-triggered CLI reporting.<br>- Study the following UE-to-UE mitigation/suppression mechanisms:<br>  - Coordination of time/frequency resource allocation.<br>  - Uplink power control to reduce the CLI.<br>  - Spatial domain methods to suppress CLI.<br>  - Information exchange between BSs for example the configuration of CLI resources, TDD/SBFD configuration. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.5 | Agreement | Study BS-to-BS CLI handling mechanisms for 6GR:<br>- The study of BS-to-BS CLI focus on deployment scenarios of dynamic TDD, semi-static SBFD, TDD-semi-static SBFD coexistence. The BS-to-BS CLI can be inter-subband interference, intra-subband interference and co-channel intra-band interference, inter-cell interference.<br>- Study the following BS-to-BS handling mechanisms:<br>  - Uplink resource muting mechanism to enable the BS to identify BS-to-BS CLI.<br>  - BS-to-BS CLI/channel measurement mechanisms/resources.<br>  - Coordinated scheduling<br>  - Information exchange between BSs including the configuration of measurement resource, TDD/SBFD configuration, DL beam information CLI measurement results and CLI mitigation request.<br>    - Note: this may or may not have RAN1 spec impact.<br>  - DL Power allocation-based mechanism.<br>  - Spatial domain solution, for example beam nulling, beam paring, restrict/preferred beam.<br>  - Note: For any of the above items, the study includes necessity. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.5.5 | Agreement | Study mechanisms for handling remote interference management (RIM) for 6GR:<br>- The study should consider<br>  - Coexist of BSs with same or different TDD configurations for 6GR BS or for 6GR and NR BSs in the same band.<br>  - Scenarios with at least one BS configured with SBFD<br>  - The remote interference between NR BS and 6GR BS<br>  - Note: The upper mid-band operation (around 7GHz) is also considered in the study.<br>- Study the potential workflow and framework for 6GR RIM<br>  - The wireless-based and backhaul-based RIM frameworks in NR are considered as the starting point for study.<br>  - Aim to minimize unnecessary complexity.<br>- Study the reference signal design for 6GR RIM, at least consider the following aspects:<br>  - Take NR RIM RS as starting point or study.<br>  - The functions of RIM RS, including how many types of RIM-RS, information indicated and carried in RIM RS, and physical layer design of RIM RS. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.6.1.1 | Agreement | For OFDM-based WUS sequence, further study time domain (e.g., DFT preprocessing) definition and frequency domain (w/o DFT preprocessing) definition with respect to the following aspects:<br>- Link-level performance<br>- BS implementation complexity<br>- UE detection complexity<br>  - Frequency domain and time domain receiver processing<br>- PAPR performance and relevancy<br>- Multiplexing and coexistence with other signals and channels | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.6.1.1 | Agreement | In FR2-1, the same SCS is assumed for DL WUS as for the 6GR Sync signals in the same band if sync signals and data channels use the same SCS. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.6.1.1 | Agreement | The baseline reference configuration for link level coverage assessment of EE processing for DL WUS detection includes the following parameters referred to NR common PDCCH<br>\| Aggregation level \| 8 \|<br>\| # Rx antennas (PDCCH) \| 4 \|<br>\| CORESET \| Refer to TR38.830 \|<br>\| PDCCH payload \| 40-bits (excluding CRC) \|<br>\| # Tx antennas (WUS+PDCCH) \| 2 \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.6.1.1 | Agreement | Study at least the 6GR sync signals for UE sync for DL WUS reception. Further study necessity (e.g., if 6GR sync signals are found to be insufficient for DL WUS) applicability and impact of additional synchronization method for DL WUS reception (e.g., additional sync signals or DL WUS with sync functionality). | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |

| RAN1#124bis | 10.6.1.2 | Agreement | For RRC connected state, study feasibility and necessity of serving cell RRM measurement by EE processing, considering at least:<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>- Coverage (e.g., achievable SINR/SNR) and accuracy<br>  - Measurement metrics<br>- Impact on the EE processing complexity<br>- Based on at least 6GR sync signal<br>  - other signals<br>- other measurement<br>- Whether there is spec impact or not | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.6.1.2 | Agreement | For RRC connected state, study feasibility and necessity of neighboring cell RRM measurement by EE processing, considering at least:<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>- Neighboring cell measurement type, e.g., intra-frequency, inter-frequency<br>- Neighboring cell identification, measurement and evaluation<br>- Neighboring cell number limitation if any<br>- Inter-cell interference<br>- Coverage (e.g., achievable SINR/SNR) and accuracy<br>  - Measurement metrics<br>- Based on at least 6GR sync signal<br>  - other signals<br>- Impact on the EE processing complexity<br>the power consumption of neighboring cell RRM measurement based on EE processing<br>- Other limitations if any<br>- Whether there is spec impact or not | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.6.1.2 | Agreement | Study 6GR OFDM sequence based DL WUS for waking up UE for at least paging purpose in RRC idle state, considering at least:<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>- Impact to latency, if applicable<br>- Network overhead, complexity, and energy consumption<br>- Distributed PO/PF and clustered PO/PF<br>- Association between WUS monitoring occasion and PO<br>- UE behaviors after waking-up<br>- WUS indication granularity and the number of information bits carried by DLWUS<br>- WUS monitoring periodicity<br>- WUS monitoring occasions<br>- Multi-beam operation including at least QCL assumption<br>- Consequence of false alarm/miss detection<br>- Other impacts, if any<br>- Other RRC state if supported<br>- Other functionalities of 6GR DL WUS<br>- Same or different carriers for DL WUS and paging | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.6.1.2 | Agreement | For the RAN1 study on DL WUS triggering PDCCH monitoring with and without C-DRX in RRC CONNECTED state, include:<br>- DL WUS is monitored periodically based on network configuration<br>- Once DL WUS is detected, UE starts PDCCH monitoring for a time duration, after a time offset<br>  - the time duration, time offset<br>  - whether WUS is monitored during the time duration<br>- How UE stops PDCCH monitoring<br>- How UE stops and starts WUS monitoring<br>- WUS triggering PDCCH monitoring is per cell or cell group<br>- One or more DL WUS configurations<br>- Other details if any<br>2603451<br>2602035<br>2602034 | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.6.2 | Agreement | - For study of feasibility for UL WUS, at least followings are observed for DS#1b.<br>  - For the case where prior to UL WUS, always-on 6GR synchronization signals with PBCH on the standalone cell are transmitted,<br>    - UE can acquire DL timing/frequency synchronization for UL WUS transmission at least via always-on 6GR synchronization signals and PBCH transmitted by the standalone cell.<br>  - For the case where prior to UL WUS, always-on 6GR synchronization signals without PBCH on the standalone cell are transmitted,<br>    - It is required to further study whether UE can acquire DL timing/frequency synchronization for UL WUS transmission at least via always-on 6GR synchronization signals transmitted by the standalone cell.<br>  - For both cases,<br>    - UE can determine UL WUS transmission power at least based on always-on 6GR synchronization signals transmitted by the standalone cell and information to determine UL WUS transmission power, if information is available<br>      - It is required to further study whether/how information to determine UL WUS transmission power can be delivered to UE by e.g., PBCH, predefined in spec, or other methods<br>    - It is required to further study the minimum information size/content necessary at least for UL WUS transmission/operation and how such information can be delivered to UE. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.6.2 | Agreement | - For study of feasibility for UL WUS, at least followings are observed for DS#2b.<br>  - If UE transmits UL WUS to cell/carrier #1,<br>    - UE can acquire DL timing/frequency synchronization for UL WUS transmission at least via always-on 6GR synchronization signals and PBCH transmitted by cell/carrier #1.<br>    - UE can determine UL WUS transmission power at least based on always-on 6GR synchronization signals transmitted by cell/carrier #1 and information to determine UL WUS transmission power, if information is possibly available at least from cell/carrier #1.<br>  - If UE transmits UL WUS to cell/carrier #2,<br>    - For the case where prior to UL WUS, always-on 6GR synchronization signals with PBCH on cell/carrier #2 are transmitted,<br>      - UE can acquire DL timing/frequency synchronization for UL WUS transmission at least via always-on 6GR synchronization signals and PBCH transmitted by cell/carrier #2, or the feasibility and applicability by cell/carrier #1<br>    - For the case where prior to UL WUS, always-on 6GR synchronization signals without PBCH on cell/carrier #2 are transmitted,<br>      - It is required to further study whether/how UE can acquire DL timing/frequency synchronization for UL WUS transmission at least via always-on 6GR synchronization signals transmitted by cell/carrier #2, or the feasibility and applicability by cell/carrier #1<br>    - UE can determine UL WUS transmission power at least based on always-on 6GR synchronization signals transmitted by cell/carrier #2 and information to determine UL WUS transmission power, if information is possibly available at least from cell/carrier #1. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.7.1 | Agreement | For the issue of HARQ stalling due to large RTT in NTN, study the following solutions (which may or may not have specification impact), including their applicability:<br>- Solution 1: HARQ feedback disabling.<br>- Solution 2: PDSCH / PUSCH transmissions that span multiple slots.<br>- Solution 3: aNB reusing the same HARQ process before receiving HARQ-ACK feedback for the previous transmission<br>- Solution 4: Sufficient number of HARQ processes<br>- Note: Combination of solutions above can be considered.<br>- FFS: Whether the above techniques can be harmonized with TN | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.7.1 | Agreement | For 6GR NTN, the link budget template attached to R1-2603091 is endorsed.<br>- Note: “Candidate 2” is not considered for NTN. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603091](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603091.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.7.1 | Conclusion | For the 6GR NTN link budget template, for the purpose of illustration the following figure and text describe how the assumed antenna virtualization mode can be used for different antenna types:<br>- For reflector antenna:<br>  - Antenna gain element (4c) captures the overall antenna gain<br>  - Scanning loss (4b-2) =0<br>  - Loss to beam edge (4b-1) can be set for the satellite and may depend on the elevation angle<br>  - (1) = (2) (same # of elements as simulated)<br>- For phased arrays:<br>  - Antenna gain element (4c) captures the per-element antenna gain<br>  - Scanning loss (4b-2) can be set according to the elevation angle<br>  - Loss to beam edge (4b-1) can be set for the satellite and may depend on the elevation angle<br>  - (2) is the number of simulated chains in LLS (either 1 or 2). (1) is the total number of antenna elements.<br>- For UT transmitters with omni antennas<br>  - Antenna gain element (4c) captures the overall antenna gain<br>  - Scanning loss (4b-2) =0<br>  - Loss to beam edge (4b-1) = 0<br>  - (1) = (2) (same # of elements as simulated)<br>NOTE: The above is for the “Transmitter” part of the 6GR NTN link budget template, the same antenna virtualization mode is used for the “Receiver” part of the 6GR NTN link budget template. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603091](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603091.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.7.1 | Agreement | In 6GR NTN, for the issue of satellite having more beam footprints than simultaneously active beams, study the following solutions, including their achievable coverage ratio of satellite and applicability:<br>- Value of default SSB periodicity<br>- Hierarchical beam structure (e.g. using beams of various sizes) | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603091](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603091.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.7.1 | Agreement | For 6GR NTN study, RAN1 considers at least the following duplex types:<br>- FD-FDD.<br>- HD-FDD on UE side.<br>\| Note the following is agreed in main session: On 6GR duplexing study, RAN1 considers at least following duplex types • FD-FDD • Semi-static TDD • gNB semi-static SBFD • HD-FDD on UE side • Dynamic TDD \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603091](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603091.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.7.1 | Agreement | In 6GR NTN, under the label of “physical layer aspects of multi-satellite operation”, at least the following aspects are studied, including its applicability:<br>- Physical layer aspects related to mobility<br>  - NOTE: Coordination with RAN2 is expected for this topic, and for some aspects RAN1 may need to wait for RAN2 to progress before discussing in RAN1.<br>  - NOTE: Companies can bring proposals on physical layer related issues related to mobility at different layers (e.g. L1/L2/L3) under this topic, and physical layer aspects of satellite selection and satellite switch.<br>- Physical layer aspects related to inter-satellite interference management.<br>  - FFS: Details of interference management techniques.<br>  - NOTE: Companies can bring proposals on physical layer related issues related to inter-satellite interference management.<br>- NOTE: For the two aspects above it is assumed that the UE is only connected to one satellite at a time.<br>- NOTE: Under the label of “Multi-satellite operation” RAN1 discusses topics involving multiple satellites which may be in the same or different orbits/altitudes. Companies may bring further aspects (other than the two aspects above) under this label in future RAN1 meetings. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603091](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603091.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.7.1 | Working Assumption | For 6GR NTN, for S-band LEO-600 and LEO-300, the following assumption for SAN antenna model is adopted:<br>- Other separate assumptions may be further considered for e.g. other satellite payload types.<br>\| Frequency \| S-band (i.e., 2 GHz) \| S-band (i.e., 2 GHz) \|<br>\| Orbit \| LEO-600 \| LEO-300 \|<br>\| TXRU mapping (for phased antenna array) \| Fully connected \| Fully connected \|<br>\| Satellite antenna configurations Option 1: Phased antenna array i.e. (M,N,P,Mg,Ng; Mp, Np), (dH,dV), (dg,H,dg,V) if any (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np) - M: Number of vertical antenna elements within a panel, on one polarization - N: Number of horizontal antenna elements within a panel, on one polarization - P: Number of polarizations - Mg: Number of panels in a column; - Ng: Number of panels in a row; - Mp: Number of vertical TXRUs within a panel, on one polarization - Np: Number of horizontal TXRUs within a panel, on one polarization Note: Mp and Np may not be applicable for fully-connected TXRU. Option 2: Reflector antenna \| Antenna elements: 1296 (36x36) Separation: 0.5 lambda Number of polarizations: 1 or 2. 1-pol: (M,N,P,Mg,Ng)= (36, 36, 1, 1, 1), (dH,dV) = (0.5, 0.5) 2-pol:(M,N,P,Mg,Ng) = (36, 36, 2, 1, 1), (dH,dV) = (0.5, 0.5) \| Antenna elements: 400 (20x20) Separation: 0.5 lambda Number of polarizations: 1 or 2. 1-pol: (M,N,P,Mg,Ng)= (20, 20, 1, 1, 1), , (dH,dV) = (0.5, 0.5) 2-pol: (M,N,P,Mg,Ng) = (20, 20, 2, 1, 1), (dH,dV) = (0.5, 0.5) \|<br>\| Satellite Polarized antenna modelling e.g., models in section 7.3.2 in TR38.901 \| Circular \| Circular \|<br>\| Satellite antenna element gain pattern \| Table 7.3-1 in TR 38.901 Horizontal/vertical 3 dB beam width of single element (degree): [90] for H, 90 for V Element max directivity: 5dBi. An antenna efficiency of 50% is assumed which results in an effective element max gain of 2dB. The resulting overall antenna gain (after applying the array factor) is 33.126dB \| Table 7.3-1 in TR 38.901 Horizontal/vertical 3 dB beam width of single element (degree): [90] for H, 90 for V Element max directivity: 5dBi. An antenna efficiency of 50% is assumed which results in an effective element max gain of 2dB. The resulting overall antenna gain (after applying the array factor) is 28.02dB \|<br>NOTE: For the link budget template, the 50% efficiency should be accounted for (e.g. either in the “boresight antenna element gain”, in “other losses” or in “Cable, connector, combiner, body losses, etc”) | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603091](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603091.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.7.1 | Working Assumption | For 6GR NTN, for the UE antenna model for S-band (all orbits), consider a subset of the following combinations:<br>- FFS: Which of the combinations are considered, Total Number of TXRU, and details of Alt 1/ Alt 2 for the antenna model.<br>- FFS: Whether some of the combinations only apply for some device types.<br>\| UE antenna modelling for RAN1 evaluations \| Total number of antenna elements \| Total number of TXRU \| Alt 1: (M,N,P,Mg,Ng; Mp,Np), (dH,dV), (dg,H,dg,V) if any, or Alt 2: UT device antenna model using candidate antenna locations as described in section 7.3 in TR38.901 \|<br>\| Combination0 \| 1 \| 1T1R, \| Alt 1: 1T: (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np)=(1, 1, 1, 1, 1; 1, 1) 1R: (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np)=(1, 1, 1, 1, 1; 1, 1) Alt 2: 1T 1R \|<br>\| Combination1 \| 2 \| 1T2R, 2T2R \| Alt 1: 2R: (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np)=(1, 2, 1, 1, 1; 1, 2) for single polarization or (1, 1, 2, 1, 1; 1, 1) for dual polarization, (dH,dV)= (0.5, 0.5)λ Alt 2: 2R: [(1, 5), or (4, 8)] as described in section 7.3 in TR 38.901. \|<br>\| Combination2 \| 4 \| 1T4R, 2T4R, 4T4R \| Alt 1: 4R: (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np)=(1, 2, 2, 1, 1; 1, 2) for dual polarization or (2, 2, 1, 1, 1; 2, 2) for single polarization, (dH,dV)= (0.5, 0.5)λ Alt 2: 4R: [(2, 4, 6, 8), or (1, 3, 5, 7)] as described in section 7.3 in TR 38.901 \|<br>\| Combination3 \| 8 \| 1T8R, 2T8R, 4T8R, 8T8R \| Alt 1: (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np)= (1, 4, 2, 1, 1; 1, 4), or (2, 2, 2, 1, 1; 2, 2) for dual polarization or (2, 4, 1, 1, 1; 2, 4) for single polarization , (dH,dV)= (0.5, 0.5)λ Alt 2: (1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8) as described in section 7.3 in TR38.901 \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603091](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603091.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.7.1 | Agreement | For 6GR NTN evaluations, consider the following additional orbit for S-band, at least for link budget calculation purposes:<br>- MEO-7000 | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603091](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603091.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#124bis | 10.7.1 | Agreement | For 6GR NTN, for realizing large scheduling offsets, the following is supported in principle:<br>- Reuse the k_offset concept from NR as a baseline with potential modifications.<br>  - NOTE: Under this option, additional scheduling offsets (e.g. similar to K1/K2 in NR) are assumed to be supported for scheduling flexibility, which apply in addition to k_offset.<br>  - At least broadcast (FFS whether cell or beam specific) k_offset is supported.<br>    - FFS whether UE-specific k_offset is supported.<br>NOTE: This agreement may be revised if 6GR does not define concepts such as K1/K2, or if the baseline design of 6GR scheduling can already cover the RTT range of NTN. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2603091](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2603091.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |

| RAN1#125 | 10.2.1 | Agreement | - Max rank for multi-layer UL DFT-s-OFDM:<br>  - Max rank = 2<br>- Codebook for two-layer UL DFT-s-OFDM:<br>  - For each precoder, each Tx port transmits at most one layer<br>  - Final decision is to be done in UL transmission agenda<br>- Relation of UE’s support of MaxRank for DFT-s-OFDM and CP-OFDM in the uplink:<br>  - An UE supporting MaxRank = X (X=1 or 2) with DFT-s-OFDM supports at least the same MaxRank with CP-OFDM<br>- Waveform Switching<br>  - Support semi-static (RRC configured) PUSCH waveform determination for all RRC states operation<br>    - FFS: details<br>  - Support Dynamic PUSCH waveform determination for RRC connected mode operation<br>    - FFS: details<br>- Note: UE capability for above features can be discussed later once 6G UE capability framework is decided | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603725](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603725.zip) |
| RAN1#125 | 10.2.1 | Agreement | - FDSS with transparent filtering is beneficial to be supported for π/2-BPSK from RAN1 perspective<br>  - FFS: QPSK, 16QAM<br>- Draft LS to RAN4<br>  - FFS: contents e.g., “the exact filter requirements are up to RAN4 discussions.” | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603726](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603726.zip) |
| RAN1#125 | 10.2.1 | Agreement | Draft LS in R1-2605068 is endorsed in principle with following updates. Final LS in R1-2605069 is endorsed.<br>---<br>1	Overall description<br>RAN1 discussed coverage improvements through low PAPR enhancements for uplink DFT-s-OFDM waveform for 6GR.<br>RAN1#125 in May 2026 in Dalian reached an agreement that Frequency Domain Spectrum Shaping (FDSS) with transparent filtering is beneficial at least for π/2-BPSK. The exact wording of the RAN1 agreement is copied below:<br>\| Agreement: FDSS with transparent filtering is beneficial to be supported for π/2-BPSK from RAN1 perspective FFS: QPSK, 16QAM Draft LS to RAN4 FFS: contents e.g., “the exact filter requirements are up to RAN4 discussions.” \|<br>RAN1 has also been studying other PAPR enhancement techniques, with observations made in RA1#125.<br>2	Actions<br>To RAN4<br>ACTION: 	RAN1 respectfully asks RAN4 to take the RAN1 agreement on FDSS with transparent filtering into account in their work on 6GR.<br>3	Dates of next TSG RAN meetings<br>RAN WG#126		24th – 28th August, 2026	Maastricht, Netherlands<br>RAN WG#126bis	12th – 16th October, 2026	Jeju, Korea<br>--- | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605068](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605068.zip) |
| RAN1#125 | 10.2.1 | Agreement | - Followings are used as comparison baseline for further study<br>  - Net gain compared with legacy pi/2 BPSK with Rel-15 5G NR transparent FDSS and legacy QPSK/16QAM without FDSS<br>  - target spectral efficiency points provided by Rel-15 5G NR MCS table for UL DFT-s-OFDM waveform<br>- Companies are encouraged to report PA model used in the evaluation and whether transparent CFR is applied to the baseline<br>- Note: when enhancement technique applies transparent FDSS with ST, companies are encouraged to report at least the result using same transparent FDSS without ST when same modulation is used<br>- FFS: Support for FDSS-SE and corresponding spectral efficiency range<br>- FFS: Support for FDSS-ST and corresponding spectral efficiency range<br>- FFS: transparent/non-transparent filtering | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605068](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605068.zip) |
| RAN1#125 | 10.2.1 | Agreement | Following RAN1 report contents on waveform for communication to RAN are endorsed.<br>Waveform for communication<br>\| What RAN1 has discussed/observed/ agreed \| RAN1 agreed to support CP-OFDM for DL and CP-OFDM/DFT-s-OFDM for UL as defined in 5G NR for 6GR. RAN1 has prioritized the studies on “UL coverage improvement through low UL PAPR enhancement for DFT-s-OFDM” and “multi-rank UL-MIMO for DFT-s-OFDM”. RAN1 agreed to support maximum rank = 2 for multi-rank UL-MIMO for DFT-s-OFDM. RAN1 agreed to support semi-static and dynamic UL waveform switching RAN1 agreed for low UL PAPR enhancement for DFT-s-OFDM that for pi/2 BPSK, Frequency Domain Spectrum Shaping (FDSS) with transparent filtering is beneficial to be supported, and liaised RAN4 accordingly. RAN1 has captured interim observations on other PAPR enhancement techniques for UL DFT-s-OFDM RAN1 noted the study on other waveforms, and RAN1 concluded that further discussion is not planned in waveform agenda item in RAN1 unless tasked by RAN. \|<br>\| What RAN1 plans to continue studying after June \| On low UL PAPR enhancement for DFT-s-OFDM: Further work after June 2026 is required in order to draw definite conclusions on the benefits and potential RAN1 spec impacts of spectral extension/truncation techniques with transparent/non-transparent filtering, the benefits and potential RAN1 spec impacts of non-transparent filtering with no spectral extension/truncation techniques. \|<br>\| What RAN1 plans not to pursue after June \| RAN1 plans not to pursue the study on other waveforms than CP-OFDM for DL and CP-OFDM/DFT-s-OFDM for UL in waveform agenda item in RAN1 unless tasked by RAN. \|<br>\| What RAN1 requests RAN guidance/ decision if any \|  \| | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2603728](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2603728.zip) |
| RAN1#125 | 10.3.1 | Agreement | For the study of the new BG(s) and its corresponding PCM(s) for LDPC extension beyond NR range,<br>- Assume option B1 as agreed in RAN1#124bis for sub-matrix B, i.e.,<br>    - A weight-3 column with corresponding shifting values of [0, X, 0] or [X, 0, X], followed by a dual-diagonal structure with corresponding shifting values of [0,0]<br>- Assume option 1 agreed in RAN1#124bis with modification as below:<br>    - Row splitting, wherein one or more rows outside the core matrix (i.e., sub matrix A and sub matrix B) are split from at least some rows inside the core matrix, and the core column indices of the non-zero entries of the original row are a superset of those of the corresponding split row,<br>    - The core matrix contains Y1 rows, and all elements of Y2 rows in sub-matrix A are ones.<br>        - Y1= 3 or 4<br>        - Y1Y21<br>            - With at least the first row in sub-matrix A included in the Y2 rows<br>    - Quasi-row orthogonality in sub-matrix D, i.e., in which except for certain columns, the row 2k, 2k+1 do not share non-zero entries in the same column in the sub-matrix D<br>    - No puncturing before rate matching as starting point.<br>- Note: The design of new BG considers performance and computational complexity benefits for all applicable modulation order (e.g. 256 QAM and above) and code rates no less than approximately 2/3 at iterations (5, 7,10, 15) compared to BG1 | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605016](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605016.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |

= [3100] Mbps (e.g., 100 MHz, 4 layers, 1024QAM, 0.18 overhead)<br>- For n-th CC, the application condition of new BG, if introduced, consider the following direction<br>\|  \| X \| X > \|<br>\|  \| Case 1: Alt2-1:BG1/BG2 Alt2-2: NW configured BG1/BG2 or BG1/BG2/BG3 \| Case 2: Alt 3-1: NW configured BG1/BG2 or BG1/BG2/BG3 BG1/BG2/BG3 is configured on at least M CC(s) where M is at least 1, and the aggregated data rate (i.e. summation of Yn) on the remaining (N-M) CC(s) using BG1/BG2 is Note: it is up to NW configuration on the selection of M CC(s) Alt 3-2: NW configured BG1/BG2 or BG1/BG2/BG3 per CC Alt 3-3: BG1/BG2/BG3 \|<br>\| > \| Case 3: Alt4-1: NW configured BG1/BG2 or BG1/BG2/BG3 Alt4-2: BG1/BG2/BG3 \| Case 4: Alt5-1: BG1/BG2/BG3 Alt5-2: NW configured BG1/BG2 or BG1/BG2/BG3 \|<br>Based on the above, focus on the following directions for discussion:<br>Direction A:<br>- Alt 1-1,<br>- Case 1: BG1/BG2<br>- case 2, case 3: NW configured BG1/BG2 or BG1/BG2/BG3 per CC<br>- Case 4: BG1/BG2/BG3<br>Direction B:<br>        - NW configured BG1/BG2 or BG1/BG2/BG3 per CC for existing band<br>  - Alt 4-2 for spectrum around 7 GHz<br>- Case 2: Alt 3-1<br>- Case 4: BG1/BG2/BG3<br>- FFS on BG selection criteria when BG1/BG2/BG3 is used on a CC<br>- FFS: BG3 applicability for FR2 (single CC, multi-CC), FR1/(around 7 GHz) -FR2 aggregation<br>- FFS: UE capability for BG3 (optional, mandatory, other details, etc.)<br>- FFS: details on application condition of new BG for UL, if introduced | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605018](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605018.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.3.1 | Conclusion | Report the following for interim RAN1 report on Coding check point.<br>\| What RAN1 has discussed/observed/agreed \| RAN1 agreed for 6G channel coding, LDPC is used for data (including SIBs) and Polar code is used for L1 control information (larger than 11 bits, including PBCH). RAN1 agreed for 6G data channel coding: 1) working assumption is that reuse of NR LDPC design is supported for data rate within NR range; 2) study LDPC extension for data rate beyond NR range. In addition, RAN1 studied and identified the structure and parameters of potential LDPC extension. The evaluation results of LDPC code extension beyond NR range have been captured in R1-2605123. RAN1 agreed on the framework of application condition of potential LDPC extension, if introduced. RAN1 agreed for 6G control channel coding: 1) RAN1 working assumption is that for control information within NR range (larger than 11 bits), reuse of NR Polar code design is supported. RAN1 concluded there is no consensus on motivation(s) for potential extension/enhancement for Polar code design for payload size within NR range (larger than 11bits). 2) study Polar code extension for payload size beyond NR range based on the evaluation assumptions in RAN1#124. In addition, RAN1 agreed to study more than 2 segments for Polar code with UCI payload size larger than 1706 bits. RAN1 agreed for 6G small UCI with 3~11 bits: 1) RAN1 agreed to at least consider 5G RM code for small UCI with payload size of 3~11 bits, identify the justifiable drawback of 5G RM code, if exists, study potential solution(s). \|<br>\| What RAN1 plans to continue studying after June \| RAN1 plans to continue to study Polar code extension for payload size beyond NR range (larger than 11bits). RAN1 plans to continue to identify the justifiable drawbacks of 5G RM code, if exists, study potential solution(s). \|<br>\| What RAN1 plans not to pursue after June \| RAN1 plans not to pursue Polar code extension for payload size within NR range (larger than 11bits) after June. \|<br>\| What RAN1 requests RAN guidance/decision if any \| RAN1 needs RAN guidance on if and how to proceed on the remaining study for LDPC code extension. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605018](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605018.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.3.2 | Agreement | - Regarding the proposed geometric shaping or probabilistic shaping for modulation enhancements for 6GR:<br>  - If any enhancements for modulation based on geometric shaping or probabilistic shaping for 6GR is supported, it can be configured by network in addition to equal probability uniform QAM that is already supported for 6GR.<br>  - With respect to enhancements for modulation based on geometric shaping or probabilistic shaping, the status is captured under A, B, C as the following:<br>- A) Regarding geometric shaping for 6GR, the following options have been considered:<br>    - Option 1: 1D-NUC<br>    - Option 2: 2D-NUC<br>      - Option 2-A: QAM-CS based 2D-NUC where the constellation points are selected from a larger uniform QAM constellation<br>      - Option 2-B: NP-NUC where the receiver assumes the constellation is uniform QAM<br>      - Option 2-C: Unconstrained constellation 2D-NUC<br>  - For further study of GS, if any, the following option is considered:<br>    - Option 1. 1D-NUC for modulation order Qm in the range of: Qm>=4<br>      - For quantization bit width Y per I/Q: Y<=[10] bits<br>      - Bit to symbol mapping for 1D-NUC: Gray mapping as in uniform QAM<br>  - Total number of NUC constellations is no larger than X for one MCS table<br>    - FFS: Value of X<br>  - If GS is further studied is a separate discussion<br>  - B) Regarding probabilistic shaping for 6GR, the following options have been considered:<br>    - Option1: ESS for PS DM<br>      - Option 1-A: No limitation on the number of shaped bits per I/Q symbol<br>      - Option 1-B: The number of shaped bits for each I or Q sample is no more than 2 bits<br>    - Option2: ECC-DM for PS DM, where the number of shaped bits for each I or Q sample is no more than 2 bits<br>    - Option3: CCDM for PS DM<br>      - Option 3-A: No limitation on the number of shaped bits per I/Q symbol<br>      - Option 3-B: The number of shaped bits for each I or Q sample is no more than 2 bits<br>    - Option4: Huffman coding-based shaping for PS DM<br>  - For further study of PS, if any, below options can be considered:<br>    - Option 1-B<br>    - Option 2<br>  - If PS is further studied is a separate discussion<br>  - For further study of PS schemes for 6GR, consider maximum DM/DDM block length of X I or Q samples<br>    - 64<=X<=256 for ESS based DM<br>    - X=1024 for ECC based DM<br>  - C) Regarding the proposed enhancements for modulation based on geometric shaping and probabilistic shaping for 6GR, none of the enhancements are needed. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605122](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605122.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.3.2 | Conclusion | For interim RAN1 report on Modulation check point, the following changes are applied to R1-2605145:<br>  - For UL 1K QAM for FWA use cases, UPT performance is captured for some UL FWA UE in SLS<br>  - Enhancement of MCS table design<br>The updated interim RAN1 report on Modulation check point as following<br>\| What RAN1 has discussed/observed/agreed \| Agreed RAN1 agreed to at least support 5G NR equal probability uniform QAM constellation for 6G PS/GS: For PS, if any, down-selected to ECC-DM and ESS based DM with up to 2 shaped bits per I/Q symbol. For GS, if any, down-selected to 1D-NUC. Discussed For DL 4K QAM and UL 1K QAM, RAN1 discussed use cases, and defined SLS evaluation methodology. Multiple sources provided LLS and SLS evaluations. For constellation shaping, both GS and PS are being considered, with different schemes and parameter choices for each design. The complexity and spec impact for both GS and PS are evaluated and compared. Observed For constellation shaping, the fixed SE performance is evaluated across different channels and corresponding observations captured For constellation shaping, the complexity is evaluated and corresponding observations captured For UL 1K QAM for FWA use cases, UPT performance is captured for some UL FWA UE in SLS \|<br>\| What RAN1 plans to continue studying after June \| Enhancement of MCS table design \|<br>\| What RAN1 plans not to pursue after June \| PS/GS: PS other than ECC-DM and ESS based DM with up to 2 shaped bits per I/Q symbol. GS other than 1D-NUC \|<br>\| What RAN1 requests RAN guidance/decision if any \| RAN1 needs RAN guidance on if and how to proceed on the remaining study for UL 1K QAM, DL 4K QAM, and constellation shaping based enhancements for modulation \| | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2605122](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2605122.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |

| RAN1#125 | 10.5.0 | Agreement | For aNB semi-static SBFD, study at least the following aspects<br>- SBFD time-frequency resource determination<br>- Random access procedure<br>- UE transmission, reception and measurement behaviors and procedures within non-SBFD/SBFD symbols, or across SBFD and non-SBFD symbols, including link direction determination in symbols with SBFD operation<br>  - Note: “non-SBFD symbols” and “SBFD symbols” are only for discussion purpose. Whether to introduce configuration of SBFD symbol type will be separately discussed. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.0 | Agreement | For the RAN1 study of “Re-use of existing 5G mid-band (~3.5GHz) site grid for 6G deployments in at least around 7 GHz and targeting comparable coverage to 5G mid-band”,<br>- -999 dBm/Hz is used for (15) Receiver interference density<br>  - Note: Zero interference margin is assumed here for coverage gap identification<br>- Companies can additionally use the value(s) of (15) Receiver interference density based on SLS<br>  - Note: Non-zero interference margin is assumed here for coverage gap identification.<br>- RAN1 to discuss and agree on the value(s) for (15) from July 27th - August 7th 2026  and use it for coverage gap identification<br>- The identified coverage gap assuming zero interference margin and non-zero interference margin will be captured separately<br>- RAN1 to draw conclusions on the identified coverage gap assuming the non-zero interference margin(s)<br>Note: Interference margin (X dB) is defined as entry (16) - (14) - (13) in the link budget table<br>- (13) Receiver noise figure<br>- (14) Thermal noise density;<br>- (15) Receiver interference density: 10log(10^((X + (13) + (14))/10) - 10^(( (13) + (14))/10))<br>- (16) Total noise plus interference density: 10 log (10^(( (13) + (14))/10) + 10^((15)/10)) | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.0 | Agreement | Study the scenarios and use cases of multiple carriers where for initial access a UE can utilize resources for UL (Msg1, etc) or for both UL (Msg1, etc.) and DL (Msg2, etc.) in one carrier other than the carrier where the UE obtains the system information providing configuration for those resources.<br>- Study which resources can be provided by aNB<br>- Study how UE selects resources, including how UE obtains DL synchronization for the transmission of Msg1 including co-located and non-collocated deployment scenarios<br>- RAN1 to coordinate with RAN4 as necessary, e.g., timing, UL power control and any association between carriers | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.0 | Agreement | From RAN1 perspective, to support 6G-6G spectrum aggregation for non-collocated deployment scenarios for FR1-FR2 combination, assuming backhaul latency up to 10ms<br>- Both 6G-6G-DC with two cell groups and 6G-6G-CA with two PUCCH groups using separate TA per group to can support non-collocated FR1-FR2 spectrum aggregation with imperfect backhaul.<br>  - The physical layer functionalities for both solutions are similar.<br>- For 6G-6G-CA with a single PUCCH group, the following have been identified<br>  - Compared to 6G-6G-DC and 6G-6G-CA with two PUCCH groups, 6G-6G-CA with single PUCCH group may provide better UL coverage in the case the HARQ-ACK for FR2 is transmitted in FR1.<br>  - The larger HARQ RTT caused by the backhaul delay and rescheduling timeline can lead to HARQ process starvation and degrade the DL throughput.<br>- Note: the term of PUCCH group similar to 5G design is assumed here only to facilitate discussions and it does not imply anything agreed for 6G design. Similarly, the same clarification is applied to any 5G term used above. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.0 | Agreement | For the 6GR smallest maximum UE bandwidth for at least one lower-tier device for FDD with 15kHz SCS:<br>- Reasons for possible recommendation of 20MHz RF and BB bandwidth for both UL and DL (i.e. Alt 1) include:<br>  - If initial access is independent of UE’s bandwidth capability, which requires initial access for both DL and UL to be contained within 20MHz and 5 MHz for Alt 1 and Alt 2 respectively even for FDD bands with spectrum allocation larger than 5MHz, Alt 1 allows both the lowest-tier device and MBB devices to operate in the same band with better system performance in terms of DL coverage assuming same PSD and capacity for initial access, paging and random access compared with Alt 2.<br>    - For Alt 2, it results in lower performance for initial access for 6G MBB UEs compared to 5G MBB UEs, since 5G networks can configure a bandwidth larger than 5 MHz for initial access. But such DL coverage losses might be mitigated by techniques such as power boosting.<br>  - The additional UE modem complexity reduction of Alt 2 compared with Alt 1 is no more than 10% when the same number of duplexers/SAW-filters/switches is considered for all alternatives<br>  - In case of a multi-RAT device, UE modem complexity reduction of Alt 2 compared with Alt 1 may not be exploitable in all implementations, e.g. when the other RAT requires supporting a 20 MHz bandwidth.<br>  - Alt. 1 can reach the minimum PHY peak data rate within a larger DL coverage assuming same PSD by using a lower MCS in 20 MHz CBW compared with Alt. 2 but in that case a bandwidth larger than 5MHz has to be used.<br>- Reasons for possible recommendation of 5MHz RF and BB bandwidth for both UL and DL (i.e. Alt 2)  include:<br>  - Alt 2 achieves ~8-10% additional UE modem complexity reduction compared with Alt 1 for a device supporting single FDD band<br>  - 5MHz BW is feasible for all device types for initial access, paging and random access for FDD based on previous study and deployment experience<br>    - More scheduling flexibility is offered by Alt 1 compared with Alt 2 if initial access is common to all device types, when the available bandwidth is larger than 5 MHz in an FDD band if an operator wants to operate both lowest-tier and MBB UEs in the same band. UL scheduling flexibility loss can be partially mitigated if fast frequency retuning (1-2 symbols) can be supported by lowest-tier UEs.<br>- Reasons for possible recommendation of 20MHz RF bandwidth for both UL and DL, and 20MHz BB bandwidth for DL and 5MHz BB bandwidth for UL (i.e. Alt 3 with only BB BW reduction for UL) include:<br>  - If initial access is independent of UE’s bandwidth capability, which requires initial access for both DL and UL to be contained within 20MHz and 5 MHz for Alt 3 and Alt 2 respectively and UL transmission is limited to 5MHz within each symbol for Alt 3 even for FDD bands with spectrum allocation larger than 5MHz, Alt 3 allows both the lowest-tier device and MBB devices to operate in the same band with better system performance in terms of DL coverage assuming same PSD and capacity for initial access, paging and random access compared with Alt 2.<br>    - For Alt 2, it results in lower performance for initial access for 6G MBB UEs compared to 5G MBB UEs, since 5G networks can configure a bandwidth larger than 5 MHz for initial access. But such DL coverage losses might be mitigated by techniques such as power boosting.<br>  - The additional UE modem complexity reduction of Alt 2 compared with Alt 3 (with only BB BW reduction for UL) is no more than 7% for a device supporting single FDD band<br>  - The additional UE modem complexity reduction of Alt 3 (with only BB BW reduction for UL) compared with Alt 1 is less than 4% for a device supporting single FDD band.<br>  - Alt. 3 can reach the minimum PHY peak data rate within a larger DL coverage assuming same PSD by using a lower MCS in 20 MHz CBW compared with Alt. 2 but in that case a bandwidth larger than 5MHz has to be used.<br>- For all the three alternatives,<br>  - There are more 5MHz spectrums than 20MHz spectrums in sub-1 GHz bands available for lowest-tier devices, although some operators may only have spectrums larger than 5MHz for lowest-tier devices<br>  - For a device supporting multiple FDD bands, equipped with multiple band-specific analogue filters (duplex filters for FD-FDD, SAW filters for HD-FDD), when comparing on the basis of same PHY peak rate (~10 Mbps) and same number of band-specific analogue filters, the additional UE modem complexity reduction of Alt 2 and Alt 3 compared to Alt 1 is less than for single band device.<br>  - For HD-FDD UL, five sources in RAN1 [R1-2603776][R1-2603941][R1-2604259][R1-2604548] [R1-2605059] observe that elimination of band-specific SAW filters can provide a substantial (~ 30-60%) modem complexity reduction for multi-band devices.<br>    - RAN1 understands that multiple options are under discussion in RAN4 to support SAW-less UE implementation.<br>    - The feasibility and benefits of different RAN4 options to facilitate supporting SAW-less UE implementation for different RAN1 smallest maximum UE bandwidth alternatives is up to RAN4.<br>    - The outcome of the RAN4 study may have implications for the RF and/or BB bandwidth for HD-FDD UL. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.0 | Agreement | For the 6GR smallest maximum UE bandwidth for at least one lower-tier device, RAN1 recommends Alt 1 for TDD with 30kHz SCS<br>- Alt 1: 20MHz RF and BB bandwidth for both UL and DL<br>Note：No decision in RAN1 about the spectrum applicability and applicable frequency bands for lower-tier device.<br>Note:  Above agreement has no linkage to FDD | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.0 | Agreement | Update agreement for the study of link direction determination for dynamic TDD in RAN1#124bis as follows.<br>For the study of link direction determination for dynamic TDD, consider the following options:<br>- Option 1:<br>  - TDD pattern is based on DL, UL and flexible symbols<br>  - TDD pattern is semi-statically configured<br>  - No dynamic slot format indication as in NR<br>  - For dynamically scheduled single transmission/reception in flexible symbols, UE link direction is determined only based on scheduling DCI<br>  - Study transmissions/receptions without associated DCI other than the configured transmissions/receptions in flexible symbols (if configured)<br>  - For configured transmissions/receptions in flexible symbols, consider the following alternatives<br>    - Alt 1: Configured transmissions/receptions in flexible symbols are valid and the link direction of the flexible symbols are not expected to be overridden by DCI<br>      - FFS exceptions<br>    - Alt 2: Configured transmissions/receptions in flexible symbols are invalid<br>      - FFS exceptions<br>    - Alt 3: Configured transmissions/receptions in flexible symbols are valid and can be overridden by dynamic scheduled transmission/reception in the same symbol if sufficient processing time is provided<br>      - FFS exceptions<br>    - Other alternatives are not precluded<br>  - FFS: Maximum one DL-UL switching point within a TDD pattern<br>- Option 2:<br>  - Semi-statically configured TDD pattern is based on DL, UL and flexible symbols<br>  - Dynamically indicated TDD pattern is based on DL, UL and guard symbols<br>    - Dynamically indicated TDD pattern indicates DL, UL or guard symbols for flexible symbols according to semi-statically configured TDD pattern<br>    - The TDD pattern should be provided to the UE with sufficient processing time to apply the TDD pattern change<br>  - UE link direction is determined based on TDD pattern<br>  - Link direction is not expected to be changed within certain time window<br>  - Configured transmission/receptions are allowed on resolved flexible symbols<br>    - Resolved flexible symbols are the symbols that already been scheduled with DL or UL within the time window<br>  - FFS: Maximum one DL-UL switching point within a TDD pattern<br>- Option 3:<br>  - TDD pattern, based on DL, UL and guard symbols (i.e. no flexible symbols)<br>  - UE is dynamically indicated with a TDD pattern out of predefined/preconfigured TDD pattern<br>    - The TDD pattern should be provided to the UE with sufficient processing time to apply the TDD pattern change<br>- Option 4:<br>  - TDD pattern is based on DL, UL and flexible symbols<br>  - TDD pattern is semi-statically configured<br>  - No dynamic slot format indication as in NR<br>  - For dynamically scheduled single transmission/reception in flexible symbols, UE link direction is determined only based on scheduling DCI<br>  - Study transmissions/receptions without associated DCI other than the configured transmissions/receptions in flexible symbols (if configured)<br>  - Configured transmissions/receptions are valid only in resolved flexible symbols<br>    - Link direction is not expected to be changed within certain time window<br>    - Resolved flexible symbols are the symbols that already been scheduled with DL or UL within the time window<br>- Other options are not precluded<br>FFS: scenarios, where whether/how to mitigate CLI of adjacent frequency between inter-operator for dynamic TDD | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.0 | Agreement | For support of UEs operating with Maximum CBW of 400MHz in DL (i.e., non-CA single CC) and Maximum CBW of 200MHz in UL in a BS with a maximum CBW of 400MHz for around 7GHz, the frequency location of UL with a maximum CBW of 200MHz should be configured with certain flexibility within the entire configured DL bandwidth.<br>- Center frequencies of DL and UL can be different from UE perspective<br>- FFS configuration flexibility | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.0 | Agreement | The following template are used to capture the identified coverage gap between ~7GHz and 5G mid-band for different signals and channels during initial access,<br>FFS: where coverage gap is determined as the trimmed mean of all samples, trimmed mean is calculated by discarding the single lowest and highest values. or where coverage gap is determined as the median of all samples, and between these two, down-selection should be done before July 31<br>Table X: Coverage gap between 7.0 GHz and 5G mid-band (3.5 GHz) for different signals and channels during initial access [Template]<br>\| Signals/channels \| Number of Sample(s) \| Coverage gap (FFS: trimmed mean/median) \| Standard Deviation (FFS: trimmed/median) \|<br>\| PSS/SSS \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| PBCH \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| SIB1 PDCCH \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| SIB1 PDSCH \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| Msg2 PDSCH \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| Msg4 PDSCH \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| PRACH format 0 \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| PRACH format B4 \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| PRACH format C2 \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| Msg3 PUSCH \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| Msg5 PUSCH \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\| PUCCH format 1 \|  \|  \|  \|<br>Single pair of reference carrier frequencies (i.e. 3.5 GHz and 7.0 GHz) is used to generate the table above. For the coverage results that have been reported by companies for 2.6 GHz carrier, a common gap between 2.6 GHz and 3.5 GHz for all signals/channels is captured as an observation by trimmed mean, or median of the samples of coverage gaps between Msg3@2.6 GHz and Msg3@3.5 GHz. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.0 | Conclusion | For the RAN1 study of “Re-use of existing 5G mid-band (~3.5GHz) site grid for 6G deployments in at least around 7 GHz and targeting comparable coverage to 5G mid-band”, to align the usage of the correction factors of BS antenna gain of common beams in co-site deployment evaluation, the following are observed as the common understanding<br>- the total antenna gain of BS DL common beam is assumed to be unchanged/comparable if the beamwidth of SSB beams is unchanged, regardless of whether the number of BS antenna elements or BS TxRUs increases or not.<br>  - For DL: The total BS antenna gain above refers to the sum value of entry (4)+(5)) in the link budget table.<br>- The total antenna gain of BS UL common beam is expected not to be smaller than that of BS DL common beam if the beamwidth of SSB beams is unchanged.<br>  - For UL: The total BS antenna gain above refers to the sum value of entry (11)+(11bis) in the link budget table.<br>Note:<br>- (4) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 3 & antenna gain component 4 of transmitter = (4a) – (4b) (dB)<br>- (5) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 2 of transmitter = (5a) - (5b) (dB) Note: zero for uplink<br>- (11) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 3 & antenna gain component 4 of receiver = (11a) - (11b) (dB)<br>- (11bis) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 2 of receiver = (11bis-a) - (11bis-b) (dB) Note: zero for downlink | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |

| RAN1#125 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | For idle mode mobility,<br>- At least 6GR SSS of 6GR SSB is supported as measurement resource.<br>  - FFS other signal in addition (e.g. other types of 6GR sync signal or reference signal, DMRS of PBCH)<br>- For measurement metric, at least RSRP is supported, FFS<br>  - RSRQ and the default SS-RSRQ definition if supported,<br>  - SINR,<br>and select one of those two in addition to RSRP | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx) |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | Study the following options for 6GR SSB time-domain structure<br>- Option 1: one 6GR SSB spans X (4<=X<=7) symbols within a slot<br>  - Option 1-1:  one 6GR SSB spans 4 symbols within a slot<br>  - Option 1-2:  one 6GR SSB spans X (4<X <=7) symbols within a slot<br>- Option 2: one 6GR SSB spans Y (7<Y<=12) symbols within a slot | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx) |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | For the purpose of discussing 6GR SSB burst pattern and associated default SSB periodicity, study the following:<br>- The max number of candidate 6GR SSBs in a 6GR SSB burst: X<br>- The max number of candidate 6GR SSB burst(s) in a default 6GR SSB periodicity: Y<br>- The max number of candidate 6GR SSB repetitions within a default 6GR SSB periodicity: M<br>- Default SSB periodicity for initial cell selection: T<br>- The max number of default 6GR SSB periodicities (similar as what is assumed for evaluation in 5G NR performance) for UE performing 6GR SSB combining: N<br>- Note: As an example, for NR in 3.5 GHz, X = 8, Y = 1, M = 1, N = 4, T = 20ms.<br>For the study on the values of X, Y, Nand M for a given default SSB periodicity T, at least SSB detection performance, UE complexity, UE measurement aspects should be considered. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx) |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | For the study of 6GR SSB performance, the following can be assumed<br>- Alt.1: Combining is performed across all 6GR SSB repetitions within a 6GR SSB burst in case of intra-burst repetition within an SSB periodicity<br>- Alt.2: Combining is performed across all 6GR SSB repetitions across 6GR SSB bursts in case of inter-burst repetition (Y>1) within a SSB periodicity<br>- Alt.3: Combining is performed across all 6GR SSBs within a 6GR SSB burst<br>- Alt.4: Combining is performed across different SSB periodicities<br>- Alt.5: Combining is not performed across 6GR SSBs within a SSB periodicity when M = 1<br>- Alt.6: Combination of above alternatives<br>Note: To compare different 6GR SSB burst patterns, the same total number of SSBs (X*Y*N) within N*T are assumed.<br>Note: The above assumptions are only for evaluation purpose and do not mandate UE implementations.<br>Note: Companies to report which combining(s) is/are assumed in the study.<br>Note: Companies to report the combining details and assumptions<br>Note: Companies to report the burst pattern design details, e.g. the duration of the SSB burst, interval between different SSB burst.<br>Note: SSB performance, UE complexity, UE measurement aspects should be considered.<br>Note: Companies to report other related assumptions, e.g., whether power boosting is applied. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx) |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | As a baseline, the number of 6GR physical cell IDs is 1008<br>- FFS: Motivation to extend or reduce the number of PCI | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx) |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | 6GR synchronization signals (PSS and SSS) in 6GR SSB carry full PCI information as baseline<br>- FFS: Motivation to carry other information<br>- FFS: Motivation to carry part of PCI information by other signals, e.g., PBCH DMRS, if 6GR synchronization signals carry part of PCI information | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx) |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | For initial access procedures starting from initial cell search and cell search of reselection till the completion of random access in multi-TRP based deployments, study the following options<br>- Option 1: Multiple TRPs are transparent to the UE, and corresponding definition about what transparent Multiple TRPs mean<br>- Option 2: Multiple TRPs are non-transparent to the UE, and corresponding definition about what non-transparent Multiple TRPs mean<br>Note: Companies are encouraged to study the coverage performance, network energy saving, the associated signalling overhead, access latency, UE complexity, and compatibility with single-TRP deployments for both options.<br>Note: RACH related procedure/behaviour will be discussed under AI 10.5.1.2 | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx) |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.1.2 | Agreement | Study the need, justification, and impact of increasing the maximum number of preambles per RO above 64 | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.1.2 | Agreement | Study if a subset of NR preamble formats is redundant for 6GR use cases and deployment scenarios. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.1.2 | Agreement | Update the TRP antenna configuration for 4GHz from (8, 2, 2, 1, 1; 1, 2) (optional combination 0) to (12, 8, 2, 1, 1; 4, 8) (combination 1).<br>Remove brackets from PUSCH duration: [28] OS for the evaluation of TBoMS. (For agreement made for LLS for PUSCH in RAN#124b) | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.1.2 | Agreement | Update the UT antenna configuration as follows:<br>\| Antenna Configuration at the UE \| For TDL: Around 700 MHz, 2 GHz: 1T1R, 1T2R, 2T2R Around 2/4 GHz: 1T2R, 2T4R Around 7/15 GHz: 1T4R, 2T4R Around 30 GHz: 2T2R per panel as agreed in AI 10.1 For CDL: - Around 700 MHz, 2 GHz: 1T1R (Combination 0) - 700MHz, 2 GHz, 4 GHz: handheld UT model (from Clause 7.3.2 of TR38.901) with 1T2R (Combination 1), 1T4R (Combination 2), 2T4R (Combination 2) - 7 GHz, 15 GHz: handheld UT model (from Clause 7.3.2 of TR38.901) with 1T4R, 2T4R (Combination 2) - 30GHz (as agreed in AI 10.1): Company to report which UE antenna configuration is used Antenna radiation pattern: based on SLS EVM (from Agenda 10.1) \| | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.1.2 | Agreement | Study and identify set of essential information, if any, that is required to be conveyed as part of random access Msg 1/Msg 3/MsgA, respectively.<br>- Consider the RAN2 LS in R1-2601765 (R2-2601354)<br>Study mechanisms of conveying identified essential information | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.5.1.2 | Agreement | Study coverage enhancement mechanics and identify essential requirements, features, and components to support random access coverage requirements, including use cases, deployment scenarios for coverage enhancements.<br>Note: Moderator summary R1-2605052 Section 4.14 contains information of PRACH sequence/preamble format proposals from companies. Companies are encouraged to review the information for future evaluations. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
