| RAN1#125 | 10.7.1 | Agreement | For S band GEO:<br>- Set 6G-1: Reuse GEO Set 1 from 38.821<br>- Set 6G-2: Reuse GEO Set 1 from 38.821 with the following modifications:<br>  - 30m antenna diameter instead of 22m antenna diameter.<br>  - Antenna gain of 55dBi instead of 51dBi<br>  - G/T of 23dB/K instead of 19dB/K.<br>  - NOTE: EIRP density remains the same | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604771](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604771.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.7.1 | Working Assumption | For 6GR NTN, for Ka-band LEO-600, the following assumption for SAN antenna model is adopted:<br>- Other separate assumptions may be further considered for e.g. other satellite payload types.<br>\| Frequency \| Ka-band \|<br>\| Orbit \| LEO-600 \|<br>\| TXRU mapping (for phased antenna array) \| Fully connected \|<br>\| Satellite antenna configurations Option 1: Phased antenna array i.e. (M,N,P,Mg,Ng; Mp, Np), (dH,dV), (dg,H,dg,V) if any (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np) - M: Number of vertical antenna elements within a panel, on one polarization - N: Number of horizontal antenna elements within a panel, on one polarization - P: Number of polarizations - Mg: Number of panels in a column; - Ng: Number of panels in a row; - Mp: Number of vertical TXRUs within a panel, on one polarization - Np: Number of horizontal TXRUs within a panel, on one polarization Note: Mp and Np may not be applicable for fully-connected TXRU. Option 2: Reflector antenna \| Antenna elements: 1600 (40 x 40) Separation: 0.5 lambda Number of polarizations: 1 or 2. 1-pol: (M,N,P,Mg,Ng)= (40, 40, 1, 1, 1), (dH,dV) = (0.5, 0.5) 2-pol:(M,N,P,Mg,Ng) = (40, 40, 2, 1, 1), (dH,dV) = (0.5, 0.5) \|<br>\| Satellite Polarized antenna modelling e.g., models in section 7.3.2 in TR38.901 \| Circular \|<br>\| Satellite antenna element gain pattern \| Table 7.3-1 in TR 38.901 Horizontal/vertical 3 dB beam width of single element (degree): 90 for H, 90 for V Element max directivity: 5dBi. An antenna efficiency of 50% is assumed which results in an effective element max gain of 2dB. The resulting overall antenna gain (after applying the array factor) is 34.03dB \|<br>NOTE: For the link budget template, the 50% efficiency should be accounted for (e.g. either in the “boresight antenna element gain”, in “other losses” or in “Cable, connector, combiner, body losses, etc”)<br>Note: The above does not impact whether the multi-layer MIMO will be studied or not. | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604771](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604771.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#125 | 10.7.1 | Working Assumption | For Ka-band LEO-600 in 6GR NTN for UL and DL, take the following scenarios as the starting point for further evaluation:<br>- Note: all the values in the table below will be revisited. Companies are encouraged to use these values for initial evaluations and initial alignment, but conclusions will not be drawn based on these values if they are modified.<br>\|  \| Set 6G-1 \| Set 6G-2 \|<br>\| Maximum Satellite Bandwidth per beam (MHz) \| 400.00 \| 400.00 \|<br>\| Satellite EIRP density/beam (dBW/MHz) \| 11 \| 16 \|<br>\| Total number of simultaneously active beams \| 64 \| 16 \|<br>\| Satellite Max Tx/Rx antenna gain (dB) \| 34.00 \| 34.00 \|<br>\| Payload total DL power level (dBW) \| 21 \| 20 \|<br>\| Total number of beam footprints \| [800] \| [800] \|<br>\| % of active beams \| [8] \| [2] \|<br>\| Satellite max G/T (dB/K) \| 6.7 \| 5.1 \| | [Chair notes RAN1#125_eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23125_eom.docx)<br>[R1-2604771](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_125/Docs/R1-2604771.zip)<br>`[125-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.2.1 | Agreement | Agree on the embedded template for UL PAPR reduction scheme results collection in section 4.5 of R1-2606804.<br>Companies having evaluation results are encouraged to fill the template and upload it into the draft folder indicated by FL by 27th August EOD. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606803](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606803.zip) |

| RAN1#126 | 10.2.1 | Agreement | Complete the following list of UL PAPR reduction techniques for further down-selection<br>- Option 1: FDSS with no spectrum extension or truncation<br>  - Transparent / non-transparent<br>  - Modulation: Pi/2 BPSK / QPSK<br>  - Applicable SE range: FFS<br>- Option 2: FDSS with spectrum extension<br>  - Transparent / non-transparent<br>  - Modulation: Pi/2 BPSK / QPSK / 16QAM<br>  - Applicable SE range: FFS<br>- Option 3: FDSS with spectrum truncation<br>  - Transparent / non-transparent<br>  - Modulation: Pi/2 BPSK<br>  - Applicable SE range: FFS<br>- FFS: condition on when to apply finally selected option/scheme<br>- Note: target to down select transparent / non-transparent and modulation (at least on 16QAM) for above options at RAN1#126bis | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606804](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606804.zip) |
| RAN1#126 | 10.2.1 | Agreement | For DFT size before SE/ST is applied, at least option 1 is supported, and whether option 2 is supported or not is further discussed and to be decided by RAN1#127<br>- Option 1: 12 * 2x3y5z subcarriers.<br>- Option 2: X * 2x3y5z subcarriers, where X = 4.<br>  - FFS: upper bound on applicable number of RBs for option 2 | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606804](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606804.zip) |
| RAN1#126 | 10.3.1 | Agreement | For the sub-matrix E of BG3, support the following option<br>- Option E2 (from agreement in RAN1#124bis): A lower triangular matrix, wherein the number of columns with degree larger than 1 is less than I.<br>- FFS: the value of I.<br>- E can not be the identity matrix (i.e. Option E1). | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606773](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606773.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.3.1 | Agreement | With respect to the decision for capability of BG3 in UL tasked by RANP, the following is supported:<br>supporting BG3 in UL is mandatory with capability signaling for UEs except for UEs with up to 20MHz maximum UL channel bandwidth in any band supported by UE, i.e., BG3 in UL is optional for UEs with up to 20MHz maximum UL channel bandwidth in any band supported by UE.<br>Note: Whether any additional exception to the above is needed is up to RAN plenary decision. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606774](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606774.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.3.1 | Agreement | For the evaluation of coding based candidates for small UCI with payload size of 3~11 bits, refer to the evaluation assumptions in PUCCH/PUSCH, with the following update<br>- Use AWGN channel<br>  - Note: Companies can provide results for other channels<br>- Info. block length (bits w/o CRC): 3:1:11<br>- Metrics: SNR at target BLER, BER<br>  - Other metrics agreed in PUCCH agenda can be also reported by companies<br>- Companies to report the details of PUSCH/PUCCH including DM-RS configuration, PUCCH format, if any<br>- Companies to report impact on receiver complexity/latency and transmitter complexity/latency, if any.<br>\| The channel coding based candidates for evaluation are as below \|<br>- 5G RM code (baseline)<br>- Enhanced RM code<br>- Enhanced PC-Polar code<br>- Golay-based code<br>- Vertical and horizontal code<br>- CRC polynomial code<br>- Note: Companies to report the details of coding based candidates<br>- Note: Companies to provide comparison with 5G RM code.<br>- Note: Companies can choose one or more candidates above for evaluation | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606775](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606775.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.3.2 | Conclusion | With respect to RANP task to RAN1 (copied below) to conclude the support for constellation shaping in RAN1:<br>\| Conclusion (RAN#112) RAN1 to continue the study on GS (i.e. 1D-NUC) and PS (ESS based PS and ECC based PS in Q3/26 decide by Q3 whether or not to support constellation shaping and if supported, between PS (including whether it is 1-bit or 2-bits PS scheme) and GS support of different constellation shaping schemes for DL and UL is precluded (e.g. PS for DL, GS for UL) If there is no consensus on the support of constellation shaping in Q3/26 the study on constellation shaping will be stopped \|<br>RAN1 concludes the following:<br>- There is no consensus in RAN1 to support any constellation shaping schemes for either DL or UL.<br>- Summary of companies’ preferences is in R1-2606762.<br>  - Some proponents of some constellation shaping schemes stated that they would agree to withdraw that scheme. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606883](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606883.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.3.2 | Conclusion | With respect to RANP guidance to RAN1 (copied below) for support of higher order modulation:<br>\| RAN Chair guidance (RAN#112) For 1K QAM on UL and 4K QAM on DL: RAN1/RAN4 to continue study and make decision in Q3/26. \|<br>From RAN1 perspective, for DL 4K QAM, and UL 1K QAM in 6GR, if supported, is restricted to FWA use case only and not for eMBB use case<br>- The restriction to FWA use case shall be enforced in the spec<br>  - FFS: How to reflect this restriction in the spec<br>  - Other restrictions on DL 4K QAM and UL 1K QAM can be identified<br>Multiple sources performed SLS to evaluate DL 4K QAM and UL 1K QAM under agreed evaluation methodology for FWA use case. The detailed assumptions and results are captured in the tables provided in R1-2606762.<br>From RAN1 perspective,<br>- RAN1 recommends to adopt 1k QAM for UL.<br>- No consensus in RAN1 to recommend adopting 4K QAM for DL | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606883](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606883.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.4.1 | Agreement | For further study of initial access for idle mode multi-carrier operation, consider at least the following potential use cases:<br>  - UC-1: UL coverage enhancement / DL-UL coverage imbalance<br>  - UC-2: RACH capacity / offloading / load balancing<br>  - UC-3: Network energy saving / common-signaling reduction<br>  - Other use cases are not precluded.<br>  - For each identified use case, study whether and under which conditions multiple-carrier operation in idle mode provides additional benefit over existing mechanisms. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in idle mode]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.4.1 | Agreement | For study of multiple carriers where for initial access a UE can utilize resources for UL (Msg1, etc) or for both UL (Msg1, etc.) and DL (Msg2, etc.) in one carrier other than the carrier where the UE obtains the system information providing configuration for those resources, consider the following potential scenarios for the set of candidate physical carriers for which resources are provided for initial access:<br>  - S1: 1 DL physical carrier + N UL physical carriers, where N > 1<br>  - S2: M DL physical carriers + 1 UL physical carrier, where M > 1<br>  - S3: M DL physical carriers + N UL physical carriers, where M > 1 and N > 1<br>Note 1: The carrier where the UE obtains the system information and other candidate physical carrier(s) are counted above.<br>Note 2: For a given UE and a given initial access procedure, UE may use only a subset of the candidate physical carrier.<br>Note 3: The inclusion of a scenario for study does not imply agreement to support it. The need and applicable use cases/deployment scenarios for each scenario are subject to further study. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in idle mode]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.4.1 | Agreement | For study of multiple carriers where for initial access a UE can utilize resources for UL (Msg1, etc) or for both UL (Msg1, etc.) and DL (Msg2, etc.) in one carrier other than the carrier where the UE obtains the system information providing configuration for those resources,<br>  - As a baseline, a single selected physical carrier is used per link direction throughout an attempt of an RACH procedure. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in idle mode]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.4.1 | Agreement | For further study of multiple carriers where for initial access a UE can utilize resources for UL (Msg1, etc) or for both UL (Msg1, etc.) and DL (Msg2, etc.) in one carrier other than the carrier where the UE obtains the system information providing configuration for those resources, consider at least the following deployment conditions:<br>  - D1: co-located carriers for which a common timing/frequency synchronization relationship can be assumed<br>  - D2: co-located carriers for which a common timing/frequency synchronization relationship cannot be assumed<br>  - D3: non-collocated carriers for which a common timing/frequency synchronization relationship cannot be assumed<br>  - Other relevant deployment assumptions, including carrier frequency relationship / band separation, numerology / duplexing configuration and UE RF/baseband capability, are considered as applicable.<br>Note: whether a common timing/frequency synchronization relationship can be assumed is from UE perspective. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in idle mode]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.4.2 | Agreement | Study UL transmission switching across carriers in 6GR, at least considering the following:<br>- Whether/how to achieve unified design for all UL signals/channels<br>- Signaling for switching, e.g., dynamic and/or semi-static switching<br>- Number of Tx chains<br>- Number of carriers<br>- Switching gap<br>- Interaction with carrier aggregation operation<br>- Coordination with other WG(s), e.g., RAN4 (as needed)<br>- Note: SRS antenna switching is separately discussed under UL CSI agenda | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in connected mode]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.4.2 | Agreement | Study DL reception switching across carriers in 6GR, at least considering the following:<br>- Applicable scenario(s) (e.g., the scenario(s) considered for NR LB-CA), including identify the scenario(s)<br>- Coordination with other WG(s), e.g., RAN4 (as needed) | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in connected mode]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.4.2 | Agreement | For 6GR multiple carrier operation in connected mode, study mechanisms for fast enabling/disabling access to additional carriers, considering at least the following:<br>- UE power saving and/or NW energy saving<br>- Carrier states<br>- How to enable/disable<br>- Assumed NW-side requirements/conditions<br>- Assumed UE-side requirements | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in connected mode]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.4.2 | Agreement | For 6GR multiple carrier operation in connected mode, study mechanisms for PXSCH resource allocation, considering at least the following:<br>- A PXSCH confined within a carrier<br>- A PXSCH over multiple carriers<br>  - Applicable scenario and condition<br>  - Coordination with other WG(s), e.g., RAN4 (as needed) | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in connected mode]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.4.2 | Agreement | For 6GR multiple carrier operation in connected mode, study mechanisms for scheduling PXSCH, considering at least the following:<br>- Self-carrier scheduling<br>  - Note: self-carrier scheduling is that a DCI on a carrier schedules a PXSCH on a same carrier<br>- Cross-carrier scheduling<br>  - Note: cross-carrier scheduling is that a DCI on a carrier schedules a PXSCH on another carrier<br>- Multi-carrier scheduling<br>  - Note: multi-carrier scheduling is that a DCI on a carrier schedules a single PXSCH across multiple carriers, or individual PXSCH per carrier<br>- Coordination with other WG(s), e.g., RAN4 (as needed)<br>- Applicable scenario and condition for the above mechanism(s)<br>- Multiple slot scheduling will be discussed separately in other agenda. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in connected mode]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.4.2 | Agreement | For the study of bandwidth operation with single carrier operation in CONNECTED mode for a UE in 6G, at least consider the following:<br>- A “6GR-BW” should comprise at least a frequency location and the number of contiguous PRBs within a carrier.<br>  - Study the necessity that any other parameter (s) are associated to a “6GR-BW” operation<br>    - Note: RAN1 strives to minimize the number of parameter(s) that is associated with a “6GR-BW”<br>- Signal(s)/channel(s) transmission/receiving associated to a “6GR-BW”<br>- Benefit(s) and necessity of switching of “6GR-BW” for DL and/or UL, and potential mechanisms for switching | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in connected mode]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | For the link budget evaluations in around 7 GHz for coverage gap identification, the assumptions for the receiver noise figure are 9 dB and 5 dB for DL and UL respectively (agreed in RAN1#124)<br>- Send an LS to RAN4 about the assumptions for information only. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |

| RAN1#126 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | Final LS R1-2606913 with following contents is endorsed.<br>1. Overall Description:<br>In the study of “Re-use of existing 5G mid-band (~3.5GHz) site grid for 6G deployments in at least around 7 GHz and targeting comparable coverage to 5G mid-band”, RAN1 made an agreement that for the link budget evaluations in around 7 GHz for coverage gap identification, the assumptions for the receiver noise figure are 9 dB and 5 dB for DL and UL respectively. There is no implication that RAN4 should use the same assumption.<br>2. Actions:<br>To RAN4<br>ACTION: RAN1 would like to respectfully inform RAN4 about the above receiver noise figure assumptions. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |

| RAN1#126 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | For the MaxCL requirement in TR 38.914, companies are encouraged to provide MaxCL evaluations based on the following assumptions:<br>- Assumptions used for maxCL calculations in TR38.914:<br>- Table 5.1.20.1-2: MaxCL calculation assumptions<br>\| UE Tx power \| 23dBm \|<br>\| DL Tx power \| 46dBm \|<br>\| BS receiver noise figure \| 5dB \|<br>\| UE receiver noise figure \| 9dB \|<br>\| Interference margin \| 0dB \|<br>  - Note: DL Tx power is 46dBm per 20 MHz and scales with wider bandwidth.<br>- Carrier frequency, duplex and SCS<br>  - FDD, carrier frequency: 0.7 GHz, 15kHz SCS<br>  - TDD, carrier frequency: 2.6 GHz, 30kHz SCS<br>- UE speed: 3kmh<br>- Channel model<br>  - TDL-C with 300ns delay spread<br>    - DL: 2T2R and 2T1R<br>    - UL: 1T2R<br>- Signals/channels to be evaluated<br>  - DL: NR PSS/SSS, PBCH, Common PDCCH, SIB1 PDSCH, Msg2/4 PDSCH<br>  - UL: NR PRACH format C2/B4/0, MSG3 PUSCH, MSG5 PUSCH (companies to report assumption of the payload of actual transmission),  MSG4 PUCCH Format 1<br>- The simulation assumptions in section A.1 in TR38.830 are used as a starting point.<br>  - The payload for NR SIB1 PDSCH is 1200 bits. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |

| RAN1#126 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | For SSB transmission, study<br>Option 1:<br>For a given frequency band, a single set of values for {X, Y, M} is predefined in default 6GR SSB periodicity<br>- Non-adjustable number of repetitions of transmitted SSBs<br>Note: for discussion purpose,<br>- X: the max number of candidate 6GR SSBs in a 6GR SSB burst<br>- Y: the max number of candidate 6GR SSB burst(s) in a default 6GR SSB periodicity<br>- M: the max number of candidate 6GR SSB repetitions within a default 6GR SSB periodicity<br>Option 2:<br>For a given frequency band, a single set of values for {X, Y, M} is predefined in default 6GR SSB periodicity and the actual number of repetitions of transmitted SSBs can be adjusted by the aNB<br>- FFS: how to adjust the actual number of repetitions of transmitted SSBs including potential restrictions<br>Note: for discussion purpose,<br>- X: the max number of candidate 6GR SSBs in a 6GR SSB burst<br>- Y: the max number of candidate 6GR SSB burst(s) in a default 6GR SSB periodicity<br>- M: the max number of candidate 6GR SSB repetitions within a default 6GR SSB periodicity | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | RAN1 to study and evaluate the following solutions<br>- Option 1:<br>  - SSB repetition within one SSB period and UE is assumed to at least support SSB combining within one SSB period<br>  - 160 ms default SSB periodicity for initial cell search<br>- Option 2:<br>  - SSB repetition within one SSB period and UE is assumed to at least support SSB combining within one SSB period<br>  - 80 ms default SSB periodicity for initial cell search<br>- Option 3:<br>  - UE is assumed to at least support SSB combining across different SSB periods<br>  - 40 ms default SSB periodicity for initial cell search<br>- Option 4:<br>  - SSB repetition within one SSB period and UE is assumed to at least support SSB combining within one SSB period and support SSB combining across different SSB periods<br>  - 40 ms default SSB periodicity for initial cell search<br>- Option 5:<br>  - UE is assumed to at least support SSB combining across different SSB periods<br>  - 20 ms default SSB periodicity for initial cell search<br>To mitigate the UE impact with longer SSB periodicity in Option 1/2/3/4, RAN1 to study the following solutions<br>- Solutions to reduce cell search complexity and latency<br>- Additional synchronization signals<br>- Clustered allocations of SSB, SIB1 and paging<br>- Other solutions are not precluded<br>FFS: Additional configurable SSB periodicities | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.1.1 | Agreement | Draft LS R1-2606890 is endorsed in principle.<br>Final LS R1-2606891 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |

| RAN1#126 | 10.5.1.2 | Agreement | For the study of preamble format reduction, below template is adopted for companies to provide set of preamble design targets that provide enablement of 6GR use cases and deployment scenarios. Please provide minimal set of preamble design targets that companies believe is the best representation for 6GR, including information on how the representative preamble design targets for 6GR is selected.<br>- A row represented Preamble design targets are represented by {Carrier Frequency, Delay (Max RTT + Delay Spread), Doppler Shift} tuple.<br>\| Identifier (company + Index) \| Carrier Frequency \| Delay (Max RTT + Delay Spread) \| Doppler Shift \| Notes (any information to additional share, e.g., sequence length, occupied BW, SCS, number of sequence repetition, preamble duration, frame structure restrictions, information on derivation, motivation, etc) \|<br>\|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \|<br>\|  \|  \|  \|  \|  \| | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.1.2 | Conclusion | To report the following details for PRACH evaluations:<br>- Receiver algorithm details, including:<br>  - Detection threshold strategy:<br>    - threshold configured to address false alarm only (impacting miss detection)<br>      - Example 1) Threshold is determined based on noise variance<br>    - threshold configured to address false alarm (impacting miss detection) and false detection probability jointly<br>      - Example 2) Threshold is determined based on noise variance, inter-sequence interference + noise estimated from measurements outside the window of the detected peak<br>        - Detection metric is normalized by interference+noise estimate, where the estimate is derived (e.g., maximum or median of the detection metric) from the detection metric outside the window around the detected peak within the NCS duration of the preamble. The detection peak window can be +/- estimated/approximated channel delay.<br>      - Example 3) Threshold is determined based on noise variance, sequence interference estimated from measurement of total receive signal power of a RO<br>  - In case of ZC sequence detection with Restricted Set A and B, details of detection algorithm to handle the detection sidelobes.<br>- For False alarm statistics, which option used for false alarm (and determination of detection threshold)<br>  - Option 1) if BS performs detection on a RO, the false alarm event happens for a preamble sequence detection, and a false alarm event is counted for the preamble sequence (false alarm event may happen multiple times within a RO for across the multiple preamble sequences). In this case, the denominator used to compute false alarm probability is the number of preambles to be detected (per RO).<br>  - Option 2) if BS performs detection on a RO, the false alarm event happens for any preamble sequence detection, and the a single false alarm event counted for a RO. In this case, denominator used to compute false alarm probability is 1 (per RO).<br>- Relative Tx power assumption of UEs (and any pathloss measurement and RF impairment modeling, if used)<br>- User distribution per RO, for example<br>  - Same number of users transmitting PRACH preamble in each RO<br>  - Varying number of users transmitting PRACH preamble in each RO (may be based on traffic characteristics)<br>- Preamble sequence selection<br>  - For example, NR ZC provides information on number of root sequences, number of cyclic shifts, and Ncs value among the 64 preamble set used for evaluation. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.1.2 | Agreement | For study of whether the maximum number of preambles per RO is increased beyond 64, take into account at least:<br>- the justification and use case for the capacity increase (e.g., load model, access rate per cell, target collision probability, availability of ROs in time/frequency);<br>- the available root-sequence pool and the resulting inter-cell reusing for the assumed sequence length; and<br>  - Note: Preamble sequence, format, as well as availability of ROs in time/frequency are taken into account when calculating available sequence pool and reuse.<br>- the resulting Miss detection, Type-1 (intra-cell), and Type-2 (inter-cell) false-detection performance under PRACH transmission load in the network.<br>  - Companies are asked to provide information on detection algorithm and detection metric thresholds (and adaptation strategies, if any) used for evaluations.<br>- associated transmitter/receiver complexity, and random access latency | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.1.3 | Agreement | For initial access, confirm the RAN1#125 Working Assumption that the frequency location and bandwidth of the SIB1-PDSCH are determined independently of the UE's bandwidth capability | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.1.3 | Agreement | For single-carrier operation, RAN1 to define one common resource block grid (CRB grid) across UEs for the DL of the given carrier for 6GR.<br>For single-carrier operation, RAN1 to define one common resource block grid (CRB grid) across UEs for the UL of the given carrier for 6GR. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.1.3 | Agreement | For initial access in a single-carrier cell,<br>- The schedulable frequency resources of SIB1-PDSCH contains the frequency location and bandwidth of the CORESET for SIB1-PDCCH monitoring. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.1.3 | Agreement | For initial access in a single-carrier cell,<br>- For option 2, further study<br>  - Alt 1: The schedulable frequency resources of SIB1-PDSCH are predefined without explicit signaling in MIB.<br>  - Alt 2: The schedulable frequency resources of SIB1-PDSCH are explicitly signaled in MIB, subject to minimum/limited impact to MIB coverage/overhead. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.1.3 | Agreement | Single numerology is adopted for the common RB (CRB) grid for 6GR single-carrier operation for a given band. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Bandwidth operation]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | - At least aggregation levels of 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 are supported for 6GR PDCCH.<br>  - Note: A CCE aggregation level is not used when the number of CCE in a CORESET is smaller than the aggregation level. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606611](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606611.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | - A CCE consists of 6 REGs for 6GR PDCCH<br>- REGs in a REG bundle are contiguous in frequency and time.<br>- At least REG-bundle size of 6 REGs is supported<br>  - FFS a need for other sizes. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606612](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606612.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | For the study of precoding granularity consider the following options from the UE perspective:<br>- Option 1: Precoding granularity is only a single REG bundle<br>- Option 2: The same precoder is used for all contiguous REGs in a CORESET<br>- Option 3: The same precoder is used for multiple contiguous REG bundles in time/frequency within the PDCCH candidate<br>- Option 4: The same precoder is used for multiple contiguous REG bundles in time/frequency. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606612](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606612.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | - Both contiguous and non-contiguous frequency-domain resource allocation are supported for 6GR CORESET.<br>- At least 6-PRB 6GR PDCCH CORESET frequency-domain resource allocation granularity is supported<br>  - FFS a need, feasibility and conditions for other CORESET granularities | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606613](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606613.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | - PDCCH payload bit-level scrambling before modulation is used for 6GR PDCCH.<br>- FFS: Details | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606614](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606614.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | Companies are encouraged to provide the analysis for comparison of MU-MIMO schemes (based on orthogonal and/or non-orthogonal DMRS) and single-layer, single-port, single-user PDCCH considering<br>  - The necessity to introduce orthogonal DMRS for 6GR PDCCH<br>  - At least the following two cases:<br>    - Case1: double resources for two paired MU-MIMO UEs than for a single user and the same total power are assumed from NW point of view<br>    - Case 2: the same resources for single user and two paired MU-MIMO users and the same total power are assumed from NW point of view<br>  - Performance based on link level and/or system level evaluations (e.g., BLER, spectrum efficiency)<br>  - Used models and criteria on the user pairing for MU-MIMO based on orthogonal multi-port DMRS and/or non-orthogonal single-port DMRS<br>  - Assumptions on the UE receiver<br>  - Assumptions on UE specific precoding<br>  - Impacts on UE complexity<br>  - Assumed channel model (at least CDL channel model for link level evaluations)<br>  - Assumed DCI size<br>Note: The discussion of the utilization of orthogonal DMRS ports for other purposes (e.g. intercell-interference mitigation) than MU-MIMO is not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606614](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606614.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.1 | Agreement | Study the need, feasibility including at least UE complexity and benefits of CSI acquisition for PDCCH.<br>Note: Solutions, if found to be feasible and needed, shall be coordinated with the other AIs. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606614](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606614.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - Support the following for 6GR DL DMRS<br>  - For X = 2, Nmax = 48<br>  - For X = 1, FFS Nmax where Nmax<= 24<br>  - The CDM group structure is comb-based including at least comb-2<br>    - FFS Applicable cases for comb-2<br>  - The indication of the co-scheduled UE information includes at least one of the following: the DMRS port(s) information or CDM group information or the FD-OCC despreading length information for the co-scheduled UE<br>  - Note:<br>    - X is the TD-OCC length<br>    - Nmax is the maximum number of orthogonal DMRS ports<br>    - The maximum number of orthogonal DMRS ports that a UE can support is a separate discussion in WI phase<br>    - AI/ML based DMRS overhead reduction is a separate discussion | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606676](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606676.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - For codeword-to-layer mapping for PDSCH:<br>  - For PDSCH rank 1—4, down-select from the following alternatives:<br>    - Alt 1: 1 CW for rank 1—4<br>    - Alt 2: 1 CW for rank 1, 2 CWs for rank 2—4<br>    - Alt 3: 1 CW for rank 1, 2 CWs for rank 2, 3 CWs for rank 3, 4 CWs for rank 4<br>  - Further study whether the codeword-to-layer mapping is fixed or flexible<br>  - Note: Above does not preclude different modulation order per layer(s) within a codeword | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606676](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606676.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - For codeword-to-layer mapping for PDSCH:<br>  - For PDSCH rank up to 5—8, down-select from the following alternatives:<br>    - Alt 1: 1 CW for rank 5—8<br>    - Alt 2: 2 CWs for rank 5—8<br>    - Alt 3: 4 CWs for rank 5—8<br>    - Alt 4: 5 CWs for rank 5, 6 CWs for rank 6, 7 CWs for rank 7, 8 CWs for rank 8<br>  - Further study whether the codeword-to-layer mapping is fixed or flexible<br>  - Note: Above does not preclude different modulation order per layer(s) within a codeword<br>  - Note: Codeword-to-layer mapping for rank > 8 can be further discussed subject to outcome of discussion on maximum number of layers per UE | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606676](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606676.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - For AI based receiver for PDSCH DMRS overhead reduction<br>  - Study Sub-case A (orthogonal sparse DMRS) including at least the following<br>    - AI/ML receiver assumption for evaluation purpose<br>      - Including model input, output, and label<br>    - Label quality impact including label acquisition method and how it is made in practice<br>    - Generalization and scalability performance<br>    - Model inference complexity and number of parameters<br>    - Data collection and performance monitoring<br>    - MU-MIMO pairing aspects<br>    - PDSCH processing delay<br>    - Relevant specification impact(s)<br>    - Other study aspects are not precluded<br>  - Study Sub-case B and consider how to address the following challenge aspects for Sub-case B (SIP)<br>    - Inter-layer interference, inter-UE interference, and inter-cell interference handling<br>    - PAPR<br>    - Whether/how to perform Rnn estimation<br>    - UE Coexistence<br>    - Power allocation between DMRS and data<br>    - High UE implementation complexity<br>    - Other challenge aspects are not precluded<br>    - Study the list under Sub-case A<br>  - Strive to down-select up to one sub-case | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606677](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606677.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |

(dH, dV) = (0.5, 0.8), (“Outdoor Combination 1”) | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606677](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606677.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - For maximum number layers per UE for PDSCH, down-select one of the following options:<br>    - Option 1: Up to 8 layers<br>    - Option 2: Up to 12 layers<br>    - Option 3: Up to 16 layers<br>  - Note: The UE capability is a separate discussion for normative work. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606677](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606677.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - Study (semi-)open-loop transmission and whether/how to support specification-non-transparent schemes for PDSCH<br>  - Consider, at least, high-mobility scenario, scenarios with outdated CSI, and UE specific/common PDSCH<br>  - Consider specification-transparent and closed-loop schemes as performance baselines<br>  - FFS: Impact on DMRS | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606678](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606678.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - Regarding the set of coordinated TRPs for multi-TRP transmission for PDSCH:<br>  - For discussion purposes, consider the following definitions for set of serving TRPs, the set of interference-coordination TRPs, and set of coordinated TRPs:<br>    - The set of serving TRPs per UE (of cardinality up to ) and the set of interference-coordination TRPs per UE (of cardinality up to ) are subsets of the set of coordinated TRPs at the NW (of cardinality up to )<br>  - Further study:<br>    - The maximum number of serving TRPs per UE,<br>    - The maximum number of interference-coordination TRPs per UE,<br>    - The maximum number of coordinated TRPs at the NW,<br>    - Standard impact, if any<br>  - Note: TRP may not be specified in 6G specification<br>  - Note: The above does not preclude the multiple subsets of serving TRPs. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606678](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606678.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - Consider the following for the co-schedule UE information indication/assumption for a target UE<br>  - Study at least the following options for the DMRS ports for the co-scheduled UEs<br>    - Option 0: The aNB indicates the CDM group(s) without data<br>    - Option 1: The aNB indicates the CDM group(s) for the co-scheduled UEs<br>    - Option 2: The aNB indicates the DMRS ports for the co-scheduled UEs within the same CDM group as the target UE<br>    - Option 3: The aNB indicates the FD-OCC despreading length<br>    - Option 4: The aNB indicates the DMRS port(s) for the co-scheduled UEs<br>  - Study at least the following options for modulation order and/or coding scheme for the co-scheduled UEs<br>    - Option 1: The aNB indicates modulation order for co-scheduled UE(s)<br>    - Option 2: The aNB indicates modulation order and coding scheme for co-scheduled UE(s)<br>    - Option 3: The target UE may assume modulation order and/or coding scheme for co-scheduled UE(s)<br>  - Study at least the following options on the frequency domain DMRS density for the co-scheduled UE(s)<br>    - Option 1: The target UE can assume the frequency domain DMRS density for the co-scheduled UE(s) is the same as the target UE<br>    - Option 2: The aNB indicates the frequency domain DMRS density for the co-scheduled UE(s)<br>  - Study at least the following options for the QCL/TCI for co-scheduled UE(s)<br>    - Option 1: The target UE can assume the QCL/TCI for the co-scheduled UE(s) is the same as the target UE<br>    - Option 2: The aNB indicates the QCL/TCI for the co-scheduled UE(s)<br>    - Option 3: The target UE may determine any QCL/TCI information for co-scheduled UE(s).<br>  - Support that a target UE can assume the time-domain location for the co-scheduled UE’s DMRS should be the same as the target UE’s DMRS<br>  - FFS: The target UE can assume the PRG allocation for the co-scheduled UE(s) is the same as the target UE<br>  - FFS: The target UE can assume the DMRS sequence for the co-scheduled UE(s) is the same or different as the target UE<br>  - FFS: Whether the information above can be optional or not with regard to different types of UE receivers<br>  - FFS: When the target UE can assume there is no co-scheduled UEs<br>  - FFS: For each item above, the DCI bit count per co-scheduled UE and its scaling with the number of co-scheduled UEs | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606678](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606678.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | With respect to the agreement below from RAN1#124bis, the following changes in red are made: | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606679](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606679.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - Support the DMRS for PDSCH based on the Gold sequence initialized based on at least a scrambling ID or physical cell ID<br>  - FFS: How the scrambling ID is indicated<br>  - FFS: Other factors for the sequence initialization<br>  - FFS: The number of scrambling IDs used to generate Gold sequence<br>  - FFS: Details on the Gold sequence generator<br>  - FFS: Additional supplementary sequence to reduce cross-correlation<br>  - FFS: How to facilitate PAPR reduction property<br>  - FFS: How to mitigate inter-port interference | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606679](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606679.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - Consider the following for 6GR DMRS for single-slot based PDSCH for further evaluation and down-selection<br>  - X=1, L = {1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6}<br>  - X=2, L = {1, 2, 3}<br>  - Note:<br>    - X is the TD-OCC length<br>    - L is the number of units of consecutive DMRS symbols<br>  - FFS: How to determine the value of L if multiple values are supported | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606679](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606679.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - Support PT-RS transmitted at least within the FDRA for the PDSCH<br>  - FFS: Whether PT-RS is transmitted from partial bandwidth or the whole bandwidth<br>  - FFS: Frequency-domain pattern of the PT-RS<br>- Support PT-RS transmitted at least within the TDRA for the PDSCH<br>  - PT-RS is not present in the DMRS symbol<br>  - FFS: Symbols for PT-RS<br>- Study the conditions for the PT-RS presence<br>- Study the maximum number of PT-RS ports<br>- Study PT-RS and DMRS port association | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606679](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606679.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - For TDRA for PDSCH contained within a single slot:<br>  - For starting symbol  and number of symbols, , down-select from the following options:<br>    - Option 1:<br>    - Option 2:<br>  - Note: DMRS design is discussed separately<br>  - Study to consider, at least, impact on network energy saving and spectrum efficiency | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606679](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606679.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - Study of multi-TRP transmission of single PDSCH scheduled by a single DCI to consider, at least, the following schemes assuming ideal backhaul:<br>  - Dynamic point selection (DPS)<br>  - Coherent joint transmission (CJT)<br>  - Single Frequency Network (SFN), at least, for HST scenario<br>    - Consider NR HST-SFN as a starting point<br>  - Time division multiplexing (TDM) repetition<br>  - Further study whether to consider also the following schemes:<br>    - Frequency division multiplexing (FDM) repetition<br>    - Non-coherent joint transmission (NC-JT)<br>- Companies to report target use cases for each of the schemes<br>- FFS: Whether above multi-TRP transmission schemes are transparent from UE perspective. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606679](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606679.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.2 | Agreement | - Further study whether to consider the following schemes for study of multi-TRP transmission of multiple PDSCHs scheduled by multiple DCIs assuming non-ideal backhaul:<br>  - Non-coherent joint transmission (NC-JT)<br>    - FFS: Fully overlapping, partially overlapping, and non-overlapping NC-JT<br>- Companies to report target use cases for each of the schemes | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606679](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606679.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL shared channel]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | For codebook based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, study the following coherency types of UL precoder<br>- Non-coherent type<br>- Partial-coherent type<br>- Full-coherent type<br>- Full-power transmission precoder is separately discussed. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605829](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605829.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | For codebook based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, study fixed codebook for full-coherent precoder type considering the following options and aspects:<br>- Option 1. Reuse NR UL codebook<br>- Option 2. Grassmannian codebook<br>- Option 3. Per-element phase/amplitude quantization targeting SVD precoder approximation<br>- Option 4. DFT based precoder<br>- Other option(s) are not precluded.<br>- Consider different antenna architectures and different waveforms<br>- Whether/how to separately or commonly design 2TX/3TX/4TX/8TX codebook<br>- Whether to keep constant modulus property<br>- Whether/how to consider UE PA (power amplifier) constraint<br>- Whether/how to define single-layer precoder only, and indicating a combination of precoder(s)<br>- Consider different codebook size and precoder indication overhead<br>- Whether/how to separately or commonly design codebook for CP-OFDM and DFT-S-OFDM.<br>- Note: Partial/non-coherent precoder type are separately discussed.<br>- Note: Per-layer power control is separately discussed. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605829](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605829.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | For evaluation on coherent UL MIMO based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, at least the RF coherence requirements for coherent UL MIMO defined in TS38.101-1/2 are used as baseline<br>- Companies to report<br>  - Other additional RF coherence requirements can be reported by companies (optional)<br>  - TX coherency error model | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605829](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605829.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | Support both of the following methods to convey the UCI over physical channel:<br>- Method 1: Define a UCI dedicated physical channel, i.e., Physical Uplink Control Channel (PUCCH)<br>- Method 2: UCI carried on PUSCH<br>  - Note: Whether/how to support each UCI type (e.g., HARQ-ACK, CSI) on PUSCH with/without data is up to other agendas<br>Further study on Option 3 per agreement in RAN1#124 | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605830](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605830.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | For transmission scheme(s) for 6GR PUCCH formats, study at least the followings aspects:<br>- Scheme(s) with sequence(s) without DMRS<br>- Scheme(s) with DMRS and/or channel coding<br>  - Note: It does not mean that scheme(s) with DMRS only is considered<br>- RAN1 strives for smaller number of formats for 6GR PUCCH, e.g., up to 2 or 3 formats.<br>- Study at least the following point(s) for differentiation among 6GR PUCCH formats<br>  - UCI payload size<br>  - Transmission scheme<br>  - Waveform<br>  - Modulation schemes<br>  - The number of OFDM symbols/PRBs for one PUCCH resource<br>  - Multi-user multiplexing scheme(s)/capability/capacity<br>- The study shall consider NR PUCCH formats as the benchmark | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605830](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605830.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | Support the following for UL DMRS for 6GR PUSCH at least based on CP-OFDM waveform<br>- For X = 2, Nmax = 48<br>- For X = 1, FFS: Nmax where Nmax<= 24<br>- The CDM group structure is comb-based including at least comb-2<br>  - FFS: Applicable cases for comb-2<br>- Note:<br>  - X is the TD-OCC length<br>  - Nmax is the maximum number of orthogonal DMRS ports<br>  - AI/ML based DMRS overhead reduction is a separate discussion<br>- FFS: Maximum number of orthogonal DMRS ports for DFT-s-OFDM | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605830](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605830.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | - For DMRS based on TD-OCC-X for a single-slot based PUSCH, the TD-OCC-X is applied to X consecutive symbols for DMRS<br>  - Consider the same spacing between every X DMRS symbols<br>    - Study how to identify the spacing between every X DMRS symbols<br>    - FFS: whether there is any exception<br>  - The number of DMRS symbols in a slot is L*X, and study the candidate value of L as follows<br>    - For X = 1, L = {1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6}<br>    - For X = 2, L = {1, 2, 3}<br>    - If multiple candidate values of L are supported, study how to determine the value of L<br>  - Study at least the following options for the time-domain location of the first DMRS symbol<br>    - Option 1: The first DMRS symbol is fixed as the first symbol allocated for the PUSCH<br>    - Option 2: The candidate DMRS symbol(s) are fixed in a slot and the actual first DMRS symbol is the first candidate DMRS symbol within the TDRA for the PUSCH<br>    - Option 3: The first DMRS symbol is indicated by the network relative to the first symbol of the PUSCH | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605830](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605830.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | For 6GR PUSCH transmission, study AI based receiver for PUSCH DMRS overhead reduction at least considering the followings:<br>- Candidate(s) of low overhead DMRS pattern<br>  - Subcase A<br>    - At least Comb-based low overhead DMRS pattern in frequency domain<br>    - FFS: time domain<br>  - Subcase B in power domain<br>Note: For a given low overhead DMRS pattern, it is transparent to the UE the type of receiver  (AI or non-AI) used at the network. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605831](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605831.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | For study on AI based receiver for PUSCH DMRS overhead reduction, subcase C is deprioritized. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605831](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605831.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | For codeword-to-layer mapping for PUSCH in 6GR:<br>- For PUSCH rank 1—4, down-select from the following alternatives:<br>  - Alt 1: 1CW for rank 1—4<br>  - Alt 2: 1CW for rank 1, 2CWs for rank 2—4<br>  - Alt 3: 1CW for rank 1, 2CWs for rank 2, 3CWs for rank 3, 4CWs for rank 4<br>- Further study whether the codeword-to-layer mapping is fixed or flexible<br>- Note: Above does not preclude different modulation order per layer(s) within a codeword<br>- Note: Above does not preclude different power allocation per layer | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605832](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605832.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | For codeword-to-layer mapping for PUSCH in 6GR:<br>- For PUSCH rank up to 5—8, down-select from the following alternatives:<br>  - Alt 1: 1CW for rank 5—8<br>  - Alt 2: 2CWs for rank 5—8<br>  - Alt 3: 4CWs for rank 5—8<br>  - Alt 4: 5CWs for rank 5, 6CWs for rank 6, 7CWs for rank 7, 8CWs for rank 8<br>- Further study whether the codeword-to-layer mapping is fixed or flexible<br>- Note: Above does not preclude different modulation order per layer(s) within a codeword<br>- Note: Above does not preclude different power allocation per layer | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605832](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605832.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | The following is endorsed  as Draft an LS to RAN4 regarding the 6GR the RF coherence requirements.<br>1 Overall description:<br>RAN1 are studying UL MIMO codebooks for 6G and have reached the following agreements:<br>\| Agreement: For codebook based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, study fixed codebook for full-coherent precoder type considering the following options and aspects: Option 1. Reuse NR UL codebook Option 2. Grassmannian codebook Option 3. Per-element phase/amplitude quantization targeting SVD precoder approximation Option 4. DFT based precoder Other option(s) are not precluded. Consider different antenna architectures and different waveforms Whether/how to separately or commonly design 2TX/3TX/4TX/8TX codebook Whether to keep constant modulus property Whether/how to consider UE PA (power amplifier) constraint Whether/how to define single-layer precoder only, and indicating a combination of precoder(s) Consider different codebook size and precoder indication overhead Whether/how to separately or commonly design codebook for CP-OFDM and DFT-S-OFDM. Note: Partial/non-coherent precoder type are separately discussed. Note: Per-layer power control is separately discussed. Agreement: For evaluation on coherent UL MIMO based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, at least the RF coherence requirements for coherent UL MIMO defined in TS38.101-1/2 are used as baseline Companies to report Other additional RF coherence requirements can be reported by companies (optional) TX coherency error model \|<br>RAN1 are currently using the coherent UL MIMO requirements in TS38.101-1/2 as baselines for evaluations including coherence imperfections. RAN1 would like to know if the /phase errors in these requirements  for 6G, especially for devices that support more than two port UL MIMO transmission.<br>RAN1 have not so far agreed to models of coherence imperfections for codebook evaluations, and so such models are determined by each company. Therefore, while RAN1 can continue investigations, RAN1 would welcome suggestions on /phase error modeling for 6G UEs so that these may be used to refine RAN1’s evaluations.<br>2	Actions<br>To RAN4<br>ACTION: 	RAN1 respectfully asks RAN4 if the /phase errors in current coherent UL MIMO requirements in TS38.101-1/2  for 6G, especially for devices that support more than two port UL MIMO transmission. While RAN1 can continue investigations, RAN1 would also welcome suggestions on /phase error modeling for UL MIMO.<br>The corresponding final LS in R1-2606899 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605832](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605832.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | For codebook based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, a fixed codebook is further clarified as follow:<br>- For a given precoder indication size, a set of precoder(s) in fixed codebook is pre-defined/specified in the specification<br>- Note: All 5G NR DL/UL codebooks are fixed codebook.<br>- Note: Per agreement in RAN1#124bis, a fixed codebook is a codebook which is pre-defined in specification. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605832](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605832.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.2.3 | Agreement | For codebook based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, study configurable codebook considering the following options and aspects:<br>- Option 1. Downloadable codebook<br>  - NW determines a set of precoder(s)/matrices.<br>  - From NW, UE is configured with a set of precoder(s)/matrices.<br>  - After NW acquires UL CSI, an UL precoder within the set of precoder(s) is indicated by NW to UE.<br>- Option 2. Uploadable codebook<br>  - UE determines a set of precoder(s) and reports to NW.<br>  - From NW, UE is configured with a set of precoder(s).<br>  - After NW acquires UL CSI, an UL precoder within the set of precoder(s) is indicated by NW to UE.<br>- Whether to consider constant modulus property<br>- Whether/how to indicate/obtain UE’s relevant information<br>- How to construct hybrid approach between fixed codebook and configurable codebook<br>- Memory burden / implementation complexity for UE/NW side<br>- Applicable scenario/use case (e.g., applicable number of antenna ports)<br>- How to consider PAPR increase and UE power constraint | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2605832](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2605832.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.1 | Agreement | For Option 1 of the physical channels/signals for the two stages in two-stage DCI study,<br>- Same or different CORESET for the 1st stage and 2nd stage of a two-stage DCI can be considered<br>- [Same or] different SSS for the 1st stage and 2nd stage of a two-stage DCI can be considered, if SSS is supported for the 2nd stage<br>  - FFS if SSS is not supported for the 2nd stage | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606047](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606047.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.1 | Agreement | For Option 2 of the physical channels/signals for the two stages in two-stage DCI study<br>- Option 2-1: The 2nd-stage uses PDCCH with modifications<br>  - Same or different CORESET for the 1st stage and 2nd stage of a two-stage DCI can be considered<br>  - [Same or] different SSS for the 1st stage and 2nd stage of a two-stage DCI can be considered, if SSS is supported for the 2nd stage<br>    - FFS if SSS is not supported for the 2nd stage<br>  - Study at least the following modifications beyond what supported for 1st stage:<br>    - Additional aggregation level(s) and/or flexible position (with the granularity of CCE) of the 2nd stage<br>    - Resource determination without hash function<br>    - Higher order modulation for the 2nd stage<br>- Option 2-2: The 2nd stage uses a PDSCH transmission scheme with at least the following exceptions/restrictions<br>  - Polar code is used<br>  - QPSK is used, FFS higher order modulation<br>  - Single-layer transmission is the baseline<br>  - FFS restriction on TDRA<br>  - FFS others | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606047](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606047.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.1 | Agreement | - Study multiple PXSCH transmissions for a single carrier using a single DCI and whether to support at least the followings by considering related scenarios/benefits,<br>  - Case 1: multiple slots, each slot contains at most one PXSCH<br>  - Note: for above study, followings are assumed<br>    - each PXSCH carries different TB<br>    - single DCI schedules multiple PDSCH<br>    - single DCI schedules multiple PUSCH<br>    - single DCI is single stage or two stage | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606047](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606047.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.1 | Agreement | RAN1 to study at least Cell-specific, Group-specific DCI and UE-specific DCI for 6GR, with the following functions:<br>- UE-specific DCI, including:<br>  - One scheduling on DL/UL transmission by using fallback DCI  (e.g., single-layer transmission),<br>    - Applicability for RRC connected state, and FFS applicability for other RRC states.<br>  - One scheduling on DL/UL transmission by using non-fallback DCI (e.g., multi-layer transmission), where the DCI content depends on UE-specific RRC configuration;<br>  - Non-scheduling/pure control information indication<br>  - Note: a scheduling DCI can contain non-scheduling/control information<br>- Cell-specific DCI and random access procedure DCI, including:<br>  - Scheduling on common signals/channels, e.g.,<br>    - Scheduling of system information;<br>    - Scheduling of paging messages;<br>  - Scheduling on random access procedure (e.g., scheduling of Msg2/Msg4 in DL, scheduling of Msg3 retransmission· in UL);<br>  - Non-scheduling/pure control information indication<br>- Group-specific DCI, including:<br>  - Group-common information or group of UEs’ information indication.<br>- For the fallback and non-fallback DCI design, the following principles apply:<br>  - Fallback DCIs are defined for CSS<br>    - Fallback DCI does not depend on UE-specific RRC configuration;<br>    - FFS: details of CSS<br>    - Note: applicable for UE-specific RRC reconfigurations<br>  - FFS whether fallback DCIs for USS considering use cases.<br>  - Non-fallback DCI can be dependent on RRC-configurable | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606048](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606048.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.1 | Agreement | Study at least the following mechanisms for DCI Container size determination (at least for RRC connected state), with the details as below:<br>- For Alt 1:<br>  - the container size(s) is implicitly derived from the RRC configuration,<br>    - E.g., the size for a given DCI container is the maximum possible size based on RRC configurations for the applicable functionalities.<br>- For Alt 2:<br>  - the container size(s) are explicitly signalled by the network via RRC<br>- For Alt 3’:<br>  - DCI container size(s) is not dependent on UE-specific RRC configuration<br>    - This is e.g., cell-specific DCI and random access procedure DCI in common search space. Details FFS.<br>- FFS: Padding/reservation<br>- FFS: need for truncation<br>- FFS: need for size determination during bandwidth adaptation.<br>- Regarding UE’s DCI monitoring size budget, for a single carrier operation,<br>  - FFS: the maximum number of container sizes for a UE to monitor (e.g., for BD purpose) is Xmax.<br>  - FFS: Whether the maximum number of container sizes for a UE to monitor (e.g., for BD purpose) for UE’s RNTI (e.g., C-RNTI) is Ymax.<br>  - FFS: whether the limitation is per slot or across slots<br>  - FFS: for multi-carrier cases<br>- FFS minimum container size.<br>- FFS maximum container size.<br>- FFS maximum number of container sizes for two stage DCI<br>- Note: Using a combination of the above alternatives for different use cases is not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606048](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606048.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.1 | Agreement | For the evaluation of two-stage DCI vs single-stage DCI, consider at least the following DCI container sizes (not including CRC) as starting point:<br>- Single-stage DCI: 40-bit, 60-bit, 140-bit<br>- Two-stage DCI<br>  - For Option A,<br>    - 1st-stage: 20-bit if it uses PDCCH<br>    - 2nd-stage: same as single-stage DCI<br>  - For Option A,<br>    - 1st-stage: 40-bit if it uses PDCCH<br>    - 2nd-stage: same as single-stage DCI<br>  - For Option B,<br>    - 1st-stage: 40-bit if it uses PDCCH<br>    - 2nd-stage: (single-stage DCI size minus 20-bit)<br>- 24-bit CRC is the default.<br>- Other values of DCI container size are not precluded.<br>- Companies are encouraged to report used/modified assumptions, other assumptions and corresponding justifications | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606049](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606049.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.2 | Agreement | Dynamically scheduled retransmission via DCI is regarded as a baseline for both CG PUSCH and SPS PDSCH in 6GR. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606527](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606527.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-DL/UL transmission without dynamic scheduling]` |
