| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.2 | Agreement | - For a given CG PUSCH configuration which is not immediately activated in 6GR, the configuration is dynamically (re)activated/deactivated. Study how to support dynamic (re)activation/deactivation for the CG configuration.<br>- For a given CG PUSCH configuration which is immediately activated in 6GR,<br>  - Study whether/how to support dynamic deactivation for the CG configuration.<br>  - Study whether/how to support dynamic (re)activation for the CG configuration.<br>  - If dynamic (re)activation/deactivation is not supported for this case, the CG configuration can be released/deactivated only by RRC signaling. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606529](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606529.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-DL/UL transmission without dynamic scheduling]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.2 | Agreement | - For a given SPS PDSCH configuration in 6GR,<br>  - The SPS PDSCH configuration can be activated/deactivated by DCI.<br>  - Note: the SPS PDSCH configuration can be released by RRC.<br>  - FFS activation by RRC | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606529](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606529.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-DL/UL transmission without dynamic scheduling]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.3 | Agreement | - For the study of downlink HARQ-ACK feedback granularity smaller than TB-level and the corresponding mechanism, candidate options in RAN1#125 are categorized as follow.<br>- Category A (HARQ-ACK schemes):<br>  - NR CBG HARQ-ACK feedback as comparison baseline<br>  - XOR-based retransmission feedback<br>  - CB/CBG-level HARQ-ACK feedback of high-probability error patterns / codebook-based compressed feedback<br>  - 2D lattice-based CBs feedback<br>- Category B (feedback mechanisms/procedures may be applicable on top of a Category A scheme):<br>  - Non-uniform CB-to-CBG mapping feedback<br>  - Two-stage feedback<br>  - Mixture of TB-level and CBG-level feedback<br>  - Per-PDSCH on-demand request of CBG-level feedback<br>  - Enabling/disabling of CBG-level HARQ-ACK feedback<br>  - HARQ-ACK feedback enabling/disabling for some of CBGs<br>- At least a common set of evaluation assumptions are to be determined in this meeting for Category A.<br>- FFS same/additional evaluation assumptions are to be applied for Category B. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606797](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606797.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.3 | Agreement | In 6GR, study whether/how to address the case where the UE’s HARQ soft buffer is insufficient (e.g., all HARQ processes across all carriers/cells requiring retransmissions for soft combining simultaneously with maximum TBS), considering the possibility of the case and the different ways to address the case | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606798](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606798.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.3 | Agreement | - For study of 6G HARQ-ACK codebook based on NR Type-1 codebook as starting point, consider at least the following<br>  - Whether / how to reduce the codebook payload size<br>  - Whether / how to reduce codebook construction complexity<br>- For study of 6G HARQ-ACK codebook based on NR Type-2 codebook as starting point, consider at least the following<br>  - Whether/how to achieve additional robustness against the DCI(s) missing including last DCI(s) missing<br>  - Whether the counting / accumulating indication is confined within a cell or a set of cells<br>- For study of 6G HARQ-ACK codebook based on NR (e)Type 3 codebook as starting point, consider at least the following<br>  - Whether/how to achieve additional robustness against missing the DCI triggering the feedback<br>  - Whether / how to reduce codebook payload size<br>- FFS for each codebook type:<br>  - HARQ-ACK bit ordering<br>  - Determination of HARQ-ACK codebook payload size<br>  - For relaxing the need for tight coordination between schedulers, determine which codebook type(s) should be considered<br>  - Scheduling complexity<br>  - How to carry HARQ-ACK bits for a DCI that does not schedule a PDSCH, for at least (e)type 3 codebook<br>  - Whether/how to support standalone operation<br>- FFS: other approaches | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606799](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606799.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.3 | Agreement | For the study of downlink HARQ-ACK feedback granularity smaller than TB-level, the following common set of simulation assumptions are to be used for evaluating Category A HARQ-ACK schemes:<br>\| # \| Parameter \| Assumptions \|<br>\| 1 \| Evaluation methodology \| LLS / SLS(optional) with link adaptation \|<br>\| 2 \| Carrier frequency \| 4 GHz Optional: 7GHz \|<br>\| 3 \| Channel bandwidth \| 100MHz, SNR points reported by company Optional: 400MHz, SNR points reported by company \|<br>\| 4 \| Subcarrier spacing \| 30kHz \|<br>\| 5 \| Channel model \| TDL-A 30ns, CDL-C 100ns Optional: TDL-C 100ns, CDL-A 30ns, CDL-B 30ns \|<br>\| 6 \| UE speed / Doppler \| 3km/h Optional: 30km/h, 80km/h \|<br>\| 7 \| MIMO configuration \| 4Rx UE; Rank: company to report (fixed to 1, 2, 4 or adaptive rank) \|<br>\| 8 \| Receiver functions \| Realistic channel estimation Precoding and receiver type to be reported \|<br>\| 9 \| Link adaptation \| OLLA enabled Target BLER 10% Optional: Other target BLER value \|<br>\| 10 \| NR CBG HARQ-ACK feedback as comparison baseline \| NR range 2/4/6/8 Optional: 16 for 400MHz, and other value(s) \|<br>\| 11 \| Metrics \| (Average) feedback overhead per TB Ratio of retransmitted CB(s)/CBG(s) size to original TB size Impact on DL control overhead Optional: Spectrum efficiency / UE throughput / UE power consumption \|<br>Note: Companies are encouraged to report any other assumptions/metric (e.g., traffic model, cell throughput) used in their SLS | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606799](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606799.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.3 | Agreement | For Method 1 (dynamic indication of HARQ-ACK feedback timing and resource in DCI), study the timing scheme of the HARQ-ACK feedback timing indication, including at least:<br>- Alt 1: counting/offset in physical slots<br>- Alt 2: counting/offset in available slots<br>  - FFS: how the available slots are defined/applied<br>- Study also the HARQ-ACK feedback timing indication value range/set (e.g., number of candidate values) under each alternative. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606799](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606799.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.3 | Agreement | - In 6GR, to address the issues mentioned in RAN2 LS if needed, study DCI indication for a given UL HARQ process status based on the following approaches:<br>  - Approach 1: DCI scheduling PUSCH indicating the status of the previously transmitted TB(s) associated with the same HARQ ID within same HARQ entity<br>  - Approach 2: A DCI other than DCI scheduling PUSCH indicating UL HARQ process status for one or multiple HARQ processes<br>  - Approach 3: DCI scheduling PUSCH indicating the status of TB(s) in the last transmitted PUSCH associated with the same HARQ ID within same HARQ entity | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606799](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606799.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.4 | Agreement | For 6GR, for the discussion in the collision-handling in 10.5.4.4 agenda item, operation with single PHY priority (as opposite to having two PHY-level priorities) is prioritized for future discussions in Rel-20 6G study.<br>FFS: whether two PHY priorities should be considered. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606707](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606707.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Handling of potential multiple uplink transmissions]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.4 | Agreement | For handling of potential multiple UL transmissions in 6GR, study collision-handling procedures at least for simplification and reduction of UE/aNB complexity with considering performance impacts, e.g., including<br>- Simplify the resource determination/mapping<br>- Reduce the complexity on the timeline determination<br>- Reduce the dependency between transmissions on different carriers<br>- Simplify/reduce collision-handling cases | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606707](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606707.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Handling of potential multiple uplink transmissions]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.4.4 | Agreement | For collision handling between PUCCH and PUSCH across multiple carriers, study the following mechanisms:<br>- Simultaneous transmission on different carriers<br>- Multiplexing and/or prioritization<br>- FFS: Applicable scenarios<br>Note: Collision handling among other channels/signals will be discussed separately. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606708](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606708.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Handling of potential multiple uplink transmissions]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.5 | Agreement | Support the following EVM of LLS for evaluating CB PUSCH:<br>\| Parameters \| Values \|<br>\| Carrier frequency \| 4 GHz \|<br>\| SCS \| 30 kHz \|<br>\| Waveform \| CP-OFDM DFT-s-OFDM \|<br>\| System BW \| 20 MHz \|<br>\| RB allocation for PUSCH \| 2 RBs and 14 symbols as starting point. Other values can be reported by company \|<br>\| MCS for PUSCH \| MCS index 0 in Table 5.1.3.1-1 of TS38.214 as baseline Other value can be reported by company \|<br>\| PUSCH payload size \| 56, 64 or 112 bits Company to report their choice. For 112 bits, company to report the RB allocation for PUSCH. \|<br>\| Number of simultaneous UEs in one CB PUSCH transmission. \| 1, 2, 3, 4, \|<br>\| Channel model \| CDL-A or TDL-A in TR 38.901 Company to report the choice. \|<br>\| Delay spread \| 100 ns \|<br>\| Channel correlation between simultaneous UEs \| Consider three cases: Randomly selected UEs. e.g, in CDL-A, use random UE AoA. Low channel correlation, High channel correlation, Company to report the details. \|<br>\| UE speed \| 3 km/h Other values can be reported by the company. \|<br>\| UE Tx antenna configuration \| 1 Tx antenna \|<br>\| BS Rx antenna configuration \| 4, 8, 64 Rx antennas BS antenna geometry (M, N, P) is reported by company. \|<br>\| Channel estimation \| Realistic \|<br>\| Receiver \| MMSE-IRC \|<br>\| DMRS configuration \| Rel-15 NR Type 1 DMRS Configuration to be reported by company, including total number of ports. \|<br>\| Distribution of SNR of different UEs \| Option 1: equal SNR Option 2: Unequal SNR, The SNR is uniformly distributed in [x - Y, x + Y] (dB) where x is the per UE average SNR in dB. Values of Y can be reported by companies. Company to report the details \|<br>\| Performance metric \| BLER of PUSCH reception SINR loss @1% BLER and @10% BLER with respect to single-UE transmission Optional: BLER error floor y% starting from x dB if any, company to report the definition of error floor False alarm rate and miss detection rate of DMRS port, or sequence (company to report the number of DMRS ports and number of sequences used in blind detection, detection method) Other metric can be reported by company. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.5 | Conclusion | Company to report the LLS results as the format in the following table:<br>\| BS Rx configurations \| Payload size and corresponding PUSCH RB allocation. \| Waveform, Channel model and channel correlation assumptions \| Configuration of DMRS port and DMRS sequence \| Distribution of SNR of different UEs \| DMRS ports/scramble IDs among simultaneous UEs. e.g., UEs use different DMRS ports, UEs use same DMRS ports but different scramble IDs. \| Evaluation results Note: company to report, for BLER results, whether blind detection on DMRS port and sequence is used or not. \| Evaluation results Note: company to report, for BLER results, whether blind detection on DMRS port and sequence is used or not. \| Evaluation results Note: company to report, for BLER results, whether blind detection on DMRS port and sequence is used or not. \| Evaluation results Note: company to report, for BLER results, whether blind detection on DMRS port and sequence is used or not. \|<br>\| BS Rx configurations \| Payload size and corresponding PUSCH RB allocation. \| Waveform, Channel model and channel correlation assumptions \| Configuration of DMRS port and DMRS sequence \| Distribution of SNR of different UEs \| DMRS ports/scramble IDs among simultaneous UEs. e.g., UEs use different DMRS ports, UEs use same DMRS ports but different scramble IDs. \| SNR loss @10% BLER for 2,3,4 UEs w.r.t single UE transmission \| SNR loss @1% for 2,3,4 UEs w.r.t single UE transmission \| Optional: BLER error floors for 2,3,4 UEs: yes or no, and if yes, starting SNR and level of BLER error floor. \| Miss detection and false alarm rate of DMRS port or sequence. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.5 | Agreement | For the study of multi-bit SR for requesting UL resources in 6G:<br>- Focus the study and evaluation on the following candidate reporting content:<br>  - Quantized buffer size.<br>- Note: this candidate is for evaluation purpose only.<br>- Study and determine whether/how the reported information in multi-bit SR can assist the BS to better schedule uplink resource for the UE<br>- Study and determine for multi-bit SR:<br>  - Applicable and non-applicable scenarios for multi-bit SR, including cell load (low/high) and target packet size (small/large).<br>  - Whether and how to accommodate dynamic/flexible range of payload size of UL data.<br>  - Excess granted resource caused by buffer size quantization.<br>- Study and evaluate the required/maximal bit size and study the quantization method.<br>- With the same aggregated UL resource overhead of multi-bit SR and 1-bit SR+ scheduled PUSCH for BSR, evaluate UL transmission scheduling latency performance of multi-SR vs the mechanism of 1-bit SR + BSR (the waiting time for next SR transmission occasion is included).<br>- Evaluate the link coverage of multi-bit SR transmission in PUCCH with 1-bit SR coverage as baseline.<br>- Compare the multi-bit SR carrying quantized buffer size with the solution of using multiple 1-bit SR configurations to indicate the same target quantized buffer size, considering the resource overhead and UL transmission scheduling latency.<br>- Other candidate reporting contents can also be evaluated<br>  - E.g., delay status report, LCH/LCG ID, predicted traffic timing information. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.5 | Agreement | For remote interference management for 6GR:<br>- At least support wireless-based RIM framework specified in 5G/NR as starting point for 6GR.<br>  - Further study whether to support backhaul-based RIM framework.<br>- Support the following two types of RIM-RS:<br>  - RIM-RS Type 1: transmitted by victim BS to indicate the existence of remote interference<br>  - RIM-RS Type 2: transmitted by aggressor BS to detect if remote interference still exists.<br>  - The RIM RS shall carry the following information:<br>    - BS group ID<br>    - ‘Enough/Not enough’ indication in RIM-RS Type 1<br>    - ‘Near/Far’ indication in RIM RS type 1.<br>    - FFS: the RIM RS can carry 6G specific information.<br>    - FFS: the RIM RS to carry duplex specific information, e.g., TDD frame structure, SBFD  subband/symbol configuration.<br>  - FFS the time-domain and frequency domain design of RIM RS, the sequence in RIM RS, and the configuration methods of RIM RS, time location of RIM RS.<br>  - FFS whether CSI-RS can be reused as RIM RS. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.5 | Agreement | For BS-to-BS CLI handling in 6GR, recommend to support information exchange between BSs and study the following candidate information exchange content:<br>- The slot configuration in TDD and time/frequency configuration of SBFD.<br>- The configuration of BS-to-BS CLI measurement RS: CSI-RS and/or 6GR SSB.<br>- The BS-to-BS CLI measurement results.<br>- DL Tx beam(s) information.<br>- Recommended and non-recommended beam.<br>- BS-to-BS CLI mitigation request. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.5 | Agreement | Study UE-initiated UE-to-UE CLI (UEI CLI) reporting with considering the following details:<br>- The definition of CLI event is: when at least one RSRP/RSSI measurement larger than a configured threshold for a given time length, the CLI event is triggered.<br>  - FFS: other CLI-related event(s).<br>- Study and determine the measurement metric for CLI event detection:<br>  - Option 1: non-filtered L1-RSRP/L1-RSSI<br>  - Option 2: filtered L1-RSRP/L1-RSSI.<br>- Study the CLI event detection procedure, report procedure and report contents. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.5 | Agreement | For UE-to-UE CLI measurement in 6GR, the followings are recommended:<br>- Support RSRP and RSSI as the measurement metrics<br>  - Wideband RSSI measurement is supported.<br>  - FFS on subband RSSI measurement.<br>- Support to use SRS for RSRP measurement<br>- Support to use CLI RSSI measurement resource for RSSI measurement<br>  - For SBFD, UE measures the UE-to-UE CLI in UL subband<br>- FFS: For SBFD, whether the UE can measure CLI in UL subband in the time duration of DL reception.<br>- FFS: whether the UE measures the UE-to-UE CLI in DL subband in SBFD. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.5 | Agreement | For UE-to-UE CLI reporting in 6GR, study L1 network-controlled CLI reporting with considering the followings:<br>- Study The latency requirement for CLI reporting to determine the necessity of L1 network-controlled CLI reporting.<br>- Consider periodic, semi-persistent and aperiodic reporting.<br>  - Study the applicable scenarios for each reporting mechanism.<br>- Consider the following two reporting content options:<br>  - Option 1: N strongest RSRP or RSSI and corresponding resource indicators. The UE reports RSRP/RSSI for the strongest one and differential RSRP/RSSI for the other N-1.<br>  - Option 2: a bit-map reporting with each bit corresponding each measurement resource, and bit value = 1 indicating the RSRP/RSSI measurement is above a configured threshold.<br>  - Study the benefit of each reporting content.<br>- Further study the following additional reporting content:<br>  - UE reports the time information corresponding to the measurement resource with the largest RSRP/RSSI measurement. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.5.5 | Agreement | Study non-transparent PUSCH resource muting mechanism for handling BS-to-BS CLI in 6GR and at least consider the following aspects:<br>- The muting mechanism specified in NR rel-19 is the starting point for study.<br>- Pattern(s) of resource muting.<br>  - Including study whether/how to support flexible muting patterns for resource efficiency.<br>- Whether/how to support dynamic/flexible adjustment of symbol position of muting resource.<br>- Whether/how to support dynamic disable/enable muting.<br>- Whether/how the TBS determination should consider the muting pattern in one PUSCH transmission.<br>- Whether/how same symbol power can be maintained between muting and non-muting<br>- Impact of multiplexed UCI in PUSCH<br>- Performance comparison between non-transparent and transparent PUSCH resource muting mechanism. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |

| RAN1#126 | 10.6.1.1 | Agreement | - For DL WUS evaluations in FR1 and ~7 GHz, RAN1 assumes 1 µs and 2 ppm for maximum timing and frequency errors, Temax and Femax, respectively, prior to detecting DL WUS, at least within the same carrier as SSB.<br>- Consider additionally potential solutions when necessary for addressing 2 µs timing error and 5 ppm frequency error, e.g.,<br>  - DL WUS processing timeline relaxation<br>- The actual timing and frequency errors, Te and Fe, respectively, are modelled as uniform distributions, Te ~ U [-Temax, Temax ] µs and Fe ~ U [-Femax, Femax] ppm, respectively, are assumed for link level simulations. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Design of WUS]` |

| RAN1#126 | 10.6.1.2 | Agreement | For RRC connected, study feasibility and necessity of serving cell RLM measurement by EE processing, considering at least:<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>- Coverage (e.g., achievable SINR/SNR) and accuracy<br>  - Measurement metrics<br>- Impact on the EE processing complexity<br>- Based on at least 6GR sync signal<br>  - other signals<br>- Whether there is spec impact or not | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.6.1.2 | Agreement | Study the following cases for 6GR DL WUS monitoring occasions:<br>- Case 1: A set of WUS monitoring occasions associated with at least one PO for a UE are TDMed, multiple sets of WUS monitoring occasions are TDMed.<br>- Case 2: A set of WUS monitoring occasions associated with at least one PO for a UE are TDMed, multiple sets of WUS monitoring occasions are FDMed or FDMed combined with TDMed.<br>- Case 3: A set of WUS monitoring occasions associated with at least one PO for a UE are FDMed or FDMed combined with TDMed<br>For case 1, 2, and 3, UE is not required to monitor more than one MO at given time.<br>considering at least:<br>- Impact on the UE energy consumption<br>- Impact on the UE complexity<br>- Network energy gains if any | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.6.1.2 | Agreement | For collection of UE energy saving gain results for 6GR DL WUS in RRC connected, at least the following cases are considered:<br>- Case 1:No neighboring cell RRM measurement<br>\| Case \| Schemes for triggering PDCCH monitoring \| Serving cell RLM/RRM measurement \| Neighboring cell RRM measurement \|<br>\| Case1-1 \| C-DRX only \| Non-EE processing \| Not applicable \|<br>\| Case1-2 \| C-DRX with DCP \| Non-EE processing \| Not applicable \|<br>\| Case 1-3 \| C-DRX with DL WUS \| Non-EE processing \| Not applicable \|<br>\| Case 1-4 \| C-DRX with DL WUS \| EE processing \| Not applicable \|<br>\| Case 1-5 \| DL WUS \| Non-EE processing \| Not applicable \|<br>\| Case 1-6 \| DL WUS \| EE processing \| Not applicable \|<br>- Case 2: with neighboring cell RRM measurement<br>\| Case \| Schemes for triggering PDCCH monitoring \| Serving cell RLM/RRM measurement \| Neighboring cell RRM measurement \|<br>\| Case 2-1 \| C-DRX only \| Non-EE processing \| non-EE processing \|<br>\| Case 2-2 \| C-DRX with DCP \| Non-EE processing \| non-EE processing \|<br>\| Case 2-3 \| C-DRX with DL WUS \| Non-EE processing \| non-EE processing \|<br>\| Case 2-4 \| C-DRX with DL WUS \| EE processing \| Non-EE processing \|<br>\| Case 2-5 \| C-DRX with DL WUS \| EE processing \| EE processing \|<br>\| Case 2-6 \| DL WUS \| Non-EE processing \| non-EE processing \|<br>\| Case 2-7 \| DL WUS \| EE processing \| Non-EE processing \|<br>\| Case 2-8 \| DL WUS \| EE processing \| EE processing \|<br>Note 1: Neighboring cell RRM measurement includes at least intra-frequency measurement, inter-frequency measurement is up to company report. How neighboring cell RRM measurement is performed by EE processing is up to companies’ report, including power consumption. L1 measurement is up to companies’ report.<br>Note 2: For traffic, FTP, XR, IM can be evaluated and traffic arrvival rate is up to companies’ report<br>Note 3: Assumed sleep state is up to company report.<br>Note 4: In this evaluation, no further power scaling based on the number of occupied SSB symbols within a slot for the measurement by EE processing<br>Note 5: If RLM/RRM measurement is not evaluated, please report.<br>Note 6: it’s up to companies’ report how the evaluated C-DRX different from NR C-DRX<br>Note 7: it’s up to companies’ report how the evaluated DCP different from NR DCP<br>Note 8: when comparing different cases, companies are encouraged to at least consider the same monitoring periodicity of 6GR DL WUS and C-DRX cycle. Other periodicities are up to companies’ report. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.6.1.2 | Agreement | For collecting UE energy saving results for 6GR DL WUS:<br>- Endorse the template for collecting UE energy saving results in R1-2606878.<br>Email discussion for collecting UE energy saving results in RRC idle by 2nd Oct, including potential updates to the template if any.<br>Email discussion for collecting UE energy saving results in RRC connected by 6th Nov. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.6.2 | Agreement | - For feasibility study of DS#1b, further study the following mechanisms for UE to acquire UL WUS configuration.<br>  - Option 1: Pre-configuration and/or pre-defined in specification<br>  - Option 2: Synchronization signal(s)<br>  - Option 3: PBCH including PBCH DMRS<br>  - Option 4: Broadcast PDSCH<br>  - Option 5: Common PDCCH<br>  - Note: Combination of above options can be considered.<br>  - At least the followings are considered for further study of the above options<br>    - NW configuration flexibility<br>    - NW and UE energy saving/consumption<br>    - UE complexity<br>    - Signaling overhead<br>    - Necessary payload by taking SIB26 for NR OD-SIB1 as the reference | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.6.2 | Agreement | - For study of necessity for UL WUS, at least followings are considered as candidates of use cases.<br>  - For UEs in idle mode,<br>    - UE-initiated on-demand or adaptation procedures for one or multiple of the following signals/channels:<br>      - 6GR SS/PBCH<br>      - 6GR SIB1<br>      - 6GR PRACH<br>      - 6GR DL Reference Signal<br>  - For UEs in connected mode,<br>    - UE-initiated on-demand or adaptation procedures for one or multiple of the following signals/channels:<br>      - 6GR SS/PBCH<br>      - 6GR SIB1<br>      - 6GR PRACH<br>      - 6GR DL Reference Signal<br>    - UL data indication (including ‘no UL data’ indication)<br>    - Selection of PUSCH resource and/or PUSCH transmission parameter<br>    - UL WUS based measurement<br>    - Indication of new beam for BFR (if supported for 6GR)<br>- With consideration of at least the following aspects:<br>  - 6GR SSB/SIB1 periodicity/repetition<br>  - Coverage<br>  - UL WUS detection performance<br>  - NW and UE energy/power consumption<br>  - Latency<br>  - NW and UE complexity<br>  - Resource overhead<br>  - Necessity of UE-triggered operation compared to NW-triggered mechanisms<br>  - Which DS(s) are applicable to the above use cases<br>  - Interference aspects<br>  - Inter-node coordination<br>  - Overlapping with existing or other 6GR mechanisms that could play the same functionalities | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.6.2 | Agreement | - For study of feasibility for UL WUS, followings are observed for DS#2a in addition to previous RAN1 agreement.<br>  - If UE transmits UL WUS to cell/carrier #2, and if cell/carrier #1 and cell/carrier #2 are non-co-located,<br>    - UE can determine UL WUS transmission power as the pre-defined TX power (e.g., maximum TX power) Further study<br>      - Whether/how to mitigate interference to the cell/carrier other than cell/carrier #2<br>      - Whether/how to handle the impact to UL WUS detection performance<br>      - Whether UE can determine path-loss estimation from cell/carrier #1 DL signals and power offset value provided by cell/carrier #1 when available/applicable.<br>        - Details on how to use path-loss estimation from cell/carrier #1 DL signals<br>    - UE can use wide/omni-directional beam or beam sweeping as UL WUS transmission beam. Further study<br>      - How to handle the impact of reduced cell coverage, when using wide/omni-directional beam.<br>      - How to determine narrow beam and whether/how to mitigate interference to the cell/carrier other than cell/carrier #2.<br>    - UE can use cell/carrier #1 SSB as timing reference for UL WUS transmission when cell/carrier #1 and cell/carrier #2 are synchronized when the timing can be maintained in UE, or when cell#1/carrier #1 provides underlay coverage for cell#2/carrier #2.<br>    - It is required to further study whether/how UE can use cell/carrier #1 SSB as timing reference for UL WUS transmission or not, when cell/carrier #1 and cell/carrier #2 are not synchronized, considering inter-cell distance, timing advance, etc.<br>    - This can cover the scenario where UE moves from cell/carrier #1 to cell/carrier #2 (where cell/carrier #1 may not provide underlay coverage for cell/carrier #2) and can maintain DL timing/frequency synchronization and UL WUS configuration for a certain period of time<br>      - It is required to further study the duration of the certain period of time with coordination with RAN4 as needed | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.6.2 | Agreement | - RAN1 to study necessary information and required payload size for UL WUS configuration, considering at least the following information and its necessity<br>  - Time and frequency domain resource size and location of UL WUS<br>  - Mapping between 6GR synchronization signal and UL WUS occasion<br>  - Parameters to determine UL WUS transmission power<br>  - Parameters to determine UL WUS sequence/format<br>  - Parameters for UE monitoring of response to UL WUS<br>  - Cell access related information<br>  - Note: SIB26 for NR OD-SIB1 is taken as a reference<br>  - Note: The above does not preclude that some necessary information may be implicitly determined, without need of explicit payload | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.6.3 | Agreement | - For the study of any information carried in DL-WUS with or, if supported, without the wake-up indication, RAN1 to at least use the following common assessment criteria:<br>  - Complexity of the UE EE processing and NW’s impact (if any);<br>  - Coverage impact of the resulting payload;<br>  - DL (and, where applicable, UL) resource overhead;<br>  - Energy-saving benefit at both UE side and network side, and latency impact;<br>  - Overlapping with existing or other 6GR mechanisms that could carry the same information<br>  - Whether DL-WUS used for other functions is beneficial than the existing or other 6GR mechanisms<br>  - The agreed FAR/FDR/MDR for DL WUS are used as the threshold for FAR/FDR/MDR of the additional (non-wake-up) information and associated functionality. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-WUS operation with other functionalities]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.6.3 | Agreement | - For UEs in RRC_IDLE / RRC_INACTIVE state, RAN1 to study at least the following candidate additional functions for DL-WUS:<br>  - Candidate #1: SI-modification and/or ETWS-CMAS notification carried via DL WUS.<br>  - Candidate #2: Use DL-WUS as a trigger or indication for on-demand and/or additional RS and/or common signals/channels, if supported.<br>  - Candidate #3: Use DL-WUS to indicate PF/PO resources or RACH occasions that can be used.<br>  - Candidate #4: Study DL WUS to trigger Downlink Small Data Transmission (DL SDT) in inactive state, if supported. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-WUS operation with other functionalities]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.7.1 | Agreement | For 6GR NTN GNSS-less/free/degraded PRACH, the differential time and frequency errors documented in TR 38.742 are taken as targets for the study with the addition of at least the following scenarios:<br>- S-band LEO-300<br>- Ka-band LEO-300<br>- FFS: details of these scenarios, including differential time/frequency errors | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606418](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606418.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.7.1 | Conclusion | For the 6GR NTN GNSS-less/free/degraded PRACH study:<br>- The study is conducted in common agenda item, taking into account the NTN requirements which are developed under NTN specific agenda item (i.e. AI 10.7.1.).<br>  - This does not preclude NTN specific adjustments to the common design if needed, which may be developed under NTN specific agenda item (i.e. AI 10.7.1) if the design from the common agenda item does not meet the NTN requirements. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606418](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606418.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.7.1 | Agreement | The RAN1#125 agreement on LLS assumptions for SSB in 6GR NTN is corrected as follows:<br>The following parameters are used to evaluate SSB coverage for the 6GR NTN link budget template:<br>NOTE: Structure of NR signals / channels and other functionalities/features can be initially used for evaluation until other agenda items progress on the 6GR signals / channel structure/functionalities/features.<br>\| Periodicity \| To be reported \|<br>\| Performance metric \| 1% missed detection with 0.1% false alarm rate for PSS/SSS. 1% BLER for PBCH. Performance metric for different component physical channels / signals within the SSB (i.e., PSS, SSS, PBCH) can be separately reported Note: UE is not assumed to know the SS/PBCH block index \|<br>\| SSB combining \| Companies to report which component physical channels / signals within the SSB (i.e., PSS, SSS, PBCH) are combined, and how many SSBs are combined (if any). Single-shot SSB detection is also reported. \|<br>\| Other parameters \| Reported by companies (including maximum CFO and timing drift rate (us/s) assumed, combining algorithm, etc). CFO and timing drift are mandatorily reported. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606419](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606419.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.7.1 | Agreement | For the 6GR NTN link budget template, for Ka/Ku band PDSCH and PUSCH link level evaluations, the PT-RS configuration is optional, and reported by companies if considered. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606419](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606419.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.7.1 | Agreement | For the 6GR NTN link budget template, for Ka/Ku band link level evaluations, the UE and SAN phase noise model in clause 6.1.11.2 of TR38.803 can be optionally used for BPSK/QPSK (and in that case is reported by companies) and is used for higher order modulation (if evaluated for the link budget template)<br>- NOTE: This agreement only applies to the 6GR NTN link budget template. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606420](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606420.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.7.1 | Agreement | For the link budget template of 6G NTN, at least the following scenarios are evaluated<br>- NOTE: APC = “Adverse propagation conditions”<br>\|  \| LEO 300 \| LEO 600 \| LEO 1200 \| MEO 7000 \| GEO \|<br>\| S-band \| 1) 30deg, beam edge, Outdoor 2) 30deg, beam edge, APC (Optional) \| 1) 30deg, beam edge, Outdoor 2) 30deg, beam edge, APC (Optional) \| N/A \| 1) 30deg, beam edge, Outdoor \| 1) 12.5deg, beam edge, Outdoor \|<br>\| Ka band \| N/A \| 1) 30deg, beam edge, Outdoor \|  \| N/A \| 1) 12.5deg, beam edge, Outdoor \|<br>\| Ku band \| N/A \| N/A \| 1) 30deg, beam edge, Outdoor \| N/A \| 1) 12.5deg, beam edge, Outdoor \|<br>NOTE: In the scenarios above, no “wide beam” operation is assumed. The link budget for wide-beam operation can be provided by companies in the corresponding topic. The beam edge diameter is according to the agreed tables.<br>NOTE: If companies provide results for other scenarios than mentioned above, the corresponding results can be captured in link budget summary table. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606421](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606421.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.7.1 | Working Assumption | On the link budget template for 6GR NTN, the following values are used for Ka and Ku-band:<br>- “(28a) Atmospheric loss (dB)”<br>  - For Ka-band GEO 20GHz (12.5 degree elevation): 1.2dB<br>  - For Ka band GEO 30GHz (12.5 degree elevation): 1.1dB<br>  - For Ku band GEO (12.5 degree elevation): 0.2dB<br>  - For Ka-band LEO (30 degree elevation): 0.5dB<br>  - For Ku-band LEO (30 degree elevation): 0.1dB<br>  - NOTE: These losses do not include rain loss. The values are obtained from ITU P.676 figures.<br>- “(28b) Shadow fading margin (dB)” = 0<br>- “(28c) Scintillation loss (dB)”<br>  - For GEO (12.5 degree elevation) Ka band, 1.1dB<br>  - For LEO (30 degree elevation) Ka band, 0.3dB<br>  - For GEO (12.5 degree elevation) Ku band, 0.3dB<br>  - For LEO (30 degree elevation) Ku band, 0.2dB<br>  - NOTE: This scintillation loss is due to tropospheric scintillation, as described in TR 38.811.<br>- “(28d) Polarization loss (dB)” = 0dB<br>  - This is due to matching polarizations between UE and SAN antenna<br>- “(4b-2) / (11-b2) Scanning loss (dB)” is<br>  - Calculated based on elevation angle and antenna element pattern gain for both SAN and UE (if they use antenna arrays)<br>    - NOTE: RAN1 might need to revisit the agreed antenna element pattern gain.<br>- “(20) Receiver implementation margin (dB)” = 1dB<br>- “(27) Penetration margin” = 0dB<br>- “(28e) Other losses” = 0dB<br>NOTE: The study of a particular scenario for the link budget template does not imply that, if RAN1 determines a coverage gap for that scenario, it will be studied or specified how to close it. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606422](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606422.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.7.1 | Working Assumption | For 6GR NTN MEO-7000 S-band, the following parameters are adopted:<br>- For downlink:<br>\|  \| MEO-7000 \|<br>\| Maximum Bandwidth per beam \| 5MHz \|<br>\| Beam size (note 1) \| 160km \|<br>\| Satellite EIRP density /beam (dBW/MHz) \| 44 \|<br>\| Payload Total DL power level (dBW) \| 26.72 \|<br>\| Aggregated EIRP (Total) (dBW) \| 67.81 \|<br>\| Satellite Tx max Gain \| 41.08 dBi \|<br>\| Maximum EIRP per Satellite beam (dBW) \| 51 \|<br>\| Total number of beam footprints \| 2774 \|<br>\| Total number of simultaneously active beams \| 48 \|<br>\| % simultaneously active beams* \| 1.73% \|<br>\| Note 1: Reference beam size, different beam sizes may be evaluated and reported by companies (for e.g. hierarchical beam structure) \| Note 1: Reference beam size, different beam sizes may be evaluated and reported by companies (for e.g. hierarchical beam structure) \|<br>- For uplink:<br>\|  \| MEO-7000 \|<br>\| Maximum Bandwidth per beam \| 5 MHz \|<br>\| Beam size (note 1) \| 160km \|<br>\| Satellite Rx max Gain \| 41.08 dBi \|<br>\| Satellite max G/T \| 12.18 dB/K \|<br>\| Total number of beam footprints \| 2774 \|<br>\| Total number of simultaneously active beams* \| 48 \|<br>\| % simultaneously active beams* \| 1.73% \|<br>\| Note 1: Reference beam size, different beam sizes may be evaluated and reported by companies (for e.g. hierarchical beam structure) \| Note 1: Reference beam size, different beam sizes may be evaluated and reported by companies (for e.g. hierarchical beam structure) \|<br>- Antenna parameters:<br>\| Frequency \| S-band (i.e., 2 GHz) \|<br>\| Orbit \| MEO-7000 \|<br>\| TXRU mapping (for phased antenna array) \| Fully connected \|<br>\| Satellite antenna configurations Option 1: Phased antenna array i.e. (M,N,P,Mg,Ng; Mp, Np), (dH,dV), (dg,H,dg,V) if any (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np) - M: Number of vertical antenna elements within a panel, on one polarization - N: Number of horizontal antenna elements within a panel, on one polarization - P: Number of polarizations - Mg: Number of panels in a column; - Ng: Number of panels in a row; - Mp: Number of vertical TXRUs within a panel, on one polarization - Np: Number of horizontal TXRUs within a panel, on one polarization Note: Mp and Np may not be applicable for fully-connected TXRU. Option 2: Reflector antenna \| Antenna elements: 8100 (90x90) Separation: 0.5 lambda Number of polarizations: 1 or 2. 1-pol: (M,N,P,Mg,Ng)= (90, 90, 1, 1, 1), , (dH,dV) = (0.5, 0.5) 2-pol: (M,N,P,Mg,Ng) = (90, 90, 2, 1, 1), (dH,dV) = (0.5, 0.5) \|<br>\| Satellite Polarized antenna modelling e.g., models in section 7.3.2 in TR38.901 \| Circular \|<br>\| Satellite antenna element gain pattern \| Table 7.3-1 in TR 38.901 Horizontal/vertical 3 dB beam width of single element (degree): 90 for H, 90 for V Element max directivity: 5dBi. An antenna efficiency of 50% is assumed which results in an effective element max gain of 2dB. The resulting overall antenna gain (after applying the array factor) is 41.08dB \| | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606422](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606422.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.7.1 | Working Assumption | For  6GR NTN LEO-1200 Ku-band, the following parameters are adopted<br>- G/T = 6dB/K<br>- EIRP density per beam = 13dBW/MHz<br>- Antenna parameters:<br>\| Frequency \| Ku-band \|<br>\| Orbit \| LEO-1200 \|<br>\| TXRU mapping (for phased antenna array) \| Fully connected \|<br>\| Satellite antenna configurations Option 1: Phased antenna array i.e. (M,N,P,Mg,Ng; Mp, Np), (dH,dV), (dg,H,dg,V) if any (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np) - M: Number of vertical antenna elements within a panel, on one polarization - N: Number of horizontal antenna elements within a panel, on one polarization - P: Number of polarizations - Mg: Number of panels in a column; - Ng: Number of panels in a row; - Mp: Number of vertical TXRUs within a panel, on one polarization - Np: Number of horizontal TXRUs within a panel, on one polarization Note: Mp and Np may not be applicable for fully-connected TXRU. Option 2: Reflector antenna \| Antenna elements: 1296 (36x36) Separation: 0.5 lambda Number of polarizations: 1 or 2. 1-pol: (M,N,P,Mg,Ng)= (36, 36, 1, 1, 1), (dH,dV) = (0.5, 0.5) 2-pol:(M,N,P,Mg,Ng) = (36, 36, 2, 1, 1), (dH,dV) = (0.5, 0.5) \|<br>\| Satellite Polarized antenna modelling e.g., models in section 7.3.2 in TR38.901 \| Circular \|<br>\| Satellite antenna element gain pattern \| Table 7.3-1 in TR 38.901 Horizontal/vertical 3 dB beam width of single element (degree): [90] for H, 90 for V Element max directivity: 5dBi. An antenna efficiency of 50% is assumed which results in an effective element max gain of 2dB. | RAN1#126 | 10.7.1 | Working Assumption | For 6GR NTN Ka and Ku-band UE antenna assumptions, the following parameters are adopted:<br>- NOTE: An additional set of parameters for a Ka and Ku band UEs with a larger antenna will be also adopted, details to be discussed in the next meeting.<br>\| Frequency \| Ka-band (12cm) \| Ku-band (12cm) \|<br>\| TXRU mapping (for phased antenna array) \| Fully connected \| Fully connected \|<br>\| Satellite antenna configurations Option 1: Phased antenna array i.e. (M,N,P,Mg,Ng; Mp, Np), (dH,dV), (dg,H,dg,V) if any (M, N, P, Mg, Ng; Mp, Np) - M: Number of vertical antenna elements within a panel, on one polarization - N: Number of horizontal antenna elements within a panel, on one polarization - P: Number of polarizations - Mg: Number of panels in a column; - Ng: Number of panels in a row; - Mp: Number of vertical TXRUs within a panel, on one polarization - Np: Number of horizontal TXRUs within a panel, on one polarization Note: Mp and Np may not be applicable for fully-connected TXRU. An antenna efficiency of 50% is assumed which results in an effective element max gain of 2dB. | RAN1#126 | 10.7.1 | Working Assumption | On the link budget template for 6GR NTN, the following values are used for S-band:<br>- “(28a) Atmospheric loss (dB)” = 0.1dB for LEO 30 degree elevation and 0.2dB for GEO 12.5 degree<br>- “(28b) Shadow fading margin (dB)” = 3dB<br>  - Note: Additional losses due to e.g. light indoor/in-vehicle penetration loss may be considered under APC.<br>- “(28c) Scintillation loss (dB)” = 2.2dB<br>  - This is for the evaluated elevation angles.<br>- “(28d) Polarization loss (dB)” = 3dB<br>  - Assumes SAN antenna is circularly polarized and UE antenna is linearly polarized<br>- “(4b-2) /  (11-b2) Scanning loss (dB)” is<br>  - Calculated based on elevation angle and antenna element pattern gain, e.g. ~4dB for 30 degree elevation angle in LEO 600, if the SAN antenna is modeled as an array.<br>    - NOTE: RAN1 might need to revisit the agreed antenna element pattern gain.<br>- For UEs with omni antennas (11a)/(4c) “Boresight Antenna gain” is set to<br>  - -5.5dB as baseline.<br>    - 0dB can be considered optionally.<br>- “(20) Receiver implementation margin (dB)” = 1dB<br>- “(27) Penetration margin” = 0dB<br>  - Note: Losses due to e.g. light indoor/in-vehicle penetration loss may be considered under APC.<br>- “(28e) Other losses”: is set to:<br>  - For outdoor UEs, 0dB<br>  - For UEs in adverse propagation condition (APC): [a value between 6dB and 15dB]<br>    - This value will be decided in the next meeting based on input from companies (values and associated scenarios).<br>    - APC is only evaluated for downlink broadcast channel / signals.<br>    - NOTE: UEs in adverse propagation conditions is per RAN guidance and is documented in TR 38.914 as 0% of users in Case 1 and 4% of users in Case 2.<br>NOTE: The study of a particular scenario for the link budget template does not imply that, if RAN1 determines a coverage gap for that scenario, it will be studied or specified how to close it. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606422](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606422.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| RAN1#126 | 10.7.1 | Agreement | In 6GR NTN, under the label of “physical layer aspects of multi-satellite operation”, the following sub-aspects are studied under “physical layer aspects related to inter-satellite interference management”<br>- Co-channel interference, taking into account characteristics of NTN channels (e.g. large and time-varying Doppler / timing offsets due to orbital dynamics)<br>  - At least for LEO same orbit.<br>  - FFS other parameters (e.g. elevation angle)<br>- NOTE: It is assumed that the UE is only connected to one satellite at a time. | [Chair notes RAN1#126_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2606422](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126/Docs/R1-2606422.zip)<br>`[126-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
